# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2003

## Introduction by Draviḍa dāsa

This is the thirty-fifth annual Vyāsa-pūjā book. Back in 1969 seventeen temples wrote homages, with several devotees per temple contributing. They wrote from Boston, Buffalo, Columbus, and Detroit, from Hamburg, West Honolulu, London, and LA, from Montreal, New York, New Vrindāvan, and Chapel Hill, and from Philadelphia, San Francisco, Seattle, Vancouver, and Washington, D.C. That was the totality of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. The present volume has homages from over one hundred temples—one per temple—and that’s not even a third of the total now part of ISKCON.

Certainly the expansion of the Vyāsa-pūjā book is evidence of the glorious success of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s preaching mission. That success continues, taking Kṛṣṇa consciousness into the farthest corners of the world. Where is Lome, Togo, anyway? Somewhere in West Africa, I gather. And there is a sincere devotee in Lome named Varāha dāsa who could write to Śrīla Prabhupāda this year:

I came to Lome alone in October 2002, and after five months we had a full-fledged temple, a family of devotees, and an active congregation. All this is only a testimony to your greatness, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

My prayer to you on this auspicious anniversary of your appearance is that you will bless me, and the devotees with me, with your potency so that we can continue spreading the mission of Lord Caitanya to every country in West Africa. There are many countries in this part of the world where the people are literally starving for want of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Here is the true mystery of the absolute nature of spirit: twenty-five years after Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disappearance, a grand-disciple writes with the same devotion and prayerfulness as the devotees back in 1969, knowing that Srila Prabhupada, like Kṛṣṇa Himself, will personally respond in proportion to his sincerity. As always, the substance of devotion to Kṛṣṇa consists of the surrender to the guru’s order. And the result? A heartfelt desire to illuminate the darkest corners of this Kali-yuga world with the soothing, cooling, rays of Lord Caitanya’s benediction moon.

Here is another mood we find in the old and new offerings: gratitude for salvation. Uttarā-devī dāsī wrote in the first offering of the first book:

Just at the end of night, when all is still  
And hung with blindness, breeding dark decay,  
The sun, with rays of cleansing, living light  
Moves forth on the horizon, and brings day;

So an Acharya comes, bearing the light  
Of love of God, the clearest beams of all.  
He is our Shelter from the eyeless night  
Of nescience, and we know we shall not fall.

If we but keep His teachings in our hearts,  
We shall love Krishna by this one life’s end;  
The Master’s order is our life and soul,  
For He is Krishna’s confidential Friend.

I therefore offer this most humble prayer:  
That Śrīla Prabhupād make my faith strong;  
He is the sun; illuminating all:  
Go back to Godhead, back where you belong!

This beautiful poem is as fresh and sparkling today as when Uttarā wrote it thirty-four years ago. “In this endeavor, there is no loss or diminution...”

These are two small voices in a vast chorus of prayers and praise, confession and submission, stretching thirty-five years back and ten thousand years into the future—all for the pleasure of our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda. I pray that for a little while longer I can continue to play my little impresario’s role in this vast and variegated *kīrtana,* preparing for the day when we will all rejoin Śrīla Prabhupāda in Goloka and can once again sing his praises in his personal presence.

Draviḍa dāsa  
Vyāsa-pūjā book editor, 1987–2003

## Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Laxmimonī-devī dāsī

In many cultures, one or two days are set aside each year for honoring mothers and fathers—a day for remembering loving care they showed us and the lessons they taught us throughout our lives. On this day we say “Thank you” for the simple training they gave us in the basic skills like eating, dressing, and mannerly behavior. We take these lessons for granted as we go about our daily routine, and so on these special days we are reminded to honor our father and mother.

In our Śrīla Prabhupāda we have the embodiment of the perfect mother and father, for Śrīla Prabhupāda took us from the ignorance of birth in this material world, gave us rebirth, retrained us so that we would eat, dress, and behave to please Kṛṣṇa, and taught us everything we need to know to make this lifetime successful by advancing towards *ananya-bhakti*.

It is possible that as the years pass and we daily practice our spiritual-life skills, we may forget how we first heard of Kṛṣṇa, His *prasādam,* His holy name, and His devotional service. Vyāsa-pūjā is an opportunity to meditate on that extraordinary person who sacrificed his life to give us this precious knowledge.

To our mother or father we may send a card with some touching words, or some roses perhaps. We may gather together with family members and speak of days gone by when we were ignorant and how we now appreciate life’s lessons although at the time we could not understand their importance.

But as I contemplate the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā, I am drawn to an image of a Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony held for His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī in 1961. Abhay Charan De is addressing an assembly of Godbrothers, senior to him in age and overt spiritual practice, yet he speaks boldly:

Now all of you are returning here on his order to worship him on his Vyāsa-pūjā day—  
All together you are engaged in his worship.  
But a festival of flowers and fruits is not the real worship:  
One who serves the guru’s message really worships him.  
The service of the message is the real transcendental vibration.

Vyāsa-pūjā is our opportunity to remember and honor the exceptional capacity Śrīla Prabhupāda had to flawlessly craft the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and to broadcast Lord Caitanya’s message through out the world

On this day we can remember how he stands far and above all other *prabhus*. As his own Vyāsa-pūjā offering described the uncommon vision of his Guru Mahārāja, so it describes perfectly his own exceptional foresight.

To see a sense enjoyer’s face is just like drinking poison,  
But you, master, are giving your darśana here and far away in foreign lands.  
*Mlecchas* and *yavanas* are not allowed to enter Hindu temples,  
But you, master, make them the chairman and sit them in the assembly.  
To cross the ocean is not allowed for Hindus,  
But you send your devotees across the ocean.  
In Kali-yuga’s cities the guru’s instructions are “forbidden.”  
But you remain there anyway with your special purpose in mind.  
The devotees want to go to some lonely place to perform their *bhajan* alone,  
But you don’t accept this in your judgment.  
Where there is an increase in the measurement of people,  
There your preaching work is to be seen.

Vyāsa-pūjā is an opportunity to redouble our dedication to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s real homage to his Guru Mahārāja was the offering of his life.

His one question to *his* Śrīla Prabhupāda was, “How can I serve you?” And in a letter to a senior disciple in 1976, *our* Śrīla Prabhupāda himself answers that question.

I am very pleased that you have taken up this mission of spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. This is the wish of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, that: *yāre dekha, tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa:* everyone you see or meet, tell him about Krsna; *āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra ei deśa:* and by My command you become a guru and save this land. This was also the mission of my Guru Mahārāja and it is my mission. You will perfect your life if you make it also your mission.

Vyāsa-pūjā is an opportunity to focus on this mission and plan how we will work together to spread His message to every town and village. This day is the first day of 364 others in which we can spread Śrīla Prabhupāda’s glories and rededicate our lives in his service. As individuals and as members of the movement to which he devoted all his energy, we can begin, on Śrīla Prabhupāda’s birth anniversary, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, to chart the course and become the force that is destined to shape the world for the next 10,000 years.

With profound insight Śrīla Prabhupāda addressed his Godbrothers in 1961, and in 2003 he continues to instruct us with the same perfect message:

The Vaiṣṇavas are famous for being sympathetic towards others’ suffering,  
And that fame will be increased by mature preaching.  
If eternally pure devotional service to Kṛṣṇa is awakened in everyone,  
Kali will flee of his own accord, crying “Ha! Ha!”  
Mahāprabhu’s message is *prāniṇām upakārāya:*  
Act for the welfare of others. This is the storehouse of happiness both now and after death.  
So much work has fallen into your hands:  
Everyone should join together and do something about it.  
The world is simply insurmountable Māyā.  
If there were a kingdom of Viṣṇu and Vaiṣṇavas on earth,  
Then everyone would be happy, say the saintly persons and sages.

Vyāsa-pūjā is one day, set aside each year, to show our love for Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* who compiled much of the Vedic literature, and the disciplic succession coming from him. But that is just the beginning. On this day we can cooperate and honor Śrīla Prabhupāda by making a resolution to show him, as he showed his Guru Mahārāja, that our *bhakti* will be manifest throughout the year in fearless action.

Laxmimonī-devī dāsī  
Headmistress of the Vaiṣṇava Academy for Girls, in Alachua Florida, USA.

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please guide us through this dark age of quarrel and hypocrisy.

**“O learned one, in this iron age of Kali men have but short lives. They are quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky, and, above all, always disturbed.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.10)**

We live today in a world torn by violence. During the past year or so, the quarrelsome nature of the human race has become much more visible. People are so determined to make war upon one another! We live in a world torn by international tension. Nations stand armed with weapons of mass destruction and engage in “unbeneficial, horrible works meant to destroy the world.”

People snarl at one another like cats and dogs across imaginary lines of national borders that just a few generations ago did not exist—and one or two generations forward will cease to be remembered. Yet they make terror against each other. They kill children, women, and the unfortunate animals by the thousands and millions.

Whether aroused by fundamentalism or nationalism, the desire for power or the desire for money, the desire to control or the desire to consume—such bewildered people make life hellish for others and for themselves.

In the midst of the distress of this modern age, innocent people seek shelter and a source of hope. Many seek to escape through the illusions of intoxication, the entertainment industries, illicit sex, gambling, and other distractions that bring at best temporary relief from the emptiness of material experience.

We, your followers, are most fortunate. We have been blessed by Lord Caitanya, who sent you to interrupt our purposeless lives. You bring sanity. You bring light. In the midst of the craziness, strife, and tears of our lives, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you give us hope. You bring down the fever of material consciousness. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your kindness.

You give us hope by your example. You give us hope by your soothing words. You give us hope that the world does have purpose. You give us hope that Kṛṣṇa is our supreme and best friend. You give us hope by exposing the faults of this materialistic world. You give us hope that the human spirit can be reformed through the process of *bhakti*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, under your order I have traveled much the past two years. You showed me many things that give cause for hope. Today, by your grace, our movement is growing stronger. We are maturing. Below is a list of a few of your gifts that you have shown me. I recite them to you today in appreciation of your greatness and your mercy:

**Your Tirupati temple**—where devotees work cooperatively with leaders of this famous *tīrtha* in the Rāmānuja line. Devotees here sell your books in huge numbers at the four major temples and are welcomed as friends and partners in worshiping the Lord.

**Your scientists**—who travel the world speaking to great thinkers of the day. They argue powerfully, on the basis of both empirical and śāstric research, that modern theories of universal structure and Darwinian evolution, upon which much materialism is based, are flawed and must be reexamined.

**Your Hungarian devotees**—who through lobbying and networking, praying and preaching, have changed government policy as they learn not just to protect our religious rights but to impact the policies and values of a nation.

**Your museum in New Delhi**—which utilizes sophisticated technology to portray the Lord’s teachings and *līlā* and is inspiring thousands of Indians to look upon their culture with fresh eyes.

**Your GBC**—which empowered a Śāstric Advisory Council (SAC) to study carefully questions of *siddhānta* and the application of our faith, and which now weighs that advice carefully, and humbly, striving to be free from passion and the desire to control.

**Your** ***sannyāsīs***—who, purified of ego and full of love and humility, sit in attendance at the weddings of our children and offer blessings for happy lives in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, built on commitment and respect.

**Your devotee scholars**—who have met for two days of dialog, six years in a row, with leading Christian theologians and professors in Washington, D.C., to discuss our common bonds of love and service to the Godhead.

**Your child protection office**—which courageously faces the greatest of all sins within a religious community and attracts the praise of professionals in child protection for its excellent work.

**Your congregational devotees worldwide**—who are now leaders in book distribution and dedication to preaching.

**Your Bhaktivedanta College in Radhadesh**—which just completed its first year of devotional and academic education and is close to becoming accredited by England’s Birmingham University.

**Your new mediation program**—which, under the tutelage of Professor Arnold Zack, an international expert, is developing systems of dispute resolution for ISKCON members to address their grievances and thus make our society more just.

**Your ISCOWP devotees**—who are now working with international experts to develop financially viable methods of protecting and caring for cows.

**Your Food for Life project in Vṛndāvana—**where residents of that holy city know that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers care about the well-being of the less fortunate and are dedicated to giving *prasādam* to thousands daily. Similar programs are going on in other places throughout the ISKCON world.

**Your grandchildren in the West**—who are using theater and other means to challenge their elders to face our shortcomings, and who are coaxing us to look upon their generation as equal partners holding valid visions and aspirations in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

**Your communities**—which are beginning to require new couples to accept premarital counseling, recognizing that all vows before the fire are sacred and require work to maintain.

**Your GBC members**—who are cooperating more and more, learning to discuss and critique each other with a spirit of affection arising from a desire for all to succeed in your service.

**Your disciples in Māyāpur**—who are taking leadership within the association of Gauḍīya Maṭha temples and are helping to build cooperation and respect in the shared glorification of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

**Your North American managers**—who are meeting regularly to boldly apply the techniques of *yukta-vairāgya,* modern business models, and strategic planning to “systematically propagate spiritual knowledge to society at large...”

**Your young Indian professionals**—who in India and around the world are taking up Kṛṣṇa consciousness with intelligence, depth of conviction, and the ability to influence world leaders in ways not previously possible.

**Your devotees in Oxford**—who have made the Oxford Center for Vaishnava and Hindu Studies a respectable institution in less than four years, who now court queens and ministers, nurture PhD’s in your service, count lords among their patrons, and who have become an articulate, powerful voice of the Vedic tradition emanating from the world’s most prestigious university.

These are just a few of your recent wonderful accomplishments, Śrīla Prabhupāda, executed by your servants, under your orders. They are your gifts. They give hope to your followers. They are proof of your continued presence in the world, and of the potency of your mission and your instructions. You remain a beacon of hope in a world that sometimes appears hopeless. You give us light in an age of darkness. Please let us come into that light, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And, as your servants, let us reflect and share that light—and our hope—with all the world.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Anuttama dāsa

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The older I get, the more I realize that everything you said is true; not only in terms of philosophy, but in your instructions for that even most basic of desires: a peaceful and happy life.

In the spring of 1970 I made a decision—some would say with the rashness of youth—to try my best to follow your instructions. To many at the time, this seemed like a risky venture. Naturally, I had my own doubts as well. Now, thirty-plus years have come and gone. I have passed the milestone of fifty years old, and time has taken its toll. But it has also given me the chance to compare my life to the experiences of my friends and family, those who so doubted my fateful resolution those many years ago.

Once, we all stood at the same juncture. They made their choices, and I made mine. At the time, the lines seemed parallel. They had their ideas of what would produce a life full of happiness and worth, and so did I. Now, with the passing of time, I can see how much those lines have diverged, how different the results received.

My roommate at the time told me, “What is the use of God and religion? I have everything I want and never said a prayer in my life.” Two years ago, without any warning, he dropped dead of a heart attack. Thus he was forced to leave aside his beautiful home and loving family and go to a destination unknown and unprepared for.

A close relative recently confided in me that in college she had had an abortion. She was unmarried and on a promising academic path. To carry the child to term would be a great “burden,” she was told. So, with the promise of greater happiness, she did the deed. Now, she has a recurring dream. The unborn child comes to her and asks, “Why?” She’s convinced this is the son she and her husband always wanted but could never manage to conceive.

The above is just a sampling of the tales of angst, anger, and emptiness I hear when old friends and relatives drop their guard. Without an understanding of past lives, and the present life’s purpose as a progression toward full consciousness, without the words of a saintly person to guide them, there is no way to make sense of it all.

We recently observed Rāma-navamī. Sītā was protected from Rāvaṇa’s grasp as long as she stayed within the ring of fire created by Lakṣmaṇa. Similarly, as long as I stay within the ring of your instructions, I am protected from the grip of material nature. Please continue to give me the shelter of your perfect guidance.

*Your servant,*  
Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Today is Rāmanavamī, the Appearance Day of Lord Rāmacandra, and I am remembering how you carried His mission forward within this material world in a way He must certainly be proud of.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī says:

*nādhi-vyādhi-jarā-glāni-duḥkha-śoka-bhaya-klamāḥ*  
*mṛtyuś cānicchatāṁ nāsīd rāme rājany adhokṣaje*

**“When Lord Rāmacandra, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, was the King of this world, all bodily and mental suffering, disease, old age, bereavement, lamentation, distress, fear, and fatigue were completely absent. There was even no death for those who did not want it.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **9.10.53)**

In your purport to this verse you state,

All these facilities existed because of Lord Rāmacandra’s presence as the King of the entire world. A similar situation could be introduced immediately, even in this age called Kali, the worst of all ages. It is said, *kali-kāle nāma-rūpe kṛṣṇa-avatāra:* Kṛṣṇa descends in this Kali-yuga in the form of His holy name—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Rāma. If we chant offenselessly, Rāma and Kṛṣṇa are still present in this age. The kingdom of Rāma was immensely popular and beneficial, and the spreading of this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement can immediately introduce a similar situation, even in this Kali-yuga.

To many this may sound too fantastic to be taken seriously, but we see that you took it seriously, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You had that special type of faith in the Lord which distinguishes the spiritually great from others. Without that type of faith you could not have done what you did, and without being connected with you and being inspired by your faith and the vision you had on the basis of that faith, no one would be able to push forward the movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and establish it in this almost impossibly Godless place.

You have given us so many invaluable things, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and this gift of your faith is one of those gifts. Because it is there we can appreciate that there is the possibility of making the whole world Kṛṣṇa conscious, even though many of your contemporaries gave up that idea long ago and were content to maintain a small *maṭha* and raise enough money to keep themselves fed.

Lord Caitanya gives the definition of faith:

*‘śraddhā’-śabde——viśvāsa kahe sudṛḍha niścaya*  
*kṛṣṇe bhakti kaile sarva-karma kṛta haya*

**“*****Śraddhā*** **is confident, firm faith that by rendering transcendental loving service to Kṛṣṇa one automatically performs all subsidiary activities. Such faith is favorable to the discharge of devotional service.”**

In one lecture you commented:

This is *śraddhā*. *Śraddhā* means *śraddhā-śabde viśvāsa,* full faith; *sudṛḍha,* very firm; *niścaya,* with conviction. That is *śraddhā. Śraddhā-śabde viśvāsa kahe sudṛḍha niścaya.* What is that *viśvāsa*? What is that faith? *Kṛṣṇe bhakti kaile sarva-karma kṛta haya*. “If I devote myself in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, in the service of Kṛṣṇa, all my work will be perfect.” This is called *śraddhā*. If you have got hesitation, that “Some percentage to Kṛṣṇa, some percentage to my family, some percent to my country, some percent to my dog, some percentage...” No. Simply *sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁ śaraṇaṁ vraja*. That faith, if you can raise yourself to that platform of faith, that is the beginning of faith.

You often quoted the *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* 6.23 verse:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.”**

We saw that in you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. There was never any question of an alternative. You came armed simply with your faith in your spiritual master, and your enthusiasm to carry out his mission, and on the basis of these things you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the whole world.

And how to keep this faith strong? You explained once:

So how this *śraddhā,* this faith can be increased? *Sādhu-saṅga*. If you keep yourself associated with the devotees, then this *śraddhā* can be increased. If you simply believe, ‘Yes, Kṛṣṇa is Supreme Personality,’ but you do not live with the *sādhus* or devotees, then it will drop. It will finish.

For us that means keeping your association through your books and instructions, and keeping the association of those who are faithful to you. That’s all. We are praying to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day of your appearance in this world, to instill in our hearts a drop of this same faith.

Praying in the dust of your lotus feet for your mercy,

*Your servant,*  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

You wanted us to maintain what you gave us. And out of all of your assets, you reminded us, your devotees were your most precious possessions. Whenever I think of you, you inevitably appear surrounded by your devotees. In their midst you look so glorious—like a king surrounded by his generals or the moon by the stars. All those devotees were such special individuals who loved you so much and achieved so much for you.

Those were such glorious days for all of us! All the temples were full of enthusiastic devotees, everyone was so absorbed in their service, and everywhere there were signs of prosperity and abundance, as if, due to your presence, Lakṣmī-devī was showering her abundant mercy everywhere.

All those leading devotees were such distinctly different individuals, yet they were so wonderfully united with you in the center. So many pictures have vividly captured those moments, but I prefer to close my eyes and let my mind drift down memory lane...I see Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja walking into your room with a large group of young *brahmacārīs,* their faces brighter than their saffron robes. One by one they offer flowers at your lotus feet, and you smile at them in acceptance. Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja announces with a voice resounding with grave determination: “This is my offering to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please accept it.”

Bhagavān Prabhu strides in with a group of his effulgent assistants. To me he appears like Indra surrounded by the demigods. He reports to you about his achievements—so many thousands of books being distributed every day, so many gorgeous temples opened, and so many devotees joining due to extremely effective preaching. Your face lights up with a beautiful smile as you listen. I see tears roll down the cheeks of the devotees. What will they not do to win your favor and receive your mercy?

When you were informed that Rāmeśvara Swami had arrived, you asked someone to call him. He appears to be rather shy in front of you, but answers your questions with absolute confidence. You are naturally pleased with him because he is taking care of all your affairs with such sincerity and determination. He reports how book distribution doubled all over the world and book printing was going on at an even faster speed. He tells how the community of New Dvārakā was growing so wonderfully, and so many devotees under his able guidance were accomplishing so much for Your Divine Grace. You acknowledge his service, saying that he is the most intelligent among all your devotees.

Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja informs you that Kīrtanānanda Swami has just arrived. Your face lights up with a bright smile, and you naturally want to see him immediately. He comes and sits in front of you like a little child and opens up some beautiful paintings of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and many other articles that the devotees of New Vrindavan have sent you. Then he shows you the pictures of your “Palace of Gold.” Now it is almost ready to receive you. You are so excited to see this very special gift from your very wonderful devotees. You immediately start to plan to go there and spend your time in the association of your wonderful devotees at New Vrindavan.

Harikeśa Swami comes with volumes of books he just printed in Russian and other East European languages. You glorify him for his unique achievements and remind him, “Now you know why I sent you there. You did not want to go. You thought that I was removing you from your service to me. But now you understand why I sent you there?”

Harikeśa starts to sob like a child. You silently caress his head, with tears rolling down your cheeks.

Bhavānanda Swami came to see you in Vṛndāvana from Māyāpur. His body is emaciated due to the austerities he had been performing during Cāturmāsya, and he has also grown a beard. You ask him to come close to you. He kneels down beside your bed and holds your hand. To protect your devotees in Māyāpur from the attack of the miscreants, he risked his life and suffered acute public humiliation. Looking at him, you remember all the sacrifices he has made for you, and tears roll down your cheeks. He also begins to cry and then buries his head at your lotus feet.

Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja informs you about Haṁsadūta Goswami’s success in defeating an atheistic scientist in Colombo named Dr. Kovoor, who claimed that life comes from matter. Haṁsadūta defeated him in public in a brilliant way. He hired a very prestigious auditorium and challenged him that if he could create life out of matter then he would give him Rs. 500,000. The written exchanges that took place between them, ultimately leading to Dr. Kovoor’s embarrassing defeat, were printed in one of the most prominent newspapers for few days in a row, and created quite a sensation. You were so pleased with this news of victory that you wanted to see Haṁsadūta immediately, and when he came you showered your abundant mercy upon him. I could see how proud you were of your glorious son’s outstanding achievement.

All these devotees, and many others who surrounded you at that time, are your very precious possessions. I do not know whether they will eternally be with you in the spiritual sky, but in my memory they will always remain as my very special recollections.

I have not been able to properly maintain what you so mercifully gave us. I may have also failed to express my love for you through my cooperation with other devotees. But deep down in my heart I will keep these memories as my precious spiritual wealth, and remain ever grateful to these devotees who enabled me to recognize your real greatness and glory, and who helped me surrender myself unto your lotus feet.

*Your eternally indebted son,*  
Bhakti Chāru Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You said, “Sit properly!”  
To some particular soul,  
Someone needing firmness,  
A reminder of the goal.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
Addressing one’s poor posture,  
Concern for his body,  
Ill health that it will foster.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
And so assertively  
It came, with much compassion,  
While spoken seriously.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
At a wee hour in the morn’.  
It applies to all the day, though,  
Whether happy or forlorn.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
And said it only once,  
Burst the dreamy bubble  
As though a wake-up punch.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
Meaning “give full attention,”  
Aware of all our actions  
It’s important that you mention.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
e straight in all we do.  
Duplicity is rotten,  
Two-face is a taboo.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
At the time when we do chant,  
Lips and tongue in motion,  
Never say, “We can’t.”

You said, “Sit properly!”  
A message meant for me.  
Said with tender intent,  
It resonates so strongly.

You said, “Sit properly!”  
It’s documented stuff,  
Etched in stone,  
Your word should be enough.

Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare

SIT PROPERLY!

*Your servant,*  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Most revered Śrīla Prabhupāda,

At present I am visiting one of the most beautiful Indonesian islands, Bali. Although Indonesia is the most populous Muslim country in the world, in Bali the Hindus are the majority—the only such island in Indonesia. By their fierce *kṣatriya* spirit, the Hindus resisted conversion.

I was invited to address the International Interreligious Youth Camp for Peace, held here at Klungkung on April 3, 2003. Many local leaders and youths from other religious traditions participated in the program. Our devotees of ISKCON Bali also participated in large numbers. One of the highlights of the program was the interreligious *padayātrā*, dubbed Pilgrims of Peace. Toward the end of the *padayātrā,* we all stopped at a large intersection, and each religious group performed a prayer and chanting for world peace from its tradition, while all the others stood around and watched.

I was thrilled to see for the first time in my life that our Muslim brothers have their own traditional form of *saṅkīrtana*. They were all dressed in smart uniforms, with two men carrying swords at the front. In the back the participants were reciting Islamic prayers, while the participants in uniform danced and moved their bodies in rhythm with the drums. It was wonderful to see their culture of *saṅkīrtana*.

When our turn came, and our Balinese devotees began chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* with their sweet voices, accompanied by the sound of conchshells and *mṛdaṅgas* and *karatālas,* something divine happened. Everyone, including our Islamic brothers and sisters and all others, could not restrain themselves and just watch. *Everyone spontaneously joined in the chanting and dancing with great ecstasy*. Everyone felt extremely happy to have the blessings of *hari-nāma mahā-mantra*. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your divine mercy is visible in every part of the globe, and your divine mission is expanding in all directions. Seeing this festival, we felt great hope that by your divine mercy we will soon be able to see all the youths of different communities and traditions of the world join hands in worshipping the Supreme Lord through the culture of *saṅkīrtana,* as predicted by our most merciful Lord Gaurāṅga.

The world is increasingly infected by the lower modes of material nature. The influence of Kali-yuga is becoming stronger and stronger. War, terrorist activities of various forms, new dangerous diseases, and so on have created great fear and anxiety in everyone’s mind. And the material civilization has been totally shaken. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your divine message of taking shelter of the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the only medicine. At this critical time, all the big material scientists, philosophers, atheists, etc., must be feeling totally helpless. The present situation is the greatest opportunity to accelerate your divine mission for the welfare of everyone. Even some of the leading scientists of the world are making serious attempts to undertake research in spiritual fields in order to solve some of the difficult problems of life. Serious research is going on in various parts of the world, in areas such as proving the existence of God from scientific argument; a scientific study of spiritual transformation and of spirituality’s influence in the lives of people; and a scientific explanation of unconditional love (*bhakti*).

Your divine vision of transforming the hearts of materialistic scientists is beginning to be realized to some degree.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless this most lowly servant to continue humbly serving your divine instructions to influence the world’s educational systems with spirituality through scientific and cultural interaction with all peoples. If the youths who are crowding the colleges and universities of the world are inspired to study spiritual science and culture, then world peace is surely conceivable, as your divine grace has described in your purport to the “peace formula” verse (*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.29). Spiritual education means recognizing that the Supreme Lord is the ultimate beneficiary of all our sacrifices and activities, and that He is the well-wisher and benefactor of all living beings. Therefore, education should be engaged wholly and solely for service of the Lord. On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I humbly beg again for your continued guidance, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā I want to thank you for the wonderful international family you have given us. There are so many wonderful leaders and devotees who are dedicated to your mission. By your mercy, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are all getting a chance to serve you by cooperating together.

I have never before, however, experienced such mixed and contradictory emotions in my life. First, let me say I am full of gratitude. I see beauty in devotees’ projects, and opportunities and adventures everywhere. There are so many wonderful chances to engage in all kinds of services and projects. I am finding preaching opportunities that I never felt I would be involved in manifesting in many countries within the devotee communities as well as in the secular communities. But at the same time, I am finding that in so many areas very basic things, which I previously felt comfortable with, are presenting various challenges. This is frightening at times, but I am seeing this all as birth pains that are necessary to bring on a much deeper level of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and commitment to your movement.

One of the areas I find disturbing is the amount of suffering I find in the secular as well as the devotee community. I have been praying to you intensely to help me be a change agent who can help tremendously to remove or minimize some of these sufferings. My inability to do much in this area is a great source of suffering for me. I am also disappointed because I know I should be following better the instructions you once gave me. You said that I should be “Humble, Selfless, and Brave.” You know the full purport of these words. I can only appreciate them superficially. So I beg you to force me to be a carrier of all of these qualities, for in this way I can heal others by better healing myself.

The planet is rapidly becoming infested with so many demonic influences. Therefore, there is a greater need than ever before for the devotees of the Lord to help offset the imbalances. So I beg you to allow me to assist you more effectively in this area.

*Your son who is in desperate need of your mercy,*  
Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivijñāna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

As the time goes by, I become more and more acutely aware of my helplessness. By the mercy of the holy name my false pride gradually diminishes, and sometimes the sense of genuine helplessness shines through in my habitually proud heart. The day of your Vyāsa-pūjā is a very special day, because it gives us a chance to think deeply on the matter of our helplessness and our complete dependence on your mercy.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī, speaking about the deviant branches descending from Advaita Ācārya, mentions that those branches of the Caitanya tree that do not receive the water of His mercy gradually wither and vanish. The life in the Caitanya tree is coming from His mercy only. There is no other sustenance for this tree save and except His mercy. The flourishing of one branch is made possible not by some managerial skills, not by some magical tricks, wealth, or even austerity, but only by its connection with the main trunk. Austerity, or wealth, or any other material means can create some illusion of spiritual prosperity, but ultimately spiritual well-being depends on mercy only. Sometimes we see that some group of devotees temporarily becomes prominent by some of the means mentioned above, but we can also be sure that it will not last long if it has no real connection with the flow of Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

Perhaps your ISKCON Society is the best illustration of this principle. Our connection with the tree of Lord Caitanya is through you only, and if this connection is established, the real long-term success and prosperity of this Society is guaranteed. And vice versa: wherever the connection with you is weakened, that branch gradually withers and vanishes. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can see very clearly that it is through you only that the nourishment of Lord Caitanya’s mercy comes, because you are so firmly and intimately connected with the trunk of this tree.

Your connection with the tree of Lord Caitanya is so gloriously obvious in everything you did and said that even an outsider can see it. Recently I received a letter I would like to share with you. A lady from a little town in East Siberia who happened to read your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* writes:

For a long time I was interested in Indian philosophy. But when for the first time I read your *Bhagavad-gita*, the tears constantly flowed from my eyes, because everything I read there was true. I have found many answers to my questions in this book, answers I could not find anywhere else. Since that time I have read *Bhagavad-gita* many times, over and over again. Now I want to read Vedanta-sutra. Please send me this book. I will be waiting for it.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this fortunate lady has understood something very essential. She has understood that because of your inseparable connection with the Absolute Truth, everything you said is also truth. I almost cried when I read this in her letter. I wish I could understand this too, as nicely as this simple lady. Sometimes we are tempted to seek the truth somewhere outside your teachings, or to seek mercy somewhere outside your Society and our connection with you. All these attempts will ultimately be frustrated, and today I pray at your feet, “Please give me enough intelligence not to seek mercy or knowledge anywhere else. Please give me enough intelligence to realize my utter dependence on you, the agent of Lord Caitanya’s mercy.”

*Begging for your causeless mercy,*  
Bhakti Vijñāna Goswami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Several months ago, when preparing to speak on Nārada Muni’s instructions to Vyāsadeva in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* I came across several lines within one of your purports that I had read many times before:

It is a qualification of the great thinkers to pick up the best even from the worst. It is said that the intelligent man should pick up nectar from a stock of poison, should accept gold even from a filthy place, should accept a good and qualified wife even from an obscure family, and should accept a good lesson even from a man or from a teacher who comes from the untouchables. These are some of the ethical instructions for everyone in every place without exception. But a saint is far above the level of an ordinary man. He is always absorbed in glorifying the Supreme Lord because by broadcasting the holy name and fame of the Supreme Lord, the polluted atmosphere of the world will change, and as a result of propagating the transcendental literatures like *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* people will become sane in their transactions. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.11, purport\]

I was struck by how attracted I was to “picking up the best even from the worst.” I almost didn’t wish to read the lines that I knew would follow. Of course, I am neither a great thinker nor intelligent, but upon reading your words, I clearly experienced your gentle but firm insistence that my mood was insufficient for one wishing to become a “saint.” Your words pushed me practically as well. To become a devotee, I would have to become “always absorbed in glorifying the Supreme Lord.” I especially noted the word “absorbed.”

The intrusive pressure of your words probed the depth of my avowed desire. On one side, I experienced the sincerity of my desire to serve you being questioned; on the other, I wondered what inspiration could possibly impel me to transcend my personal limitations as well as my petty attachments and desire for comfort.

But you, through your purport, were not finished with me yet. You next offered motivation: “\[B\]y broadcasting the holy name and fame of the Supreme Lord, the polluted atmosphere of the world will change, and as a result of propagating the transcendental literatures like *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* people will become sane in their transactions.”

I felt the tingle of a impending inspiration, for even cloistered me can see the need for the above result, especially the hope of people becoming “sane in their transactions.” But a larger problem then began to reveal itself.

Did I truly believe that the world would change as a result of the propagation of the *Bhāgavatam* and its message? Could I honestly even say that the small part of the world that I mostly deal with—the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement—who’s lifeblood is to accept the message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*—has become free from “quarrel and trouble,” free from materialistic “pollution,” and become “cooperative” and “sane”? Has that “change of heart” even been installed as a permanent asset of the movement?

What once was simple faith had been challenged by thirty-five years of experience.

Yet you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, remained “confident” and “certain”:

We are confident that if the transcendental message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is received only by the leading men of the world, certainly there will be a change of heart, and naturally the people in general will follow them. The mass of people in general are tools in the hands of the modern politicians and leaders of the people. If there is a change of heart of the leaders only, certainly there will be a radical change in the atmosphere of the world.

I also fell back to reflecting upon the storehouse of your gifts that I have accumulated during the thirty-five years of my attempting to serve you: I have noted that the *Bhāgavatam* with your Bhaktivedanta Purports is truly transcendental. I am unable to deny either the potency of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, holy association, the supramundane sweetness of the Deities, and especially your gift to me of Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma. Despite my unlimited deficiencies, I have also noted the truth of your promise of Kṛṣṇa’s protection.

Although my weak faith had been confronted by you in the form of your words, as well as by your merciful gifts, I needed still more instructions to push me forward.

Your purport next explained why difficulties occur even amongst devotees:

When there are too many materialistic activities by the people in general all over the world, there is no wonder that a person or a nation attacks another person or nation on slight provocation. That is the rule of this age of Kali or quarrel. The atmosphere is already polluted with corruption of all description, and everyone knows it well.

You then offered me a personal instruction, a service:

Similarly, let there be systematic propaganda for popularizing reading of the *Bhagavad-gītā* and the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which will act like sugar candy for the jaundicelike condition of sense gratification. When men have a taste for this literature, the other literatures, which are catering poison to society, will then automatically cease.

Your purport also offered hints as to how it could be done:

After all, it is a technical science of spiritual values, and thus we are concerned with the techniques and not with the language. If the techniques of this great literature are understood by the people of the world, there will be success.

And instructions on proper moods while serving:

But we are sure that with all our faults in this connection the seriousness of the subject matter will be taken into consideration, and the leaders of society will still accept this due to its being an honest attempt to glorify the Almighty God.

And

When there is fire in a house, the inmates of the house go out to get help from the neighbors who may be foreigners, and yet without knowing the language the victims of the fire express themselves, and the neighbors understand the need, even though not expressed in the same language. The same spirit of cooperation is needed to broadcast this transcendental message of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* throughout the polluted atmosphere of the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you then concluded your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.11with the following sentence: “We are sure, therefore, that everyone in human society will welcome *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* even though it is now presented with so many faults, for it is recommended by Śrī Nārada, who has very kindly appeared in this chapter.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for also kindly appearing in that chapter.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

I have been meditating upon your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.50, wherein you state:

A devoted disciple of the spiritual master would rather die with the spiritual master than fail to execute the spiritual master’s mission. As the Supreme Personality of Godhead comes down upon this earth to reestablish the principles of religion, so His representative, the spiritual master, also comes to reestablish religious principles. It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly. Otherwise the disciple should decide to die along with the spiritual master. In other words, to execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations.

In this purport you have given us the recipe for attaining pure devotional service in this lifetime through the austerity that surpasses all other austerities in importance. Certainly you personally exemplified this dedication to the mission of your guru.

However, both of these instructions—“to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly,” and “to abandon all personal considerations”—have to be understood in the context of all your directions. For example, one should not think that the statement “abandon all personal considerations” means that one should neglect his or her own spiritual practices or health.

In fact, without proper spiritual practices one will not be able to carry out your mission. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, in his *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu (*1.2.101), explains this principle:

*śruti-smṛti-purāṇādi-pañcarātra-vidhiṁ vinā*  
*aikāntikī harer bhaktir utpātāyaiva kalpate*

**“Devotional service of the Lord that ignores the authorized Vedic literatures like the** ***Upaniṣads, Purāṇas,*** **and** ***Nārada Pañcarātra*** **is simply an unnecessary disturbance in society.”**

In other words, if we neglect spiritual practices in the name of pushing forward your movement, our actions will be a disturbance to your Society instead of a help. Those essential spiritual practices are listed in your books, such as *The Nectar of Devotion* and *The Nectar of Instruction*.

And, you have instructed that the most important service of your GBC leaders is to see that the devotees are following these spiritual practices. You wrote to Haṁsadūta in February, 1972,

So as GBC you must see to it that the highest standards of routine work are maintained throughout all the centers, and that chanting, rising early, cleansing, and all other aspects of our regular program may not be neglected. That is our first business. GBC men should not dictate very much.”

Therefore, in order to carry out your mission in your absence, my highest priority is to set a good example of *sādhana* for the devotees and to educate others in good *sādhana*. It is important that the devotees be trained in good *sādhana* from the very beginning of their spiritual lives, and if possible from an early age.

The first time I saw you, a reporter asked you why everyone there was so young. You replied with a question about the age for education. The reporter replied that generally youth is the age for education. Then you stated, “Yes, because it is difficult to teach an old dog new tricks.” Thus I understood it is important for me and those I instruct to develop good *sādhana* habits while we are young. Practically I can see that this simple statement of yours—“It is difficult to teach an old dog new tricks”—is absolutely true. Habits developed while one is young stick with one throughout one’s life.

So “abandon all personal considerations means” that one should understand that everyone belongs to Kṛṣṇa and should be engaged in His service twenty-four hours a day under your direction. As far as “taking care of the mission of the spiritual master and executing it properly” is concerned, this must be done in terms of your direction and under the jurisdiction of your GBC body, which is your representative.

If one executes your will in this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there will never be any separation from your lotus feet, and pure devotional service will be quickly achieved by your mercy. Please bless me with the determination to be your chaste disciple whose only reason for living is to serve your mission according to your directions.

*Your servant,*  
Bīr Kṛṣṇa dāsa Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Day by day, as I meditate upon your legacy, the more I conclude that your genius is beyond my calculation. Your divine corpus of books, instructions, and orders—what to speak of your example—sustained us during your manifest presence. Most importantly, now your *vāṇī* successfully carries us through the heartbreaks and exultations since your *vapu* has gone.

Today your remaining disciples are nearing the sunset of their own time in this world. Indeed, some have already passed on. I wonder, What will be our legacy? What will we leave behind to sustain the next generation of servant-leaders in your mission? How can we inspire another wave of devotees to champion your desires, through thick and thin?

Upon studying the aftermaths of other contemporary Vaiṣṇavas and what the disciples do with their legacy, I can see no greater example of handing down an inheritance than yours. You facilitated our spiritual lives when you were physically present. But most importantly, you also took great pains to love us when you were gone. That is, you gave us a mission, a structure, and guidelines that would, in your physical absence, not only shelter us but also propel us forward.

How rarely fortunate are we to have a father who left his future generations such a magnificent Vaiṣṇava bequest!

Now I can see that the inheritance from a Vaiṣṇava has to be surcharged enough to sustain the disciple’s enthusiasm and stamina throughout the routine trials and tribulations of the devotional journey through this world. Also, the *paramparā* inheritance has to grant disciples an organized preaching mission for their future development and the development of the world.

Besides precious personal nourishment, you gave us an institutional framework for achieving your desires. How can I find anyone more mercifully expert than you? The level of care you supplied, both personally and organizationally, is incomparable. Let us declare it boldly: No disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī understood him better than you!

Obviously you are more than just a link in the *paramparā*. You are the *ācārya extraordinaire,* who constructed the very stage upon which we little bit players sing and dance. We will come and go, as new players take our place; but your stage, script, and score will remain. I beg you that my lowly self may leave this world in the consciousness of a selfless servant of your divine mission and will.

Your followers, unlike other Vaiṣṇava lineages, are responsible to leave behind more than just a guru-disciple relationship. We must also bequeath the continuity of the worldwide organization you labored so valiantly to establish. Our sacred task is to instill in at least some of our dependents a commitment to staff ISKCON and push it forth. Minor adjustments may be necessary, but your sacred core must march on, to the goals you have preordained.

Lastly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive me if I sound like a giddy teenager gushing from the heart, but I want so much to say that I love your books. They enraptured my heart and mind on our very first date. Such an imbecile was I—a puffed-up Yale sophist and New York sophisticate. A prancing dog of Kali-yuga, I was diving freestyle into *puṇya* depletion and *pāpa* inundation.

But then I met a life-saving *saṅkīrtana* devotee at a subway station in Jersey City. Attracted by his soft heart, I purchased *The Transcendental Teachings of Prahlāda Mahārāja*. One hour later, sitting down to read it, I had only reached page three when your empowered words literally dropped me to the floor:

You are after sense gratification, but sense gratification is automatically achieved by contact with this body. Because a hog has a certain type of body, his sense gratification comes from eating stool, the very thing that is most obnoxious to you. At once, after evacuating, you leave to get free from the bad smell—but the hog is waiting. As soon as you evacuate, he will at once enjoy. So there are different types of sense gratification according to different types of body. Everyone who has a material body receives sense gratification. Don’t think that the hogs eating stool are unhappy. No, they are getting fat in that way. They are very happy.

Another example is the camel. The camel is very fond of thorny twigs. Why? Because when he eats thorny twigs, the twigs cut his tongue, blood oozes out, and he tastes his own blood. Then he thinks, “I am enjoying.” This is sense gratification. Sex life is also like that. We taste our own blood, and we think we are enjoying. This is our foolishness.

Suddenly the boil of my polluted heart burst. The very next day I sought out a trendy bookshop in Greenwich Village, determined to find more books by this Indian swami whose name I could not pronounce. Then, afraid that the exotic-looking devotees would allure me to join them, I tactfully ordered my next books from BBT mailorder—rather than risk another monumental encounter with *saṅkīrtana* devotees on the street. For six months straight I read your books four hours a day—before even once going to a temple or preaching center.

Certainly your books saved me from a hole in hell—both now and after death. You told us, “My books are better than me.” When will I be able to appreciate fully just one drop of your *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*? What more could an aging disciple want from you than that? You always said that even in our whole life, we don’t have enough time to fully penetrate the depths of your books. Crying at the feet of your lotus presentation of Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī’s classic, and always desiring your mercy, I, insignificant Devāmṛta Swami, seek to eternally follow your footsteps.

*Trying to be your unconditional servant,*  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I am writing to you aboard a train bound for the next city on my preaching tour. The ride is long, and thoughts of the past year keep coming to mind. It has been an intense year, the most intense of my devotional life. One year ago my closest Godbrother—and your dear disciple—Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami Mahārāja left this world, and this year I fell seriously ill. The pain of these trials, along with the weight of my duties as GBC chairman, have left me pondering my debt to you and to your mission.

Were I to leave this world today, I would leave having failed you in many ways. Seven immediately come to mind. The first is not chanting the holy name without offense; the second, not taking enough time to study and teach your books; the third, not seeking the association and guidance of my advanced Godbrothers; the fourth, not taking every opportunity to preach to the devotees and those not yet devotees; the fifth, not caring for the devotees as much as I could; the sixth, not developing projects that were within my reach; and the seventh, not doing everything I could to improve ISKCON locally and globally.

I feel ashamed to even stand before you, but maybe this admission of failure will be the first step toward success. In fact, you once told us how to deal with failure, in a *Bhagavad-gītā* lecture:

But Kṛṣṇa says that this failure is not, I mean to say, unsuccessful. As we have sometimes the proverb, that “Failure is the pillar of success,” so especially in the spiritual life, this failure is not discouraging. This failure is not discouraging.

And in *Śaraṇāgati,* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura teaches us the sixfold process of surrender. It begins with admitting our shortcomings and repenting them and ends with complete surrender to the Lord.

So with head bowed and palms joined, I come before you on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā to admit my failings, to repent, and to beg you for the strength to become a surrendered disciple at your lotus feet. Only then will I leave the world as a success.

Time will bear witness to the truth of my petition, submitted to you this day.

*Your servant,*  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

**Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Love**

My dear Devotees,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, our eternal lord and master, and our dearmost friend.

Before I begin my offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would like to say to you something about his love for us: Out of his love for us, he engaged us in devotional service, and he encouraged us to continue under all circumstances, because he wanted us to realize the ultimate happiness, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Out of love for his eternal lords and masters, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he wanted to expand the number of devotees, to increase his masters’ pleasure with the expansion of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. To that Śrīla Prabhupāda and his divine love this offering is humbly dedicated.

Thank you. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your servant,  
Girirāj Swami

\*\*\*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On your auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day, we meditate on your transcendental qualities and accomplishments, as we should every day. And today, as I do on other days, I focus on what to me is your most special quality, if not your very essence: your sublime love.

I think of the first time Bharadrāja Prabhu met you in Boston, in 1969. Although he had joined the temple in Montreal, he never actually met you there. And so he came to Boston, around the same time I too first met you.

He entered the room where you were meeting with some devotees, and he experienced...“love—just love,” as he later told me. And he remarked that he had the realization then that Kṛṣṇa consciousness was simply “love masquerading as a philosophy.” And I had the same realization: “Yes, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is just love, just love.”

Specifically, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is your love, Śrīla Prabhupāda, or a manifestation of your love—for Kṛṣṇa, for your devotees, for all living beings. Because you “loved Vāsudeva,” so you said, you came to America at the age of seventy to introduce Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And the philosophy, the *harināma-saṅkīrtana*, the *prasādam*—all the items that you introduced and that ISKCON even now offers—are just sort of hooks to catch people and bring them to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Of course, in the absolute sense the practices of Kṛṣṇa consciousness are also Kṛṣṇa, who is pure love Himself. But they are also lures to attract people. And after they are attracted and actually enter into Kṛṣṇa consciousness, it is just love. It is nothing but love.

And you are the personification of that love.

Your very presence exuded pure love. Your voice resonated with love. Your glance was laden with love. Just sitting in your presence, we could feel your love. The first time I went to Vṛndāvana, to meet Gurudās Prabhu, I actually felt something different, as if Kṛṣṇa and His cowherd friends and cows were still there. Later, in Bombay, after you asked me about my visit, you yourself articulated for me what I had felt there: “When we are in Vṛndāvana we don’t see Kṛṣṇa now, but we can feel His presence.” In the same way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we could feel your love; we would sense your love in every molecule of your being, and all around you.

And that love was manifest in the way you dealt with individuals, and in the way you endeavored to offer Kṛṣṇa consciousness to all souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, how you traveled by steamer to the United States, suffering two heart attacks on the way; how you survived in New York City, on the Bowery and on the Lower East Side; how you preached to the hippies and engaged them in Kṛṣṇa’s service; how you circled the globe fourteen times and established more than 108 temples, making and maintaining thousands of devotees; how you introduced great festivals like Ratha-yātrā; how you established Kṛṣṇa conscious farms, schools, and restaurants; and how you labored through the night to translate and present the most confidential knowledge of the *Vedas* in more than sixty volumes, in language that ordinary people could understand and scholars would praise, inspiring your disciples to rapidly publish and distribute unprecedented millions of them, not only in English but translated into dozens of languages—in short, how you spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement worldwide in pursuance of the order of your Guru Mahārāja, fulfilling the desires and predictions of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Himself—all this was been well documented and is widely known.

But how you engaged and encouraged your disciples so personally, with compassion and care, at times with tears—how you loved them and inspired them to love you—such affairs of the heart, and such acts of individual kindness and grace, are less well known and published. And it is the duty of those who knew you, and experienced such exchanges, to document and share their knowledge, both for their own purification and for the benefit of others. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how you so loved your disciples individually, and engaged them ultimately for the spread of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, will be our focus here—though what we offer are but a few droplets from the vast ocean of your love, now gratefully offered back into it.

When I first came to the Boston temple, after a lecture a gentleman challenged you: “Are you happy?” In the course of your answer, you said, “Before, when I was married, I had five children, and there were always so many problems. But now, in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I have five hundred children, and I love them, and they love me, and there are no problems.” The beauty of your answer struck me, and I felt the truth of your words right there, in your relationships with your students, your spiritual children.

Still, as the movement expanded, there were problems—not personal problems, of course, but difficulties in the line of Kṛṣṇa’s service—if we can call them “problems.”

Here I remember how one of your disciples came from Hyderabad to meet you at Juhu. He was disturbed by the conflicts he was having with the other leaders there, and he sat before you in your room in Juhu, expressing his grief and presenting the various points of contention. You listened, with full sympathy and empathy, until late into the night. And you explained, ever so carefully and patiently, the philosophical principles that governed the various issues of contention.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although you were in full sympathy with the disciple before you, I knew you would have listened with equal sympathy to any of the leaders from Hyderabad, had any one of them come to explain and complain, even about the disciple presently before you. From the material point of view, one could view your complete sympathy for apparently opposing parties as contradictory or even duplicitous. But actually, your support of them was a manifestation of your love for them—and of your intelligence—in loving service to your spiritual master and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and to their mission.

Here I think of one humorous example from the Ardha-kumbha-mela in Allahabad, 1971. An argument erupted between Madhudvīṣa Prabhu and Yamunā Prabhu about Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma. Madhudvīṣa was maintaining, “Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma are the same, only Kṛṣṇa is blackish and Balarāma is whitish.”

Yamunā, however, was insisting, “No, They are different, because Kṛṣṇa is the only enjoyer of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī.”

So the two were arguing, and Tamāl Krishna Goswami, then your zonal secretary for India, brought the matter before you. First he presented Madhudvīṣa’s argument: “Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma are the same, except Kṛṣṇa is blackish and Balarāma is white.”

You replied, “Yes, he is right.”

Then Tamāl continued, “But Yamunā says They are different, because Kṛṣṇa is the only enjoyer of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī.”

You replied, “Yes, she is right.”

Then Tamāl said, “But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, they are both saying different things. They both can’t be right.”

You answered, “Yes, you are right.”

Hearing this, Tamāl asked, “Then, which is right?”

And you replied, “You decide.”

Thus, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is inconceivable; it can be seen differently from different points of view. And one of Kṛṣṇa’s qualities is that all opposites, all contradictions, are reconciled in Him. And you have the same quality: the ability to reconcile all opposites—in love. Like Kṛṣṇa, you could give yourself to each and every devotee, and fully satisfy each one. As each *gopī* in the *rāsa* dance felt, “Kṛṣṇa is mine,” so each of your disciples could also feel, “Śrīla Prabhupāda is mine.” And you were. You were hers or his—but you were also the other devotee’s—and you were Kṛṣṇa’s. Such was your love. You gave yourself in love.

Your grand-disciples and other followers now also feel your love. Mahāprabhu dāsa in Bombay spoke to me of the intensity of your love—and also of the contradiction. He said, “I have never met anyone who loved as intensely as Śrīla Prabhupāda. But at the same time, he was completely detached. In the material world, if someone loves somebody, he is not detached. And again, if someone is detached, he cannot love. But Śrīla Prabhupāda loves, and he is detached. The two seem contradictory.”

Whatever the case, you always tried to encourage us.

Haṁsadūta Prabhu tells a story about how, as your secretary, he had written a letter to encourage a devotee, even though his personal feelings about the devotee were different. But when he was writing on your behalf, on your instruction, he felt he had to represent your spirit. And what was your spirit, your mood? To appreciate and encourage the devotees, even when you had to correct them. In fact, you even stated that it is the duty of the spiritual master to encourage the disciples.

And you never gave up on anyone.

Here I think of one example from Bombay. One of the first devotees to join you in New York City in 1966 had gone to Calcutta and somehow gotten involved in smoking *gañjā*. So the temple president from Calcutta phoned you in Bombay to inform you. After hearing his report, you instructed the temple president, “Tell him that if he doesn’t stop smoking *gañjā* I will reject him.”

Afterward, Tamāl Krishna Goswami asked you, “Is that true, that you will reject him?”

You replied, “No. I cannot reject anyone.”

Tamāl Krishna Goswami also asked you, “But isn’t there some limit? Don’t you have to draw the line somewhere?”

And you replied, “Lord Nityānanda’s mercy has no limit.”

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the embodiment of Lord Nityānanda’s mercy and love. In fact, mercy (*dayā*) is a function of love (*bhakti*). *Namo mahā-vadānyāya kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te*. You are the embodiment of Lord Caitanya’s love and mercy, and you told all of us who represent you, who represent Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, that we too must show mercy. And although we can never equal you (we can’t even equal a fraction of a speck of dust on your lotus feet), still, whatever we are—tiny, insignificant living entities who had the good fortune to meet you, in person or through your instructions—we have the immense responsibility now to try to reflect your qualities. And I believe the one quality that embodies all your other qualities is your love for all of us, out of your mercy.

Your love for us inspired in us such love for you that we would go to any extent to serve you and please you, to practice and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But you also taught us that to love you meant to love and serve your other servants as well, for our love for you would be shown in our relations with each other—in service to you and your mission. And finally, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you made it clear that, if we truly loved you, we would follow in your footsteps by loving and serving all Kṛṣṇa’s creatures, doing our utmost to engage them, too, in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

I think of myself now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and I appeal to all your followers: “Please give me your mercy! Please grant me your association! Although I can never properly reciprocate with you, at least I will do what I can to please Śrīla Prabhupāda—and to please all of you, in his service.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as we serve your instructions and receive your mercy, we also develop pure love like yours—for Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga, and ultimately for Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa—and Their dear devotees.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for your causeless, limitless mercy—your pure, sublime love! May we realize your true essence and mercy by hearing and chanting always, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare, and preaching and glorifying Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga! *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your eternal servant,*  
Girirāj Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine instructions, which are continuing to guide the fallen souls of Kali-yuga on the path back to Godhead.

You appeared in order to help realize Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the holy name would spread to every town and village. Being a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, you cannot be separated from the Supreme Lord. Thoughts of Your Divine Grace inspire us to think of the Supreme Lord. Similarly, the more we try to serve the Supreme Lord, the more we think of you as being the one who introduced us to the process of devotional service. Every year your appearance day reminds us of the need to take full shelter of your divine instructions.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you do not need our glorification or devotional service. You are *ātmārāma—*self-sufficient. However, by your causeless mercy we get the opportunity to attempt to glorify your exalted personality and to engage in devotional service. Due to your purity the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is equipped with spiritual strength. Anyone who comes in contact with your movement experiences peace and happiness. Last Saturday we had a very ecstatic Ratha-yātrā in Nairobi. Thousands of Africans, including young children from our school in Kisumu, sang and danced ecstatically during the parade. In Seeta, Uganda, our devotees are running a school for young Ugandan children. It is amazing how in a short time these children have learned the process of *kīrtana* and become blissful. With a shaven head, *śikhā,* and *tilaka,* along with *dhotī* and *kurtā*, these African children look like Vaikuṇṭha children. After participating in the Ratha-yātrā, the mayor of Nairobi remarked that he had never seen such blissful children anywhere in Kenya. Similarly, all over the world we have seen that even those who had previously had no exposure to Kṛṣṇa consciousness dance and chant in ecstasy and become peaceful after taking to this process.

Your glories are spreading unlimitedly with the passage of time. Even in countries that do not officially recognize religion, your books are being widely read, and the Kṛṣṇa consciousness penetration is increasing. The transcendental books you translated into English have now been translated into almost every major language of the world. Their distribution is bringing newer and newer results. People of all backgrounds are benefiting from Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement, demonstrating that your teachings represent the real peace formula for today’s troubled society.

Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī advised that we should always remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him. This is the sum and substance of the Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you bring about this remembrance of Kṛṣṇa because you are always thinking of Kṛṣṇa. You are the captain of the ship that can carry millions across the ocean of material existence. Your instructions are the source of spiritual strength. Once, in India, a guest asked you if you had performed any miracles. You said no. But you went on to add that you had performed one miracle. The man was curious as to what the nature of that miracle was, and you responded by saying that you have turned thousands of *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas*. Your movement will continue to turn *mlecchas* into *brāhmaṇas* for the next ten thousand years. You are expert at engaging everyone in Kṛṣṇa’s service and also at teaching others to do the same. You will continue to be the pre-eminent *śikṣā-guru* for the rest of this *yuga*. In your association the illusion of *māyā* is transformed into practical loving service to the Supreme Lord. You single-handedly started a world-wide spiritual revolution that will continue to transform the lives of millions for generations.

In a lecture in Detroit you advised that our only prayer to Kṛṣṇa should be “Dear Lord, please give the strength to serve You.” No other prayer will make one happy. Therefore, on this auspicious day, I want to beg you to please bless me to have the strength and determination to go on serving the Supreme Personality of Godhead. May I always have the opportunity to stay in the association of your followers and help expand the glories of your ISKCON movement.

*Your servant,*  
Gopāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,
