Please accept my most humble and prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, the ocean of Vaiṣṇava qualities!

As I mentioned last year, I would like to continue my meditation on how you personify all the twenty-six qualities of a Vaiṣṇava to the highest degree. I pray for your mercy in describing you, since I have no power to do so.

The next quality Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja attributes to pure devotees is *satya-sāra,* being thoroughly true. When you first came to New York City, one of your hosts suggested that you would have to adapt to the Western environment. But you retorted that you had come to change the Westerners, not to be changed by them. No one has mastered the art of innovation, while at the same time conserving the essence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, more than you; old wine in a new bottle. Many have innovated, but the essence has been diluted. Others conserve, but often with no tangible progress in spreading the message of Lord Caitanya. As Sanātana Gosvāmī said to Haridāsa Ṭhākura:

*‘ācāra’, ‘pracāra’,—nāmera karaha ‘dui’ kārya*  
*tumi—sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya*

**“You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Antya*** **4.103)**

The quality of *sama,* being equal to all, applies both to your self-composure and to how you dealt with others. Your equanimity manifested so many times when the devotees would be disturbed by the onslaught of adversity. In Juhu, when even the devotees’ safety was endangered, you remained the transcendental general giving perfect indications. When the Naxalites threatened your pandal program, you brought them under your spiritual sway by *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. And when you were glorified, you remained the most humble servant of Kṛṣṇa.

Even after becoming the most prominent Vaiṣṇava preacher in the world, you still took time to advise ordinary devotees to take care of their physical and spiritual health. You were always concerned that each devotee, and anyone who came to us, got sufficient *prasādam*. You made everyone feel important, notwithstanding their particular service.

There are unlimited examples showing how you possess all these qualities to an immeasurable degree. I am just pointing out a few to remind myself of the perpetual debt that remains before me. I pray to always remember your boundless glories by the mercy of your unalloyed servants, your immaculate teachings through example, and your spiritual vision constantly unfolding before us. Even more, I humbly request the strength to follow your teachings and example.

*Your pleading servant,*  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. In your famous poem of 1935, glorifying your own spiritual master, you wrote about him:

The oldest of all,  
but in new dress;  
miracle done,  
Your Divine Grace,

This is the miracle of your peerless preaching: that you have come as the empowered representative of the oldest of all teachers, Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself, who first imparted Vedic knowledge to Brahmā, yet you have dressed this primeval message in a language, style, and method that are appropriate to the contemporary world. You have revealed the adaptive power of eternal Vedic culture, that it is truly a universal spiritual science and not an ancient ethnic or sectarian tradition.

Now I pray to you that you bless us with this adaptive power, intrinsic to Vedic culture, which you manifested in such brilliant fashion. Because you are the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, we who follow you must discover adaptations that both conform to the basic standards you set and remain within the boundaries you drew.

In the famous Vyāsa-pūjā essay you wrote to your spiritual master, you cited the Vedic phrase *phalena paricīyate,* “\[a thing\] is measured by its fruit.” During your mission on earth, you often and ardently expressed your desire for a particular fruit: the wide distribution of love of Godhead, the broad expansion of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. You clearly envisioned ISKCON as a movement that could and should provide mature spiritual leadership to the world. You specified that by achieving prominence in America, ISKCON would achieve great influence throughout the world. The challenge then before us is to bring you that fruit which you desired out of your own pure love for Kṛṣṇa.

If we water down your movement to popularize it, or if we lack the mature realization to make it relevant—in either case—we will not fulfill your vision. Therefore I humbly pray to you on this day that you bless us with the power to preserve intact and uncompromised your divine mission, while at the same time keeping it relevant, appealing, and successful.

*Your servant,*  
Hridayānanda dās Goswami

## Jayapatāka Swami

My dear spiritual father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this sacred occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, we remember your transcendental qualities and feel your presence in our midst and in our heart. You expressed how in this Kali-yuga working together amongst devotees, and especially those in leadership roles, is something very special. You said that to do that we need to listen to the voice of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in our heart and ignore and neglect the voice of Māyā in our mind. This is a difficult task, but it is possible by your causeless mercy. Please bless us that we can perform this task successfully for your pleasure.

I remember Your Divine Grace drinking Māyāpur water from your gold goblet raised high in the air and declaring: “Māyāpur!!” “Ahhh! Māyāpur!!” “ Māyāpur is the spiritual world!” “Living or dying in Māyāpur is the same!” “When we live in Māyāpur we are in the spiritual world! When we die in Māyāpur we return to the spiritual world!” Your Divine Grace appreciated Māyāpur *dhāma* so much. When will we have even a fraction of the love and appreciation Your Divine Grace had for Śrīdhām Māyāpur?

We are trying to ready the Pañca-tattva life-size Deities for Their installation during the 2004 Gaura Pūrṇimā Festival. This installation will help fulfill Your Divine Grace’s instructions. By your causeless mercy, we hope that thereafter we can soon move the Deities to the main temple—The Temple of the Vedic Planetarium. Once Their Lordships have descended into this world by Their and your causeless mercy, we hope that the temple will soon manifest.

When we would report yearly book distribution figures to Your Divine Grace, you would instruct us to double our distribution the next year. Now that the Congregational Development programs have spread around the world, the devotees are busy harvesting the dormant devotees who are waiting to be discovered in various places. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we need your mercy so that we can also at least double the number of new devotees made through Congregational Development every year! In this way your divine family will multiply exponentially!

Your Divine Grace has more then a million followers. It is wonderful to feel united with all these followers of yours. Your Divine Grace would care lovingly for all those devotees who came to you. May we also be empowered to be able to care adequately for all the devotees who come to us for help, care, and shelter. Only by Your Divine Grace’s mercy can these conditioned souls get the unlimited divine mercy of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Your Divine Grace’s books are the basis, yet we often don’t read your books as much as we need to, due to an extreme workload. We need your mercy so that we can fully imbibe all the teachings of your books and be empowered to transmit the nectar of your teachings to the fallen souls. May we all enjoy a natural taste of nectar from reading your transcendental literature and be able to transmit that nectar to others.

Your Divine Grace has instructed that “Preaching is the essence,” and the nectar of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness is inconceivable! Please let us always bathe in the unlimited ocean of Kṛṣṇa conscious preaching. Today your grand-daughters are also preaching dynamically and expanding the congregation alongside the male preachers. In this way more persons are tasting the nectar.

Your Divine Grace had very high expectations for us. Please bless us so that we may live up to the highest expectations you had of us and never disgrace your good name.

Thanking you again and again for the causeless mercy of Your Divine Grace’s devotional service and association,

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Jayapatāka Swami

## Kavicandra Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him. When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you taught us that Cāṇakya Paṇḍita advised that some things should be immediately eliminated—that is, fire, debt, and disease. But we have also heard that the debt to Śrī Guru can never be repaid. I pray that I will never fall into the illusion that I have repaid or can ever repay my debt to you. You have given Kṛṣṇa, and He is unlimited. But please allow me to try to repay the debt. The problem is that for whatever little service I try to do, you give more.

There is no way to know all that you have given, since Kṛṣṇa is constantly revealing Himself more and more. I can observe some of the invaluable things, but others are too sublime for my perception. Your love is showered in so many ways—the Holy Name (*japa* and *kīrtanas*). I am in Māyāpur and have been hearing the twenty-four-hour *kīrtana,* which is surely descending form Goloka. *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta, The Nectar of Devotion,* and so many other books. Temples, Deities, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam, sādhu-sāṅga,* Śrīdhām Māyāpur, Vṛndāvana-dhāma...

Ordinary fires should be extinguished, but there is one fire that I pray will start and be ever-increasing. That is described by Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself;

*yathāgninā hema malaṁ jahāti*  
*dhmātaṁ punaḥ svaṁ bhajate ca rūpam*  
*ātmā ca karmānuśayaṁ vidhūya*  
*mad-bhakti-yogena bhajaty atho mām*

**“Just as gold, when smelted in fire, gives up its impurities and returns to its pure brilliant state, similarly, the spirit soul, absorbed in the fire of** ***bhakti-yoga,*** **is purified of all contamination caused by previous fruitive activities and returns to its original position of serving Me in the spiritual world.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.14.25)**

I suffer from the worst disease of attachment for sense gratification

*indriyair viṣayākṛṣṭair ākṣiptaṁ dhyāyatāṁ manaḥ*  
*cetanāṁ harate buddheḥ stambas toyam iva hradāt*

**“When one’s mind and senses are attracted to sense objects for enjoyment, the mind becomes agitated. As a result of continually thinking of sense objects, one’s real consciousness almost becomes lost, like the water in a lake that is gradually sucked up by the big grass straws on its bank.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **4.22.30)**

You have given everything that is needed to cure this worst disease. I pray that I can take that medicine with faith and, by the mercy of your sincere followers, let the fire of love Godhead burn up the disease.

You kindly give us so many challenges, which often seem insurmountable, but if we take shelter of your instructions, even a blind man can see stars in the sky. I know that I can never begin to repay you for what you are freely giving, but please let me try.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, manifesting in your transcendental appearance!

So inscrutably vast is the difference between a liberated soul and a conditioned one!

The infinite sky of pure consciousness is your place of residence. There you relish the association of the nectarean forms of the Personality of Servitor Godhead: Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s eternal associates, all blissful and infinite in number. Their ever-manifest selfless loving attitude toward the Divine Couple is your treasured wealth, a wealth that you invest, increasing it unlimitedly with your expert intimate service.

To us the most incredible aspect of your service to Kṛṣṇa and His associates is your coming to this ever-decaying coercive dungeon of illusion, the material world, carrying the immortal message of love of Godhead for the upliftment of the multitude of conditioned souls.

Visiting the residence of those who taste the rotten forms of selfishness and deceit, endlessly running after hallucinatory objects of desire, you sat with us, walked with us, ate with us, cooked for us, spoke with us, smiled at us, accepted our anguished service, and loved us—all the while remaining untouched by our madness: a liberated soul of the highest caliber.

The paltriness of the scientists’ proclamations, of the humanitarians’ appeals, of the politicians’ promises, and of the intellectuals’ pride did not affect you in the least, and by your association even we could reflect your irrevocable realizations.

Let your association never die within our hearts, never be obscured, corrupted, or diminished, for it is the metamorphic agent that will take us from the conditioned stage to the liberated one.

The brilliant tears of ecstasy flowing from the radiant lotus face of Lord Caitanya fall on the ground of His unalloyed devotees’ hearts and then come out of their eyes like rivulets of kindness and compassion for the hopeless slaves of vacuity.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am one of these slaves, I don’t have any merit. All I do is cry out of self-pity, and my attempts to serve you are punctually frustrated.

I can’t give up hope, though. So many times I saw your eyes anointed with that sacred compassion, dispersing glances that acted like a miraculous balm for a leper like me.

You are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, the receptacle of the desires of the previous *ācāryas,* the most loving, intimate servant of Lord Caitanya, and my spiritual master: *tomāra śakati āche*.

You can take me (force me) back to Godhead.

I take shelter at your lotus feet.  
*Your unworthy servant,*  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to me; please let me serve you eternally. You are my master, I am your slave; please let me serve you day after day after day after day. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I recall an intimate moment when you increased the mystique of your being here among us foolish “boys and girls.” Nonchalantly recalling how as a child you “always got your way,” you proceeded to tell a little vignette about your desire for a cowboy pistol. Finally, after much insistence, your father complied and bought you a toy gun. But you were not to be satisfied until you had two guns, one for each hand.

“Oh,” said Harṣarāṇī, “you were a cowboy!” With complete gravity, you replied, “Yes.” At that second, no one was thinking of you and the Wild West. We just knew you were speaking about being with Kṛṣṇa and the cows in Vṛndāvana. We were only spiritual toddlers at best (it was ’67 or ’68), but you mercifully gave us a glimpse into your heart. I felt very small being there with you at that moment.

After that incident, the same devotee penned a short poem about you. In it she described you as playing leapfrog in a pasture with Kṛṣṇa and the cows and other *gopas*. It seemed funny and frivolous to us, yet you gravely remarked, “She has become advanced,” and ordered that it be printed in *Back to Godhead* magazine. As the ocean can only be described as very, very deep, you are also very, very deep. Sitting there with you at that moment, I felt very, very shallow.

My prayer is to remain like this, always feeling small before you in your eternal loving presence, always engaging in your causeless devotional service, birth after birth.

May you continue to accept me, this fallen soul, your servant and loving daughter,  
Mālatī-devī dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

**Substance and Form**

I make my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace, A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter of His Lotus Feet. Obeisances unto you, O servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Śrī Caitanyadeva and are delivering the Western countries, which are filled with voidism and impersonalism.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Knowing you are ISKCON is not enough. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, also had a quality that transcended institutional ISKCON: in a word, love—in the truest and deepest sense of the term.

All of your devotees, with few exceptions, felt you that cared about them, that you protected them and were their well-wisher. This was true for literally thousands—something almost unprecedented in the entire material manifestation.

Note to devotees: Prabhupāda *is* ISKCON and ISKCON is Prabhupāda, but understanding *how* ISKCON is Prabhupāda is more important than just the outer meaning of “ISKCON is Prabhupāda” and vice versa.

What is *meant* by “ISKCON is Prabhupāda?” You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, created what you often referred to as an “institution” to perpetuate your “style” of *bhakti*. But calling it “your style” is not altogether accurate either, because the *bhakti* that you brought wasn’t something new or unique, and you made that emphatic. What you brought was the same time-tested *bhakti* of great sages of yore, especially that of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, that of the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, Viśvanātha Cakravartī, and others in the disciplic succession.

Although you made it clear that we had to “work” for Kṛṣṇa and “do” devotional service, you also explained and showed by your personal life how we should deal with all kinds of internal and external struggles. We must discuss them threadbare with intimate devotees (*guhyam ākhyāti pṛcchati*).

You were the personification of substance over form. You stressed personal initiative and freedom to love and choose, as essentials for making progress on the transcendental path.

What does “substance over form” mean? For now, let’s call “substance” chanting the *mahā-mantra,* doing what we are best at for Kṛṣṇa, doing what we like to do best for Kṛṣṇa, associating with those we like most and whose *bhakti* we admire the most, surrendering, and serving Kṛṣṇa together.

In a now-famous letter to Karandhara, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wrote:

…the regulative principles must be followed by everyone. Otherwise their enthusiasm dwindles and they again think of sex and become restless, and so many problems are there. There is some symptom of missing the point. The point is to be engaged in doing something for Kṛṣṇa, never mind what is that job, but being so engaged in doing something very much satisfying to the devotee that he remains always enthusiastic. He will automatically follow the regulative principles because they are part of his occupational duty—by applying them practically as his occupational duty, he realizes the happy result of regulative principles. So the future of this Krishna Consciousness movement is very bright, so long the managers remain vigilant that 16 rounds are being chanted by everyone without fail, that they are all rising before four morning, attending mangal arati—our leaders shall be careful not to kill the spirit of enthusiastic service, which is individual and spontaneous and voluntary. They should try always to generate some atmosphere of fresh challenge to the devotees, so that they will agree enthusiastically to rise and meet it. That is the art of management: to draw out spontaneous loving spirit of sacrificing some energy for Kṛṣṇa. But where are so many expert managers? All of us should become expert managers and preachers. We should not be very much after comforts and become complacent or self-contented. There must be always some tapasya, strictly observing the regulative principles—Krishna Consciousness movement must be always a challenge, a great achievement to be gained by voluntary desire to do it, and that will keep it healthy. So you big managers now try to train up more and more some competent preachers and managers like yourselves. Forget this centralizing and bureaucracy.

This letter contains the essence of your secret of management: always provide a fresh challenge, forget bureaucracy, be austere, rise early, and strictly follow initiation vows. But the most important part of this message was for your ISKCON leaders to “draw out spontaneous loving spirit of sacrificing some energy for Kṛṣṇa.”

Often you reminded us that without love and trust there would be no ISKCON, and that despite the expert way in which you set up ISKCON, we could “ruin everything.” (letter to Bali-mardan dāsa and Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa dāsa, 18 September 1972, and letter to Tamāl Krishna Goswami, 11 January 1974)

Maybe it’s a usual occurrence with institutional growth, but presently ISKCON seems unintentionally more inclined to maintain its form and structure at the expense of its substance. Developing love for Kṛṣṇa and nurturing loving relationships with ISKCON colleagues may be taking a back seat. Some leaders are succumbing to what Pūrṇacandra Prabhu would call “authoritarianism.”

Although top leaders are usually to blame and should be exemplary, all of us need to realize where the problem lies. It’s not always the fault of “leaders,’” many of whom *are* exemplary. It’s the mistake of everyone, as all are meant to be leaders of sorts (*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa/āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*).

This offering is merely an attempt at a state-of-the-movement observation and a gentle reminder to myself and other ISKCONites. It’s a reminder that we want to offer a perfect ISKCON to you, its founder-*ācārya;* that we need to recognize our problems and take steps to correct them if we truly want to make ISKCON everything you expected it to be.

I guess it’s ironic that I feel a need to repeat something I penned in the driver’s seat of 1957 Ford Mustang in 1977. I was cold and could barely see what I wrote as the sun set on Venice Beach, Los Angeles, where I was parked on the asphalt lot. It was shortly after you left us. The fading hour was upon me. It was the deadline day for my first BBT assignment. After twenty-five years, these words still describe my relationship with you:

*To me he was a master, a father, and my dearmost friend.*  
Mukunda Goswami

## Niranjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this anniversary of your divine appearance in this world, I would like to express my gratitude for everything that you are doing for me. The mercy you are pouring upon this unworthy soul is immeasurable. I cannot imagine where I would be had you not reached out to save me from Māyā’s clutches. Wherever it might have been, it would have been hell, that place where there is no association of Kṛṣṇa’s devotees. I would have been totally lost, floundering somewhere in this universe, repeatedly tossed by the powerful waves of Kṛṣṇa’s illusory energy. Having not heard your divine message, no doubt I would have also accumulated an additional burden of sinful reactions, sufficient to keep me entrapped for several million more births than I’m already due.

When I look where I am now, more than thirty years from the time of our first meeting (when you appeared before me in your purports to the *Bhagavad-gītā*), I realize that although I’m still in this material world, by your divine grace I know that I do not belong here. This world of duality and illusion is not my home. You revealed that my real home is in Goloka Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa’s eternal abode, and that I am meant to be with Kṛṣṇa, serving Him in spontaneous and ecstatic loving devotional service.

In your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* (11.14), I have read:

Kṛṣṇa’s devotees relate to Kṛṣṇa in various relationships; one might treat Kṛṣṇa as a son, or one might treat Kṛṣṇa as a husband, as a friend, or as a master. Kṛṣṇa and Arjuna are related in friendship. As the father tolerates, or the husband or a master tolerates, so Kṛṣṇa tolerates.

And in a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture (1.5.2) in Los Angeles, 1968, you once said:

“\[T\]his world, the manifested world, is false.” How it is false? That is not explained by Śaṅkarācārya. That is explained by Caitanya Mahāprabhu, that reality, real father...If you accept Kṛṣṇa as your father, oh, He’ll give you all protection. If you accept Kṛṣṇa as your husband, oh, there is no question of divorce. If you accept Kṛṣṇa as your friend, oh, Kṛṣṇa will give you everything.

So, so many relationships. All these relationships in the relative world, they are all shadow or temporary. But the same thing is there in the spiritual world. And if we come to that platform, then there will be happiness.

These and countless other revelations have come from your lotus mouth and entered my heart. It is only fitting that from the core of my heart I must thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I am eternally indebted to you for all your divine revelations. Kṛṣṇa—His name, form, qualities, pastimes, and transcendental abode—all manifest in your words. Before I met you, nobody ever told me I belonged somewhere else. I was misled into thinking I could find a shelter here in the world of no shelter. But somehow, by your unlimited causeless mercy, I met you. You took my hand and began leading me on the most direct path back “home,” back to Kṛṣṇa.

I am still on this journey, and you still have my hand. But while I accompany you, you have mercifully shown me one other place I could go—a place more accessible to me here until I’ve completed my journey to Kṛṣṇa’s abode. You mercifully manifested this place to shelter me from the vicious attacks of lust, anger, greed, illusion, madness, envy, and everything else Māyā uses in her arsenal of weapons against those she holds captive. This place is another home, a safe haven here in this world, where wayward souls can find permanence and shelter. That place is none other than under the shade of your two lotus feet! Your two lotus feet are the abode of ecstatic love for Kṛṣṇa. Who would be so foolish and not take full shelter there for all of eternity?

In *Jaiva Dharma*, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura writes:

If he \[a person tormented by the troubles of material life\] intelligently understands that his sufferings are all caused by the illusory potency *māyā,* and that the entire material world is by nature inauspicious, and that his only shelter is the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord and the association of the Lord’s devotees, then he will take shelter of the feet of the devotees who have taken shelter of the feet of the Lord. Taking shelter of their feet is the most effective means to attain devotional service. That is the way one attains the feet of the Supreme Lord.

And Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura has also written:

We all should aspire to be Mathurā people. We should have a proper conception of spiritual Mathurā instead of the mundane Mathurā which is the place of enjoyment of people with very low aspirations. Our Sahajiyā brothers think that they can have access to Mathurā by paying the railway fare. It is by surrendering ourselves to the Preceptor’s Feet that we can have an abode in Mathurā. Otherwise, we cannot get an entrance into the transcendental region.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this material world, I place my head at your lotus feet and beg you to please keep me under the shade of your lotus feet for all of eternity. Please make me your fully surrendered servant and take everything I have—my mind, body, words, and my very life air—as my offering unto your divine lotus feet, the safest place in all the three worlds.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dearmost master,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Recently I read the following purport in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (4.20.15):

In the modern age of democracy there are so many government representatives voting for legislation. Every day they bring out a new law. But because these laws are only mental concoctions manufactured by inexperienced conditioned souls, they cannot give relief to human society....Under these circumstances, not only are the leaders unable to guide people toward liberation, but they cannot even give them peace of mind.

I am sure that humanity will gradually give the deserved importance to your wonderful work. Not only have your life and teachings revealed the most intimate and eternal *līlā* of the Supreme Lord, but they have also given practical instructions that will change the planet step by step. The truth and certainty of this change were foreseen in the *Vedas*.

The strong influences of the modes of passion and ignorance fill the world with suffering, and therefore we must spread your legacy to mitigate the pain with the antidote of knowledge conveyed by your words.

This process may take time, but the least we can do is leave a solid foundation for the future generations to continue your work.

The following is my humble request at your lotus feet on this very sacred anniversary of your appearance: May I be take birth after birth on this planet so I can continue assisting your mission.

*Your eternal and worthless servant,*  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Due to His unlimited compassion upon the fallen conditioned souls, Lord Kṛṣṇa sometimes comes Himself and sometimes sends His representatives in order to deliver them. He sent you here from His abode as His special ambassador, and as such you personify Kṛṣṇa’s unlimited compassion. That is why you utilized every moment to preach and exemplify Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You never missed a preaching opportunity, and even in old age you would serve and preach with such enthusiasm that none of your youthful disciples could keep up with you. This was due to Kṛṣṇa’s direct empowerment, and you were empowered by Kṛṣṇa in that way because of your surrender to Him.

It is said that of the twenty-six qualities of a devotee, one is primary and twenty-five are secondary. The primary quality is surrender to Kṛṣṇa. As a result of such surrender, Kṛṣṇa empowers the devotee so that the other twenty-five qualities become manifest in his character. Of course, the extent to which these qualities manifest depends on the degree of the devotee’s surrender. As Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā, ye yathā māṁ prapadyante tāṁs tathaiva bhajāmy aham:* “As all surrender to me, I reward them accordingly.” In your case, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can see that you were fully surrendered to Kṛṣṇa and that is why Lord Kṛṣṇa caused you to manifest all twenty-six qualities in great abundance.

As for ourselves, we have no qualification, but by your potent preaching you have convinced us to try and follow the process of devotional service. In order to engage us in steady and serious service, you have kindly created ISKCON and the BBT with their many variegated projects. In this way we can live and serve in the constant association of committed devotees and thus be constantly reminded of Lord Kṛṣṇa. At the same time, we can act as your instruments in spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement all over the world. Thus, by your arrangement, we are simultaneously being purified and are helping others to become purified. This is all a manifestation of your compassion and desire to bring all conditioned souls back to Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you so much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us this rare and priceless opportunity to assist you in your service to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga. I pray that I may continue to assist you in your mission in whatever way I am able.

*Your worthless servant,*  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Praghoṣa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I sat in the corner of the public house,  
with plastic friends and liver doused,  
looking for miracles—loaves and fishes—  
all fed up with unattainable wishes,  
reaction level remained too high—  
I really didn’t want that stimuli.

Living way beyond my entitled quota,  
all burned out with a run-down motor,  
suddenly I heard your penetrating voice,  
piercing my heart, leaving me no choice.  
Meditating on your sublime message—  
while pondering your undeserved blessing.

So now new hope flows through me  
And I know you care for all.  
No luck and no half chances,  
No need of any special circumstances .

It’s no more than common sense,  
Cos there’s no such thing as accidents.

I am thinking—how can I please you?  
I am feeling—I want to help you, too.  
I am willing—if you will allow me to  
Forever serve you, forever serve you.

Hearing your words, like thornless flowers,  
soothing for all as Māyā cowers.  
Material laughter with its callous bite  
can only respond by taking flight,  
racing away at the speed of sound,  
retreating further and further underground.

So now new hope flows through me,  
And I know you care for all.  
No luck and no half chances,  
No need of any special circumstances.

It’s no more than common sense,  
Cos there’s no such thing as accidents.

I am thinking—how can I please you?  
I am feeling—I want to help you, too.  
I am willing—if you will allow me to

Forever serve you, forever serve you.

*Your servant,*  
Praghoṣa dāsa

## Prithu dāsa Adhikārī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance. All glories unto you!

Back in 1965 when crossing the Arabian Sea on your way to America, you suffered a massive heart attack. When there was some concern that you might not survive, Lord Kṛṣṇa appeared in your dream. The Lord was in an open boat along with His incarnations. He was smiling as He rowed the boat, which was pulling your ship. Indeed, just as the Matsya *avatāra* pulled King Satyavrata’s ship out of trouble during the inundation of the three worlds, in your dream the Lord pulled your ship all the way to America.

Such are the pastimes of a blessed soul.

While awake you tirelessly preached, tirelessly wrote, tirelessly instructed and supervised your disciples, and even in your dreams you were absorbed in Kṛṣṇa, who came Himself to help you accomplish His divine mission.

Please be merciful to this great fool.

Kindly sprinkle some drops of your mercy on my head, so that I can follow in your most amazing footsteps.

*Your servant,*  
Pṛthu dāsa

## Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this auspicious anniversary of your appearance, I would like to attempt to remember some of your glories for the sake of my own purification.

Your complete dependence on the instructions of your spiritual master and on the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa are your most wonderful qualities. With determination and dedication you traveled around the world to spread Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission. He could have easily spread the holy name to every town and village Himself, but He chose to give you the opportunity of doing so.

You have performed a historically unparalleled feat by spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. With your intelligence you figured out how to turn forty rupees into a worldwide movement of devotees. People all over the world are inspired by reading your books. These people come from all walks of life. They are professionals, students, housewives, children—all sorts of people who, ultimately by your efforts, are becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious. You have established many oases of devotion to Kṛṣṇa where the soul can sing Lord Kṛṣṇa’s praises and cherish the shelter of His love. In this fragile, lonely material world, your presence continues to change lives forever.

It is only by your causeless mercy, which is coming through your disciples to us, that your grand disciples like me have the opportunity of being a part of this glorious movement. Kindly accept my humble attempts in trying to help them in their mission of serving you.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for coming into my life, and please help me continue to keep you there.

*Your servant,*  
Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa dāsa

## Rādhānātha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear worshipable lord and master,

Twenty-five years have passed since that historic night in Vṛndāvana. It appeared that Kṛṣṇa had taken you away from us. Our broken hearts suffered the inconceivable. Afflicted by such anguish and despair, *why* did we survive? Only darkness and confusion permeated the four directions. The world appeared as a dreary void. Helplessly lost in such sorrow, we could only cry. How fortunate was Jayānanda Prabhu! He did not have to endure this closing chapter of your earthly *līlā*.

We were inexperienced, helpless children, yet Lord Kṛṣṇa took you from us. Why *then*? This unfathomable mystery was our grieving reality. We had heard from you about love in separation. It sounded so sweet, not like this. Your departure from Vṛndāvana shattered our lives. The Sāṁvartaka cloud of separation inundated us with a torrential rain of sorrow and confusion. Where to turn amidst such perplexity? Our devotional lives were centered on the hope of your next visit. “Śrīla Prabhupāda is coming!” These words sustained our aspirations. They motivated unconditional surrender from our reluctant hearts. Will we never hear those cherished words again?

My mind was being haunted by the ghost of regret—regret for all those opportunities when I could have come to see you, hear from you, and offer my love to you, but I didn’t. Will those lost opportunities ever come again? In the shadow of your disappearance, we realized how pitiful we were for taking your association for granted. On that fateful Vṛndāvana night, Kṛṣṇa revealed our desperate need for your mercy. From a depth of the heart which was unknown to us sprang an all-consuming feeling of love and gratitude for you. This became our only reality. All else became painfully irrelevant.

In this desperate state, our only hope for spiritual survival was to seek shelter of your words and example. Your illustrious life personified loving service in separation. In special moments we heard you weep in separation from your beloved Guru Mahārāja. Yet you would tell us that there was never a moment when you were separated from him. You taught us that the personal relationship between guru and disciple exists in the realm of divinity. It is not bound by the limitations of time and space. Love is expressed through selfless service. Our love will be nourished by our faithful commitment to please you. To please you is simple. We must wholeheartedly accept what you have given us and share it with others.

With the desire to please you, I have been meditating on four particular instructions you have given us: to sincerely worship your books; to honor and serve the devotees; to faithfully serve your dream for ISKCON; and to sincerely chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

It is in your books that we find the treasure of your unlimited compassion. You lovingly speak to each of us with every word. Please allow us to understand the sacrifice of love you invested in your sacred literatures. Let us gratefully reciprocate by reading them again and again for your pleasure. Please protect us from the misfortune of ever minimizing their perfect and complete spiritual value. Let us share this treasure with the world.

If we wish to please you, we must also show honor and respect to those you love. In your last days, you instructed us, “You can show your love for me by how you cooperate with one another.” Once in New Vrindāban you were asked, “Will your palace be lit with jewels?” With a beaming smile decorating your compassionate face, you replied, “My devotees are my jewels; they will illuminate the whole world.” Thus you yourself told us how important it is that we respect, honor, and serve the devotees. You told us that to bring a devotee to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa, you shed “buckets of blood.” Both publicly and privately, you proclaimed your indebtedness to each devotee who helped you in the mission of your Guru Mahārāja. Through gracious tears, embraces, smiles, and words of affection, you expressed your gratitude. This was the all-attractive nature of your humility, forgiveness, and love. We pray to honor, encourage, and serve those you love.

In order to facilitate love between the devotees, you created the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. You declared ISKCON to be your body. To nourish and sustain this glorious branch of Lord Caitanya’s tree, you sacrificed your life. ISKCON is your offering of pure love to our blessed *guru-paramparā*. Still, serious mistakes have been made and many devotees have suffered. In a young spiritual movement, painful lessons must be learned. I pray at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you empower us to give our lives, as an offering of gratitude, to help make ISKCON the glorious movement you dreamed it would be. However small we may be and however impossible it may appear to fulfill your dream, your mercy can empower us just as Garuḍa empowered the insignificant sparrow to move the ocean. If we sincerely endeavor to serve your mission, you can empower each of us to perform great miracles. Please grant us the strength to shed our negativity and seek positive opportunities for strengthening and uniting ISKCON, for your pleasure. Pleasing you is our life and soul. In pleasing you, the sweetest *rasas* of Vṛndāvana *dhāma* can be realized.

You have told us that our spiritual advancement depends on the sincerity with which we chant the holy names of Kṛṣṇa. In the early days of our movement, you fell seriously ill. The devotees were beside themselves with grief upon hearing that you would be returning to Vṛndāvana. At the time of this crucial event, you imparted an instruction which we pray to never forget. With the authority of all the great *ācāryas* and scriptures, you spoke a simple but profound truth: “If you are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and I am chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, we will always be together.” We vowed before you to sincerely chant the holy names of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. I pray, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you may be pleased with our sincere efforts to chant the holy names with humility, tolerance, and great determination.

On that Kārttika night in Vṛndāvana, you left us with a great challenge. Our actual love and gratitude were being tested. How seriously did we hear your words? How would we show our gratitude? Sincere love is both tested and nourished through service in separation. In such service, we find your Divine Grace intimately appearing in the innermost chambers of the heart. In being instruments of your compassion, we can live always in the shade of your lotus feet. But due to our conditioning and weaknesses, we have failed you again and again. Still, our eternal hope is in your causeless mercy. You are our ever well-wisher—always waiting to forgive us, inspire us, and bring us into your eternal association in the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara.

*Your servant,*  
Rādhānātha Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, in 1968 the devotees wrote, “Our master has not finished his work.” On occasion in subsequent years, you were ill and we prayed for you to get better. Kṛṣṇa, being very kind and merciful to His dear devotee, fulfilled our desire. In November 1977 you were again ill, but most of us thought that you would soon recover.

However, Kṛṣṇa had a different plan, to take you back home, back to Godhead. I remember thinking, “How can this be? Śrīla Prabhupāda can’t leave us now! We’re just neophytes! And what about all the books Śrīla Prabhupāda is translating, especially the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*? He hasn’t finished yet!” But the will of the Lord is supreme, no matter how much we protest.

Once you were sitting in your room in a very grave and silent mood. Your eyes were closed. Then, suddenly, tears began flowing from your eyes. In a choked voice you said, “My spiritual master was no ordinary spiritual master.” Then you paused for some time, and wiping the tears from your cheeks, you said in an even more choked voice, “He saved me.” This is exactly how we feel, Śrīla Prabhupāda, about you.

Obviously, you accomplished your mission. At least the Supreme Personality of Godhead thought so; otherwise, why would He have taken you from this world when He did? It is up to us, your disciples, to keep pushing this *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu forward. You have so kindly left us something to do in the service of the Lord.

However, what is our report? You asked us to increase from what you had done, or at least maintain what was already there. Have we fulfilled your desire? In answer, I would say that in some areas we have and in others we haven’t. We must become more serious to follow your instructions, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and please you. We pray for your mercy so that we can right our wrongs and increase our successes. It is only possible by your divine grace. In this regard I have written a small poem:

Where were we before you came, so kindly and by grace?  
You were of another world, and we of a miserable place.  
The spiritual world is full of bliss—beautiful and serene.  
What it said of our world, where people are harsh and mean.

Lord Caitanya sent a *jīvan-mukta* to spread His word divine.  
The Lord entrusted you to teach beyond mundane mortal time.  
Traveling abroad you went, sent by your predecessor teacher.  
What can be said of him, another powerful and brilliant preacher?

Following his order, you endured hardship, but preached so bold.  
Always humble and tolerant, you gave a message of pure solid gold.  
Now that you are no longer personally with us, what will we do?  
We can only pray for mercy because we are nothing without you.

*Your foolish disciple,*  
Ramāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet, which are my shelter, my succor.

At the critical moment of my life, I decided to place my trust and faith unconditionally in you, as the bona fide representative of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. On that decision I have since based the entire conduct of my physical, social, emotional, intellectual, and spiritual life. I have never once been disappointed in you. You have on no occasion let me down. Rather, I have found in my commitment to your authority the firm basis for the limitless expansion of life in all areas.

It is true that nowadays we sometimes hear around us a shrill thin chorus, like the keening of a sharp northern wind: the bitter wail of the voices of the disappointed. It didn’t work out for them, and they blame you for it. They invite me to reconsider, to revisit my youthful commitment to you, to be more knowing and more sophisticated, to attach (in fine print) conditions to my faith in you. Isn’t that faith simple-minded, naive, immature? Face it: Isn’t it just plain mistaken?

This sound is newly audible in the society of devotion you founded. I supposed that the shared confidence in you was once so strong that the disappointed simply went away silently, or if they remained, muted their doubts. No more.

It is of course true that we in ISKCON have encountered much in the way of failure and disappointment, both personally and institutionally. I will spare you here a catalogue of our many failures. Certainly in my own life I have met with far too much disappointment in my spiritual aspirations. Too often I have been thoroughly unhappy and frustrated with this movement. But I cannot pin my personal failures or our joint failures on you.

*Your* fault is your mercy.

You have given us the highest of ideals, and we have simultaneously embraced them and spurned them. High ideals have their downside: They torment us, mocking all our enterprises. I owe countless sleepless nights to the sheer reproach of their perfect repose. Yet I cannot exorcize them. They draw us eternally on.

I cannot find it in me to blame you for your mercy. You have shown us the living image of perfection, you have offered sainthood to us as our real vocation. You have shown us the clear way to perfection, in this life and the next. Now that we know it, we cannot go back to being mere beasts, however much we would, in our worst moments, desire such oblivion.

Therefore there is no other way left to go but to follow you forever.

I beg you for the spiritual strength to never stop trying to offer you what I owe to your mercy. You have shown me what it means to be a civilized human being; you have taught me the language of transcendence. Please make me fit to receive and to use your gifts.

Falling at your feet and begging for your mercy, I beg to remain

*Your servant,*  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dearly beloved spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and prostrated obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories unto to Your Divine Grace!

I am coming before you on the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā event with great joy in my heart. I am so eager to express to you my ever-increasing happiness in assisting you in your preaching mission! The opportunities to bring the all-comforting and sublimely elevating *bhakti* process firmly and irrevocably into the lives of others are so *abundant*! In nearly unlimited ways you are providing the means to bring sincere souls together to taste the nectar of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and I am simply delighted and most grateful to you that I can be part of this great task, assisting and serving you as you serve all of humanity and the whole world.

I am certain that you will be pleased to receive this report: to this day, candidates for Kṛṣṇa consciousness are responding very positively to your teachings here in America; I feel that your Society’s dedicated members, both leaders and rank and file, are maturing and becoming more and more capable representatives of your teachings as time and experience increase their scope of service; and personally I would like to report that day by day I am feeling increasingly full and satisfied in my own Kṛṣṇa conscious life!

As age creeps upon the body, and as health gradually weakens, and as the demands of travel and performing joyous but taxing services on your behalf unceasingly increase, I cling to the powerful example you amazingly demonstrated of impeccable regulation and *sādhana*. Such strength is found in the discipline of spiritual regulation! Thank you so much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being our perfect guiding light and example to follow, even in such a detail as executing daily duties consistently, in a quality way, powered with spiritual strength, and thereby assisting us to maintain Kṛṣṇa conscious decorum. This is no small gift that you gave us, just one more brilliant jewel adorning your perfect *ācārya* example.

When I hear your recorded lectures or read your books, I find ever-fresh inspiration. I hear the clarity of your message consistently pointing out the worse-than-fruitless nature of being situated in material existence, while simultaneously you appeal with a deep sense of urgency and alacrity to your audience, your followers, to assist you in the mission of rescuing these same wayward souls. Like the compassionate Prahlāda Mahārāja, you knew the abject foolishness of the materialists, denouncing them as “fools and rascals”; yet you refused to abandon them but instead sacrificed your health and your very *life* with indomitable determination to bring the same souls to a civilized existence filled with wisdom and purity. In the words of Prahlāda Maharaja,

O best of the great personalities, I am not at all afraid of material existence, for wherever I stay I am fully absorbed in thoughts of Your glories and activities. My concern is only for the fools and rascals who are making elaborate plans for material happiness and maintaining their families, societies, and countries. I am simply concerned with love for them. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.43\]

In this way, you exhibited the perfection of being Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s emissary.

*vairāgya-vidyā-nija-bhakti-yoga-*  
*śikṣārtham ekaḥ puruṣaḥ purāṇaḥ*  
*śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya-śarīra-dhārī*  
*kṛpāmbudhir yas tam ahaṁ prapadye*

**“Let me surrender unto the Personality of Godhead who has appeared now as Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. He is the ocean of all mercy and has come down to teach us material detachment, learning, and devotional service to Himself.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **6.254)**

As an empowered *ācārya,* you gave the whole world perfect knowledge, a way of life in devotion to Kṛṣṇa that brought with it the very means of realizing that knowledge, and detachment from all other things. You provided us, by way of your own example, a life of perfection to illustrate these same teachings, and by so doing you were simultaneously the unquestionably perfect instrument of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu! I simply pray to likewise be your instrument.

I want to illustrate your expertise in accomplishing these three objectives which Lord Caitanya brought into this world, and which you extensively broadcast worldwide, by drawing attention to one ray of your brilliance, which is but part of a vast effulgence of light you endlessly displayed.

In June of 1975 you conducted a conversation with a professor of psychology, Dr. John Mize. For all of his overt sincerity and his respect for you, Dr. Mize posed the same question over and over again in his interview with you, yet he found no resolution to this question entering his heart from your direct replies.

Dr. Mize was puzzling over the following basic question: “How does the mind come to know that there is a soul?”

You replied:

\[Y\]ou have to \[be\] educated. How these people \[the devotees\] are convinced about the soul? They have been educated by practice and by knowledge. Everything has to be learned by being educated. And therefore the Vedic injunction is *tad-vijñānārtham,* “In order to know that science,” *gurum eva abhigacchet,* “you must go to guru, teacher.” So the answer is that you must go to the teacher who can teach you how the soul is there.

A devotee suggested:

In other words, Śrīla Prabhupāda, without practice of the method, it’s very difficult to understand the philosophical concepts?

And you confirmed:

Yes, yes, yes.

That which was clear to your young disciples present in the room was unclear to a scholarly Ph.D. and a good soul. Prabhupāda, you gave us more than just knowledge: you gave us the real thing, *bhakti,* which is the very means of purifying consciousness. By the strength of surrender to the *bhakti* process, all undesirable coverings can be removed and real knowledge can be understood with complete realization. Moreover, Nārada Muni instructs that a faithful student of the self-realized teacher becomes endowed with all the qualities of the teacher, starting with attachment to Kṛṣṇa. I simply pray to be forever attached to you and your service, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In this way I hope to deepen my attachment for Kṛṣṇa, knowing that if I dedicate my life to your service this spotlike existence of insignificance will have true and everlasting value and meaning, and at the same time I will be protected from the most treacherous onslaught of the very powerful illusory energy.

You are most merciful! Please keep this insignificant servant of yours forever in the cooling shade of your divine lotus feet.

*Your servant,*  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are my worshipable deity. All your devotees are worshipable, and they spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this horrible Age of Kali. Please continue to engage me in your service.

*Your eternal son,*  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! You are my unfailing beacon in the raging storm of time. I take shelter at your lotus feet, where the passing of days becomes the key to eternity.

2:34 am: Early in the morning, but past deadline, I sit to compose your Vyāsa-pūjā homage.

Just a minute! My back is giving me so much trouble I have to lay down on the floor, legs up against the wall to realign my spine. The chiropractor recommended it. Perhaps the extra blood flowing from my feet will oxygenate my brain and give me fresh inspiration. In this position I think, “What should I write to Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

2:45 am: Back to the table and feeling a bit better. I open my computer, and true to form, my early morning headache starts to dawn. How long can I tolerate it before I reach for the medicine chest? Yes, it is a chest, not a box. There are all sorts of pills to treat ailments, like a swollen prostate, high blood pressure, vitamin deficiency, nervous disorder, and of course my headaches. In the hopeless battle against age, my newest opponent to serving you, my multicolored medication is a newfound friend.

2:50 am: “Now that is a good theme for an offering”: how Śrīla Prabhupāda, at the age of sixty-nine, despite his heart condition, headaches, and fragile health, raced with time to establish his mission. It will probably be more acceptable to the editors than “How the GBC Gave Away its Authority to ISKCON gurus,” “Why Vaiṣṇavas Outside ISKCON Claim to Be Prabhupāda’s Followers,” or “Parallel Structures Within ISKCON.”

3:00 am: But first I need to take my series of pills and drink a liter of water. With luck I won’t catch a cold from drinking unheated water. The last cold took two months to get over. What a state! Here I am, moaning and groaning, and still a generation younger than you were when you stepped aboard the *Jaladuta*. While I cringe at the thought of a two-hour flight in an economy seat, you braved a steamer cabin for one month. The effects of age have widened my perspective on your achievements.

3:12 am: Some years back (I can’t remember just how long ago—my memory is not so good any more), our Queens Counsel (QC) lavished unending praise upon you. To better represent us in the public inquiry on the Manor, he had speed-read through all our books. The one he liked best was *Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta*. What touched him most was that you ventured out from India at the late age of sixty-nine and began your twelve-year *yajña* of traveling the world to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

3:15 am: I can see the QC’s stocky bowed form, his white hair and age-lined face. While speaking in the best Queen’s English to a group of devotees, he leant forward and said, “Do you know what a marvelous thing your leader did?”

I nodded. Of course we did.

“No you don’t!” he answered his own question.

I blinked. What did the QC mean that *we* did not understand the marvel of your achievements?

“I am not just speaking about how he started the movement, established temples, and wrote books. That was undoubtedly a miracle. But...” he raised his hand for emphasis, “to do all that at his age. To begin at seventy! That was the miracle.”

The QC looked at each one of us, and shook his head. “You can’t know what that was like. To set out on such a mission at seventy.”

“Just a minute here!” I was just about to protest, explaining how we, as disciples, certainly understood our spiritual master. But he saw it coming and in his best courtroom manner added. “And do you know why you can’t know?”

And, out of courtesy we said nothing.

“Because you do not know what it is like to be old.”

That was a bit of a bombshell. After all, I had read the *Gītā* and *Bhāgavatam* and certainly understood about inevitable old age. Perhaps the QC needed a few lessons in philosophy. Didn’t he know that realization through hearing was, for the wise, far superior to experience?

He repeated, “You don’t know what it is like to be old. But I do. And because I do, although I am not his follower, I recognize that what he did was a miracle. Therefore he was not an ordinary man.”

3:25 am: Since that conversation, many years, many groans, many pills, and a few nature cures have passed. Now I am faced with the most humiliating and burdensome challenge to my service—the inevitable results of age. I don’t yet understand what it is like to be seventy, but I know what it is like to be fifty-four. If I project my pains and aches forward fifteen years...well I prefer not to think about it.

3:27 am: A blazing fire is more beautiful at night than in the day. Similarly, the miracle of your achievements has been made all that more wonderful having taken place in the last years of your life instead of, say, in your youth.

I now say that from experience.

I am getting to know what it means to grow old. I don’t like the experience, the suffering. But with every passing day that experience focuses my attention on how wonderful, how empowered you were—how empowered you are.

3:27 am: Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give me a little of that empowerment, which enables old men to outpace youths, which gives them unlimited energy to travel the world, which makes them sleep less instead of more. Please give me a little of that empowerment, which makes the old wise, which makes them remember what they learnt instead of forgetting it. Above all, give me a little of your mercy, which, in the twilight of their years, makes foolish disciples into pure devotees.

3:29 am: With that prayer, my offering is finished. I rise to close the door. A newly discovered phenomenon—the draft—threatens me with a chill.

My obeisances at your lotus feet.

*Your servant,*  
Śivarāma Swami

## Suhotra Swami

My worshipful, all-merciful spiritual master, Your Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, cornucopia of Mahāprabhu-*kṛpā,* and the Śrīla Vyāsadeva of our age! This insignificant wretch begs a tiny corner of shelter under your fearless lotus feet. Kindly permit me to utter the auspicious prayers voiced by your countless servants, prayers that will continue to be voiced throughout ages to come:

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this anniversary day of your merciful descent into the realm of human perception, we search our hearts for a monument to you that we can form into words of praise. We might retrieve a memorable pastime of yours; a chastisement that saved and transformed our lives; or a realization that dawned by the divine grace of your books, lectures, or letters. We might come up with a small but brilliant jewellike moment when you blessed us with your glance, your nod, your smile. We might reflect upon the weighty mission of a lifetime that you ordered us to fulfill.

One thing is certain: when we plunge into our hearts to find this, our yearly Vyāsa-pūjā monument to you, we, like deep-sea divers exploring a sunken fleet of Spanish galleons, turn up treasure after treasure without end. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are a mine of wonders.

Today, on this Vyāsa-pūjā Day of year 2003, something that is indeed a priceless monument to your most wondrous nature has, with a life of its own, *forced* its way up to the surface of my heart. This treasure is that most fortunate host of great souls, the assembly of my Godbrothers and Godsisters: they whom you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, blessed with initiation into the eternal path of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*.

Now some of your spiritual children have grown into leaders of your great ISKCON. Other disciples render steady service to your movement from positions of near-anonymity. Many are not actively connected with ISKCON at all. Some have left this world to serve you elsewhere.

Whatever their apparent status might be, the great majority of boys and girls you gathered to your lotus feet from 1966 to 1977 remain faithful to you. Hardly any that I know of committed the mad elephant offense of rejecting Your Divine Grace. I have no doubt that each and every loyal disciple of yours is a “matchless gift,” a spiritual ornament that adorns your eternal form.

*śrī-bhagavān uvāca*  
*ahaṁ bhakta-parādhīno hy asvatantra iva dvija*  
*sādhubhir grasta-hṛdayo bhaktair bhakta-jana-priyaḥ*

**“The Supreme Personality of Godhead said to the** ***brāhmaṇa:*** **I am completely under the control of My devotees. Indeed, I am not at all independent. Because My devotees are completely devoid of material desires, I sit only within the cores of their hearts. What to speak of My devotee, even those who are devotees of My devotee are very dear to Me.”** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **9.4.63)**

Among the *bhakta-janas* (“the devotees of My devotee”) mentioned here, some may not be up to the mark of a pure *bhakta* like you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are “completely devoid of material desires,” so spotless in heart that Lord Kṛṣṇa is pleased to serve your every desire to serve Him. Nonetheless, the Lord proclaims all the *bhakta-janas* to be *priya,* “very dear to Me.”

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.19, Nārada Muni declares:

*na vai jano jātu kathañcanāvrajen*  
*mukunda-sevy anyavad aṅga saṁsṛtim*  
*smaran mukundāṅghry-upagūhanaṁ punar*  
*vihātum icchen na rasa-graho janaḥ*

**“My dear Vyāsa, even though a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa sometimes falls down somehow or other, he certainly does not undergo material existence like others \[fruitive workers, etc.\] because a person who has once relished the taste of the lotus feet of the Lord can do nothing but remember that ecstasy again and again.”**

In the video series “Śrīla Prabhupāda Memories,” we actually witness the truth of this verse. So many Godbrothers and Godsisters—in whose hearts are firmly enshrined the words *nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya...,* the prayer that glorifies you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as forever situated under the lotus feet of Mukunda—are *rasa-grahis*! They can do nothing but remember again and again the ecstasy of your divine association by which they relished the taste of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. According to Śrī Nārada, their status, however it may appear to our imperfect senses, is not material. Whether or not they exhibit the external signs of Vaiṣṇavism, as they relish their remembrances of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, they are with you. They love you. And loving you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, they love the lotus feet of Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, who are forever seated upon the throne of your heart.

*tathā na te mādhava tāvakāḥ kvacid*  
*bhraśyanti mārgāt tvayi baddha-sauhṛdāḥ*  
*tvayābhiguptā vicaranti nirbhayā*  
*vināyakānīkapa-mūrdhasu prabho*

**“O Mādhava, Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord of the goddess of fortune, if devotees completely in love with You sometimes fall from the path of devotion, they do not fall like nondevotees, for You still protect them. Thus they fearlessly traverse the heads of their opponents and continue to progress in devotional service.”**

On this day of your appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I fear to be counted as an opponent of my Godbrothers and Godsisters. I tremble at the countless offenses I committed against your treasured family of disciples who even now, decades after your seeming disappearance, continue to taste the ambrosia of your softest of soft, sweetest of sweet, lotus feet. O my dear spiritual master, what must I do to break down the wall of false ego that keeps me aloof from those whose abiding love for you is so plain to see?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, why is my heart so forbiddingly cold?

Why, even after my heart warms a bit due to such reflections as this that I’ve just now put into words, do the Arctic winds of impersonalism again blow through its chambers to turn it once more into a *māna-mandira* of solid ice?

Is there any hope for me? Only if my Godbrothers and Godsisters will mercifully forgive me, this *aparādhī* moving among them like an *asura* disguised as one of the Lord’s cowherd boyfriends!

My dear Godbrothers and Godsisters, so beloved by Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most fortunate of days that commemorates the appearance of our eternal father, I implore you to have a care for my misfortune. I am a breathing but dead brute stranded upon an iceberg of hard-heartedness. Will you not glance a melting, uplifting ray of mercy my way? Without that mercy, I am lost and drifting in the ocean of birth and death.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda and his transcendental family of beloved spiritual sons and daughters!

*Praying to be your humble servant and the humble servant of your humble servants,*  
Suhotra Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Ātmanivedana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisance. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I am writing this offering after a very long time. Every time I begin to think about this most wonderful day of your divine appearance, I am reminded of the good fortune I am graced by. I especially think about this when I get a reminder of the deadline to submit the offering. I start to think about writing—then again I feel very inadequate. Who am I to glorify the supreme pure devotee of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu? I have no qualification to write.

So I thought I would offer my life at your lotus feet. I beg for your causeless mercy to allow me to stay in shade of your lotus feet and permit me to serve this great institution, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Please empower me to take the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to the people of Gujarat.

For your pleasure, my colleague and I have constructed a traveling temple to take Lord Śrī Jagannātha, Śrī Baladeva, and Śrī Subhadrā-devī to the villages of Gujarat. We have named the project Śrī Garuḍa, “Garuḍa” being an acronym for Gaurāṅga’s Association for Rural and Urban Devotional Advancement. For the past two years we have been visiting the people of Gujarat and allowing them to experience the sweet pleasure of chanting and hearing the holy name of the Lord. By your mercy, we hope to carry on as long as we can.

Please accept this as my offering to you on this most auspicious day.

*The servant of your servants,*  
Ātmanivedana Swami

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my respectful obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. All glories to you!

There is nothing I can say or do or offer to you which would factually begin to describe your glories or be actually worthy of offering to you. For the simple truth of the matter is that this *jīva* is here only because of you, and whatever I have is yours. Whatever I know has come from you, whatever good qualities that may be visible in me are due to you and your mercy. You have given life to this otherwise dead body, you have given purpose to this *jīva’s* existence. You are the Lord and master of this *jīva*.

You forced open my eyes. I do not want to pretend that I see, but what little I do see or realize is by your mercy. Now by your mercy I take pleasure in hearing souls chanting the holy names. I take pleasure in hearing of the books that are distributed by the *saṅkīrtana* devotees. I get pleasure in watching the devotees cook for the Lord, clean His temple, offer *ārati* to Him, and perform other services. One of the greatest pleasures is hearing the stories of the *saṅkīrtana* devotees. These are real pleasures in life. The treasures of relishing devotional service. All by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have given the entire world this great process of devotional service, and it is so wonderful to see *jīvas* taking up the process. Thank you again and again for this greatest of gifts.

As you know, Gurudeva, I am no philosopher. I just would like to see the sweet holy name of the Lord spread throughout the entire world. That thought excites me so much. What can I say? You gave the name to the entire world—indeed, you came to give that name of your beloved Lord to all souls. Wonderful! Wonderful! Wonderful! It will be, it will be, it will be. This useless fool would dearly like to be your instrument in spreading *harināma* worldwide. I always think, “What else is there for us to do?” The holy name alone can render all benedictions, and you gave the name. I become excited just writing this to you. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. What can I say to you? Whatever good comes out of this soul’s mouth was put there by you. I can only keep thinking, *All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!* again and again and again. Sorry for using the “I” word so often, but by your mercy this useless soul now has some identity. “I.” I belong to someone. I am a servant. I have been given the holy name. I have been engaged in Prabhupāda’s service. I have wonderful brothers and sisters. I have seen your mercy working again and again. I am fortunate. I am Śrīla Prabhupāda’s.

Thank you again and again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness is simple for the simple. You have made Kṛṣṇa consciousness available to all.

I desire to become your simple instrument. Thank you for all the service you have given me and for the association of devotees. Please forgive me for all my offenses.

Useless as I am, you still keep hold of me.

Thank you. Your mercy knows no limits.

*Your insignificant servant, the worthless*  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhakti Puruṣottama Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I came in connection with ISKCON while you were still on this planet. I had a great desire to serve you. But by the time I joined ISKCON Your Divine Grace had left the world. I am serving your ISKCON society thinking this is your body. I am very thankful to you for arranging such a nice spiritual master for me as His Holiness Jayapatāka Swami, who is guiding me in my spiritual life and always training me how to serve you and your Society better. I feel very safe being in ISKCON. Though many of your followers have left this Society, still I feel there are enough of your disciples in this Society who can lead this movement nicely. You have really created a nice home. Whoever takes shelter of your lotus feet without any duplicity can feel safe in this home built by Your Divine Grace.

I do not have good scriptural understanding, and I have realized that by scriptural understanding alone one cannot develop love toward Kṛṣṇa. To get love for Kṛṣṇa one must have the blessings of His pure devotees. I am sure I have not developed love for Kṛṣṇa. What to speak of getting love for Kṛṣṇa, even my faith is not so strong. I am still in the neophyte stage. But I am convinced that by serving your Society without any personal motive, with the sole purpose to please Śrī Kṛṣṇa and deliver the fallen living entities, I will be purified. Though I have been serving for last twenty-five years in your movement, I still have a lot of impurities within me. I am not yet free from impurities even after serving you for so many years. But still I have the hope that one day I will be purified, since this is the only process for purification. I am satisfied that the process is correct, because even though I have not been freed from all my impurities, at least I have understood that I *have* impurities. This in itself I consider a good achievement for me.

Though you are not with us personally to guide me, I have solely taken shelter of your representatives, the GBC, which you created to look after the Society. I am trying my best to follow the orders of the GBC, accepting their orders as having come from you. By their order I am going to take on the big responsibility of serving as one of the zonal secretaries of ISKCON. I have no qualification for this service. I have accepted it simply with the idea that those Vaiṣṇavas who have given me this service will also give me the potency to do this job. I feel you are still on top of everything. You started this movement, you are running this Society now, and you will lead this Society for as many years as it exists. If you feel I deserve your blessings, then please empower me to serve your Society efficiently. By serving your Society, which is filled with so many nice Vaiṣṇavas, I am sure my life will be successful.

Thank you very much for accepting me as a member of your Society.
