*Your insignificant grand-disciple,*  
Bhakti Puruṣottama Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

**“When will Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfill the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who remain a constant source of inspiration to me and to so many other living entities in this material world.

By your kind mercy we now have a brand new temple here in Yogyakarta, along with the Lakṣmī/Nārāyaṇa Hostels for students. Daily, from the Garuḍa Hall attached to the temple, we are able to imbibe your sweet transcendental voice playing continuously over the loudspeaker. It makes for a very special atmosphere because your transcendental *bhajanas* can attract and purify anyone who comes in contact with such pure sound vibration. This is how you began captivating the young hippies in New York City way back in 1966. You knew that the holy name has great power because such sound is coming directly from the spiritual sky: *golokera prema dhana, harināma saṅkīrtana*. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bringing us such transcendental sound vibration, because through it you have given us entrance to the spiritual world.

I remain forever indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything you have given me. I don’t know where I would be without your gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I met a person the other day in Bali, a foreigner from Europe, maybe close to sixty years of age, who had just come in contact with the devotees. We took lunch *prasādam* together and he shared his deep appreciation for having come in contact with this wonderful process of devotional service. He is a doctor, an educated person. While speaking about his experience, as we were taking *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* his voice choked up, he could no longer speak, and he began to cry. After a few moments, he explained how he had been searching in so many countries all these years but to no avail. It was only upon coming here and contacting devotees that he found what he had been looking for all his life. This transcendental knowledge and the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness are so potent that anyone with even just a slight inclination can become transformed into a surrendered devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is so wonderful to see conditioned souls taking wholeheartedly to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is like finding a long-lost friend. Actually it is so:

*bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasāṁ sarva-loka-maheśvaram*  
*suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ jñātvā māṁ śāntim ṛcchati*

**“The sages, knowing Me as the ultimate purpose of all sacrifices and austerities, the Supreme Lord of all planets and demigods, and the benefactor and well-wisher of all living entities, attain peace from the pangs of material miseries.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **5.29)**

Also, just yesterday a student discovered our preaching center here. He was elated to know that devotees lived in Yogyakarta. For many years he has been practicing by himself—reading your books, reading the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* which he has pasted in large letters on the wall of his room. And then, due to some transcendental arrangement, he came in contact with one of your representatives and came to know that Kṛṣṇa’s temple and His devotees are here in the city.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so expert in brining others to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I pray that you kindly empower this fool so that I can somehow become an instrument in bringing devotional service into the lives of others. We have a difficult situation here in Indonesia. We would like to perform public *harināma,* we would like to walk the streets wearing *tilaka* and *dhotī,* we would like to openly distribute your books and openly preach the glories of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. But we are restricted due to circumstances. I feel that without your special mercy we will not find the ways by which we can somehow or other bring the many lost souls here in Indonesia back to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are expert in preaching according to time, place, and circumstance. I pray that you may kindly empower our devotees here in Indonesia, in particular here in Yogyakarta, where I am based, to reveal ways and means to attract significant numbers of souls back to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. We have some little clue, some little idea, but without your blessings upon us, these will not bear fruit. Please allow me to become more surrendered so that I may better understand your mission.

Although we are pushing for an educational complex here in Yogyakarta, the student capital of Indonesia, I know that our long-term solution lies in establishing *varṇāśrama* communities. Last year in a national newspaper an editorial appeared entitled “Solution to Indonesia’s economic problem? Petani...Farming...Back to the land!” I am convinced this is the solution not only to Indonesia’s economic problem but to every country’s economic problem. Hence your vision and mission of *daivī-varṇāśrama* is so important for us to understand and to implement. Through *varṇāśrama* we will more easily learn to serve and please Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Thank you once again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything you are doing. Please forgive my many frailties, incompetence, and shortcomings. Kindly bless me to help carry on your preaching mission, for without your kind blessings I am simply lost in the ocean of Māyā.

*Your servant,*  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

You have established the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world for the satisfaction of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the previous *ācāryas*. You always encouraged your disciples to continue to expand the mission of Lord Caitanya to each and every corner of the world, and you also showed us how important the work of traveling and preaching was by your own example. Despite your advanced age, even in failing health you continued to travel and deliver the message of Kṛṣṇa to the world.

For the last twenty years or more I have been traveling throughout the countries of Southeast Asia. Some of these countries openly profess that they are atheistic, while some are Islamic and some Buddhist. In fact, we know that they are all basically materialistic and absorbed in the business of economic development and sense gratification while sometimes making a show of religious belief. Still, one can find sincere seekers of truth everywhere, and when such persons meet the devotees of the Lord, they are happy to accept the Vaiṣṇava teachings and join the *saṅkīrtana* movement. This confirms what you told us—that devotees of Kṛṣṇa are everywhere and are just waiting for us to come and find them. Recently in Bangkok, Thailand, we had the experience of meeting one such man. Some years ago he had gotten his first devotional book through amazon.com, and then he got another from harekrishna.com, and a few years had passed before he met a devotee on book distribution. He wrote to me recently of his search for God. He explained that from childhood he’d been searching for God, trying different religions. Then he wrote:

For a long time, then, I went around in a circle. At times, I was Catholic, going to Mass and confession. Then I would go into a Muslim phase, trying to be Muslim again, going to Friday prayers. Then I would retreat into Buddhism because the other two just weren’t working for me....

I’m pushing 40 now, and I got started on this religious journey when I was 15 (I joined a minor seminary and then a religious order). So, I’ve been around a few blocks and have come to a few conclusions about religion, which I want to share with you so that you’ll know approximately where I’m at.

I think spiritual life progresses in stages, doesn’t it? From all my reading and studying and experiencing, I think the stages are pretty similar, no matter what path you’re going down.

One of the conclusions I reached years ago is that God isn’t a wish-granting gem....We should not ask God for things—God knows what we need, and what we don’t, and we should not be begging and pleading and whining for this or that. So I became very suspicious of my own intentions—did I “love God” just to curry favor, or in hope of getting something in return? Was it like some sort of business deal—I do my part, obligating God to do his part? Why should I ask God every day for my daily bread—doesn’t he know that I have to eat?

Another conclusion: you can’t love someone if you’re afraid that person will destroy you. That’s \[the view of several major religons\]: love God, or else be destroyed in eternal hellfire. What good is that sort of love? And how could you love a “god” that’s so vindictive that he destroys people who don’t believe in him?

Another conclusion: God should be loved out of gratitude, because he made us, he created us, and obviously has some sort of plan for us. In other words, we should submit ourselves to the one who created us. We should acknowledge the truth of our position, that we are merely creatures, and that there is a creator out there who deserves our gratitude, and whom we should obey if he has provided guidance as to how we should live our lives.

Another conclusion: thinking and studying only takes you so far. You can run yourself in circles and never get anywhere. It’s the actual doing that makes a difference. At some point you have to give up the intellectual approach, because God cannot be found that way (or at least not easily).

Another conclusion: Of all the scriptures that I’ve read...the Bhagavad-Gita is far and away the best, the most eloquent, the clearest, the deepest, as though God did indeed inspire or write this book....

The Gita gets into the heart of spiritual life, and talks about the different paths and approaches, and offers a picture of God that is far grander than anything else I’ve ever encountered, a God worthy of being worshiped, of being loved, a God that is big enough to accommodate those who don’t believe in him.

And what does this God want? Not holy wars, or the destruction of evil-doers, but simply to be loved, for us to remember our position as creatures that he has created, to situate ourselves in devotional service, to chant his names, to refrain from harming other creatures, to take just what’s needed from the world around us (and leave some for others! ), to refrain from addictions and questionable behavior like gambling and drinking that can harm oneself or others.

In so many ways, the picture that is painted of Śrī Kṛṣṇa—youthful, happy, tolerant, fun-loving, full of opulence, a real person (not some nebulous cloud of energy or something), someone who answers Arjuna’s questions in the Gita (instead of silencing him)—this picture seems to me to be exactly how God should be. God should not be vindictive. God should not need to send his “son” to die so that he will be persuaded to stop being angry at the human race. God has no need of anything—we cannot buy his favor by building lavish temples or giving huge donations, or tormenting our bodies with excessive penances. The only thing that we should do is love, not with a selfish love that is hoping to get something in return, but with a love that a child feels for the parents who created him, with a love that’s given freely because the recipient is so worthy of it.

Well, I suppose I could go on and on...

I’ve been studying Krishna Consciousness for about 2 years now, being very cautious indeed. I’ve read the Bhagavad-Gita As It Is twice already, and I realize now that my search is over, that I don’t need to look any further.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is just one example of your mercy on the souls of this age. You have delivered countless souls, and we know that you will continue to do so as long as there are souls in need of your mercy. I only pray that I can somehow or other be of some assistance at your lotus feet.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

## Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Svāmī

*vṛndāvane ramaṇa-reti prasiddha-bhūmau*  
*tatrāpi kṛṣṇa-balarāma-supāda-mūle*  
*jñānaṁ paraṁ parama-kṛṣṇa-sudharmīty uktaṁ*  
*daṇḍas tu deva prabhupāda namo namas te*

**“In the transcendental land of Vṛndāvana’s pleasing Ramaṇa-reti sands, situated at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, Śrīla Prabhupāda is delivering the highest knowledge about Kṛṣṇa. I repeatedly offer my respectful obeisances unto that lord at whose lotus feet all the** ***prabhus*** **sit.” (From an eight-verse poem composed in 1975 by Paṇḍita Ananta Śāstrī, the then Sanskrit professor at the Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Gurukula)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I recently noticed in Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda’s *Āmnāya Sūtra,* verse 69, this *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* verse 9.4.21quoted:

*evaṁ sadā karma-kalāpam ātmanaḥ*  
*pare ’dhi-yajñe bhagavaty adhokṣaje*  
*sarvātma-bhāvaṁ vidadhān mahīm imāṁ*  
*tan-niṣṭha-viprābhihitaḥ śaśāsa ha*

**“In performing his prescribed duties as king, Mahārāja Ambarīṣa always offered the results of his royal activities to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, who is the enjoyer of everything and is beyond the perception of material senses. He certainly took advice from brāhmaṇas who were faithful devotees of the Lord, and thus he ruled the planet earth without difficulty.”**

Although this verse is glorifying how Mahārāja Ambarīṣa (the world emperor) served Kṛṣṇa in his kingly administration, it also reminded me of one of your many devotional qualities. For in all your manifested devotional activities you demonstrated faithfully how to offer oneself and all he has and does to Kṛṣṇa. In this you were fully true to the orders of your spiritual master.

The quotation continues:

“Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī explains:

*tac ca dehādi-śuddhānta-paryantasya sarvato-bhāvena tasminn evārpaṇam. tat-kāryaṁ cātmārtha-ceṣṭā-śūnyatvam.*

**“The pure-hearted soul should offer his body and everything else to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. If one does not do this, but instead acts selfishly for his own benefit, all that he does will become worthless.”**

I take this comment of the *ācārya* as his grace to further warn me (and indeed all of us, disciples and other followers in your ISKCON) that while we must now live in separation from your physical association, we must not misuse our minute independence to stray away from the devotional path as established by you in your *vāṇī*. Especially for those of us like me, who had very limited personal association with you, you are especially now manifested in your books, your letters, and your lectures.

Lately I have found myself attempting to drink once more, in the company of stalwart relishers in Vṛndāvana, the nectar of your rendition of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s ślokas* with purports. I find so much wonderful nectar there to churn with all your delighted followers. You worked so hard in the late evening hours to mercifully bestow this nectar on humanity. How could anyone desirous of good fortune desist from repeatedly drinking it? Even more amazing is that some want to try to make a feeble attempt to twist some “new interpretation” out of it.

While some others (even some among the ISKCON Vaiṣṇava community, however “senior,” “advanced,” or so-called “liberated” by their long amount of service) may have doubts and dare to openly think, question, or debate whether, twenty-five years after your departure, all your books, instructions, etc., you gave are still fully relevant “for the preaching, prabhu” to the “modern” world that, as conditioned souls, we have to live in, co-exist with, and preach your message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in on your behalf.

I can never agree with such doubts. To have doubts in your or any such liberated soul’s instructions, to consider such instructions open to speculative scrutiny, to wonder if “we’ll be effective in preaching” by following and preaching the letter of your books, is like a boy who can’t swim risking walking on very thin ice in a lake.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your mercy you’ve allowed me to see a few people and things come and go in ISKCON in the thirty years you’ve tolerated me, and if I’ve learned anything from all this, it’s this:

I’d rather not risk walking out on such spiritually thin ice. The *sāgara* (ocean) of *saṁsāra* (birth and death) is lying right below the thin ice of feeble conditional speculative discrimination. Even in the name of “spiritual life” or so-called “academic congeniality,” who can risk their spiritual life for the same?

If I treasure the uplifting grace, association, and protection of my ISKCON superiors and subordinates, then in that atmosphere of devotional accountability I’ll be protected and dare not fall through the thin ice of trying to subject the liberated souls, and especially my spiritual master’s words, to mundane comparison.

Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda kindly writes something to this point in the second song at the beginning of his *Kalyāṇa Kalpataru,* verses 6–9, which inspired me in this connection. Oh! When will I sing this from the heart with real *bhāva*?

*āmi to’ durbhaga ati baiṣṇaba nā cini*  
*more kṛpā koribena baiṣṇaba āpani (6)*

**“I am very unfortunate. I do not understand the Vaiṣṇavas. I pray that the Vaiṣṇavas will be merciful to me.**

*śrī-guru-caraṇe more bhakti koro dāna*  
*je caraṇa-bale pāi tattwera sandhāna (7)*

**“O Vaiṣṇavas, please give me the gift of devotion to my spiritual master’s feet. By the power of his feet, I will find the truth.**

*brāhmaṇa sakala kori’ kṛpā mora prati*  
*baiṣṇaba-caraṇe more deho dṛḍha-mati (8)*

**“O** ***brāhmaṇas*** **(of ISKCON!), please be kind to me. Please make my heart steady at my spiritual master’s feet.**

*ucca nīca sarba-jība caraṇe saraṇa*  
*loilāma āmi dīna hīna akiñcana (9)*

**“I, who am poor, lowly, and have nothing of value, take shelter of the feet of all living entities, both high and low.”**

Verse 9 of the song shows us how all of us who took birth in and who live in the present “modern” Western scheme of things should humbly pray to the Vaiṣṇavas for shelter and protection.

This dangerous Western *vīdeśī-bhāva* is now becoming increasingly manifested duty free in “modernized-liberated” Indian cities and towns, courtesy of modern media.

The *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* (1.22) says:

*durjātir eva savanā-’yogyatve kāraṇaṁ matam*  
*durjāty ārambhakaṁ pāpaṁ yat syāt prārabdham eva tat*

**“Low birth is thought to be the cause of one’s disqualification to perform the** ***soma*** **sacrifice. That sinful activity, however, which caused the low birth, is known as** ***prārabdha*****.”**

In your *Nectar of Devotion* you write:

A person is born in a *brāhmaṇa* family or in a family of dog-eaters due to his past activities. If a person is born in a family of dog-eaters it means that his past activities were all sinful. But if even such a person takes to the path of devotional service and begins to chant the holy names of the Lord—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—he is at once fit to perform the ritualistic ceremonies. This means that his sinful reactions have immediately become neutralized.

One real example of devotional humility that arises due to genuine spiritual advancement is not to take one’s low-class birth, mundane education, etc., for granted but rather to be even more vigilant then the “deśī” Indians practice.

I never forgot what wonderful example of this genuine Vaiṣṇava humility born out of carefully considering one’s “durjāti” (in actuality real *sujāti*—a true sense of being situated factually *acyuta-gotra*) in the real light and spirit of your above NOD statement.

In 1978, I practically witnessed in the regular example one of your great generals you subsequently cried for and blessed in London in September 1977, who in his *brahmacārī* life told me at three o’clock in the morning, “Prabhu, this body I’ve taken birth in is inherently fallen, much more than your body. Therefore I have to try \[to follow the devotional path\] twice as hard as you.”

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 25.278, you are right to warn us not to forget to read your books, lest we go back to the state we were in before we took your shelter:

All the devotees connected with the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement must read all the books that have been translated (the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā,* and others); otherwise, after some time, they will simply eat, sleep, and fall down from their position. Thus they will miss the opportunity to attain an eternal, blissful life of transcendental pleasure.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 24.130) it is said:

*bhaktye jīvan-mukta’ guṇākṛṣṭa hañā kṛṣṇa bhaje*  
*śuṣka-jñāne jīvan-mukta aparādhe adho maje*

**“Those who are liberated by devotional service become more and more attracted by the transcendental qualities of Kṛṣṇa. Thus they engage in His service. Those who are liberated by the speculative process eventually fall down again due to offensive activity.”**

Furthermore, in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 8.26, purport) you clarify the danger of a devotee offending one’s Lord or spiritual master by such speculation.

The word *vāsanā* (“material desires”) refers to dry speculative knowledge. Such speculative knowledge is only material. As confirmed in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.14.4), a person without devotional service who simply wants to know things (*kevala-bodha-labdhaye*) gains only dry speculative knowledge but no spiritual profit. This is confirmed in the *Bhakti-sandarbha* (111), wherein it is said:

*jīvan-muktā api punar yānti saṁsāra-vāsanām*  
*yady acintya-mahā-śaktau bhagavaty aparādhinaḥ*

**“Even though one is liberated in this life, if one offends the Supreme Personality of Godhead he falls down in the midst of material desires, of which dry speculation about spiritual realization is one.”**

In his *Laghu-toṣaṇī* commentary on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.2.32), Jīva Gosvāmī says:

*jīvan-muktā api punar bandhanaṁ yānti karmabhiḥ*  
*yady acintya-mahā-śaktau bhagavaty aparādhinaḥ*

**“Even if one is liberated in this life, he becomes addicted to material desires because of offenses to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”**

A similar quotation from one of the *Purāṇas* also appears in the *Viṣṇu-bhakti-candrodaya:*

*jivān-muktāḥ prapadyante kvacit saṁsāra-vāsanām*  
*yogino na vilipyante karmabhir bhagavat-parāḥ*

**“Even liberated souls sometimes fall down to material desires, but those who fully engage in devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead are not affected by such desires.”**

These are references from authoritative revealed scriptures. If one becomes an offender to his spiritual master or the Supreme Personality of Godhead, he falls down to the material platform to merely speculate.

You write in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 10.84):

Obeisances are therefore offered to the spiritual master as *sārasvata-deva,* or a member of the Sārasvata family (*namas te sārasvate deve*), whose mission is to broadcast the cult of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu (*gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*) and to fight with impersonalists and voidists (*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*).

Śrīla Prabhupāda, through your matchless *vāṇī,* you are *still* delivering the Western world (*durjātis* like me!) from voidness conceptions.

Here in your Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir, every day first thing in the morning at your *samādhi maṅgala-ārati,* and especially on this wonderful day of your appearance, it is a wonderful opportunity you give us all to sing from our hearts our gratefulness for your shelter.

For it is that strict adherence by all my ISKCON *śikṣā-gurus* who have thus far protected me, within that shelter *only* which allows us to confidently follow you as you demonstrate Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī’s words:

*tac ca dehādi-śuddhānta-paryantasya sarvato-bhāvena tasminn evārpaṇam*

**“The pure-hearted soul (Prabhupāda) offers his body and everything else to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.”**

Thus this shelter is presently keeping our attempts at devotional activities from plunging through the thin ice of into *tat-kāryaṁ cātmārtha-ceṣṭā-śūnyatvam:* “If one does not do this, but instead acts selfishly for his own benefit, all that he does will become worthless.”

On this day, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’ll follow Yāmunācārya (*Śrī Stotra-ratna* 49) to pray to you like this:

*vapur-ādiṣu yo ’pi ko ’pi vā*  
*guṇato ’sāni yathā tathā vidhaḥ*  
*tad ayaṁ bhavataḥ padābjayor*  
*aham adyaiva mayā samarpitaḥ*

**“O Lord, whatever I possess in terms of this body and its paraphernalia, and whatever I have from the modes of nature, today I offer at Your lotus feet.” (Quoted from Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda’s** ***Āmnāya-sūtra,*** **Chapter 2,** ***Sūtra*** **70, commentary).**

*Prabhupāda kiṅkara-dāsānudāsaḥ,*  
Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Svāmī

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, there are innumerable ways in which you can be glorified. In this offering I want to focus on what you have done for me, and who and what I am in relation to you. To others it may seem egoistic that I write about myself, but this offering is meant primarily for you and only marginally for others.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the more I attempt to glorify you, the more I realize how extraordinarily great you are and how totally insignificant and miniscule I am. Thus I more and more appreciate your extraordinary mercy to have engaged in your mission someone as low as myself. Who am I to act as a spiritual leader, to go from place to place and speak about the most exalted, purest-of-the-pure Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa? It would be more fitting that I be beaten with shoes and rejected from any assembly of pious persons. Instead, I am often offered honor and respect. But that is only because of my relationship with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for I am a very ordinary person from a very ordinary background of lust, sin, and illusion. Devotees are so kind to me due to my connection with you, as I happened to be one of the many persons sprinkled with drops from the tremendous ocean of mercy you so kindly brought. Devotees honor me and tolerate my many faults and insufficiencies because I am yours. I was and am a crow, but you are making me dance like a peacock.

As you yourself wrote in utmost humility:

You say that before meeting me you could not read or write, so this is all Krishna’s grace. If He likes, he can make a crow into a peacock. That is His grace. Krishna consciousness is so valuable that it plays wonderful, and there are many instances within our society. Similarly I was a crow, and now they consider me a peacock. But, I was trying to be a peacock whenever there was the opportunity. I took the endeavor to publish the “Back to Godhead,” but I wasted so much time. Ultimately Krishna saved me. \[Letter to Haṁsadūta Prabhu, 14 November, 1974\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made me an example of how your extraordinary potency can act through even the most unlikely instruments. My realization is meager: because you want me to repeat your message, I must strive to do so. And my speaking does have some effect, for at least a few listen and feel benefited. Yet I know it cannot be my potency or realization that is affecting them, for I have virtually none. It must be that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are working through me.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t want to compete with you. Such an attempt would anyway be impossible, for competition can only be between equals or near-equals. Of course I could make a show of being as realized as you, or nearly as realized as you—or even more advanced! Crazy as it may sound, there will always be enough gullible fools to comprise the supporting cast in such a farce. In the past some of your disciples have enacted such dramas, even successfully for some time, until finally being pummeled into infamy and degradation. So let it be clear that I am a struggling neophyte, clinging to your lotus feet as my only hope. You are the great personage chosen by Lord Caitanya to fulfill His heart’s desire of *kīrtana* all over the world. You are uniquely great even among *mahā-bhāgavatas.* If I am able to give any illumination to others, it is as stars reflect the sun. If I properly represent you as an emissary, that is my glory.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, it seems most unlikely that in this lifetime I will develop as an accomplished scholar, musician, singer, expert manager, or great preacher; nor do I have much aptitude for Deity worship or cooking. Here I am struggling to chant sixteen rounds, so what should I hope to achieve? Nevertheless, a real disciple is he who has embraced the spirit of service, so I must do my utmost to serve you as well as I can, despite my incompetence, and without being carried away by delusions of grandeur if my efforts sometimes yield success. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please guide me to discriminate between what is possible for me and what is not, what is required for me and what is not, and please engage me eternally in the service of your mission.

*yogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi,*  
*tomāra karuṇā—sāra*

**“In examining my eligibility for devotional service, I find none. Therefore your mercy is essential.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you echoed these words of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura by presenting yourself as one of us:

Actually we are all conditioned souls so our demand from Krishna to accept us is unreasonable. From my personal point of view, I think that I am so sinful that I cannot even approach Krishna to show me any favor. But I have only one hope—my Spiritual Master—He is very kind. So someway or other He is dragging me towards Krishna. That is the only hope. Sri Caitanya Caritamrta says therefore: Guru Krishna. By the mercy of the Spiritual Master, and by the mercy of Krishna, one gets into Krishna Consciousness. Narada Muni is our original Spiritual Master and he has dragged so many fallen souls towards Krishna, and we are also hoping to be dragged by Him through the disciplic succession. Otherwise, if we study our own qualifications, there is none—rather I have got so many disqualifications. \[Letter to Brahmānanda Prabhu, 10 March 1969\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you certainly possess all transcendental qualities, among them the extraordinary humility to pray as if devoid of good qualities. But such self-deprecation is actually suitable for a fallen soul like myself. So let me always remember those words of yours and my actual position in relationship to you and your dearmost Lord Kṛṣṇa, lest I think of myself according to what others say about me: “*sādhu,*” “scholar,” “fundamentalist,” and so on. Of course, I am not worthy of such compliments, but it is a fact that the process for self-realization you have given us is so exalted, especially in comparison to the fallen condition of the world today, that if anyone simply tries to follow your instructions and repeat them to others, it will surely be appreciated at least by devotionally inclined persons. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray for the spiritual strength and equilibrium of mind to remain in your service till the time of leaving this body, and beyond. And though it is not at all necessary that I be remembered after my departure, nevertheless should anyone care to do so, then I aspire that it not be as a “*sādhu,*” “scholar,” or whatever. My glory and the fulfillment of my life will be if others say, “He was a faithful servant of Śrīla Prabhupāda. He genuinely believed in the words of his spiritual master.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, a Godbrother of yours once told me, “My guru was like a lion, your guru was like a lion, and you should also be like a lion.” Considering that lions do not give birth to rabbits, I am trying to be your worthy disciple by repeating your words and speaking strongly as you did—for which some members of your movement criticize me: “Who are you to talk like that? We have seen so many hardliners talk big, big things, only to fall down and make fools of themselves. What is the guarantee that you also won’t fall down?” In reply I may contend, “Undoubtedly it is true that if ever I were to disconnect myself from the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda, then that act in itself would render me fallen. But although I and thousands of others may come and go, Śrīla Prabhupāda and his instructions will stay; and those who remain faithful to those instructions will triumph over the material energy. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s words are always glorious, and so is the endeavor to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda by repeating them. I am certainly not fit to do so; I agree that it is simply my impudence in doing so. But *śāstra* states that the disciple should praise the guru and repeat his instructions. So why should I deny my desire to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda? If you think that I will fall down, then better I take this opportunity to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda now, before my intelligence becomes spoiled. Certainly I must unceasingly pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda to purify my consciousness by constant meditation on his lotus feet. And if I follow his instructions carefully and don’t commit offenses, then I can stick tightly to his lotus feet without falling away. Fit or unfit, I must endeavor to speak, write, and be purified. Though my words may be infected with nasty consciousness, madness, lethargy, duplicity, dullness, desire for name and fame, anger, envy, immaturity, anarchy, *tamo-guṇa, rajo-guṇa,* maybe a little *sattva-guṇa,* and millions of inauspicious qualities piled on thickly in innumerable ghastly layers, still I must attempt to repeat what Śrīla Prabhupāda said. That is glorification of Śrīla Prabhupāda and is automatically auspicious. I must try to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda despite my inability.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep me engaged in your service and in the service of your servants, while always remembering my actual position:

*jagāi mādhāi haite muñi se pāpiṣṭha*  
*purīṣera kīṭa haite muñi se laghiṣṭha*

**“I am a worse sinner than Jagāi and Mādhāi and am lower than worms in stool.”**

*Praying to be your dāsa eternally,*  
Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

## Candramauli Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The song beginning *śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma,* Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s deep devotional expression of love at the lotus feet of his guru, Śrīla Lokanātha dāsa Gosvāmī, has become such a great source of spiritual enlivenment, for each morning we are reminded of the unlimited glories of your lotus feet. Personally I derive great happiness and satisfaction by remembering you each day when we chant these prayers.

*śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala-bhakati-sadma*

**Your sweet lotus feet are the complete abode of pure devotional loving service, our only meditation and shelter.**

*bando mui sāvadhāna mate*

**With great care and attention I humbly offer my head in complete surrender to your two lotus feet, feeling completely satisfied.**

*jāhāra prasāde bhāi, e bhava toriyā jāi,*

**Your mercy is not some cheap material boon or something that is lost due to the influence of time. It is the desire of our hearts desire.**

*kṛṣṇa-prāpti hoy jāhā ha’te*

**Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet are your property. If you recommend someone to Him, He is bound by your pure loving devotion.**

*guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya,*

**Your words alone will purify our heart and mind. Everything has been given.**

*ār nā koriho mane āśā*

**This is our determination; no other process is necessary.**

*śrī-guru-caraṇe rati, ei se uttama-gati,*

**Our faith will be tested. These tests, when accepted as your mercy, strengthen one’s attachment at your lotus feet.**

*je prasāde pūre sarva āśā*

**Whatever else we may desire, that is not a concern. It will automatically come, or not come, by your mercy. We desire only devotional service at your lotus feet.**

*cakhu-dān dilo jei,*

**This is your main business, to wake us sleeping souls from the lap of Māyā. We are being devoured by the darkness of material existence. You bear the torch of knowledge, restoring our vision.**

*janme janme prabhu sei,*

**We want no other master but you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, however many births it takes to obtain the complete shelter of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. The thought of anyone else never even enters our mind.**

*divya jñān hṛde prokāśito*

**Transcendental knowledge is your gift—that knowledge of our real identity as eternal servants of Lord Kṛṣṇa.**

*prema-bhakti jāhā hoite, avidyā vināśa jāte,*

**You are the fountainhead of pure love for Kṛṣṇa in the full mood of Lord Nityānanda, distributing the holy name everywhere and to anyone, thereby destroying the ignorance born of the bodily conception of life.**

*vede gāy jāhāra carito*

***Ācāryavān puruṣo veda.*** **That knowledge you speak is backed by the Vedic scriptures. It is not sentiment or speculation. Your glories are expounded throughout the** ***Vedas*****.**

*śrī-guru karuṇā-sindhu, adhama janāra bandhu,*

**Your mercy is vast, deep like the ocean. You are our only real friend because you give knowledge of Kṛṣṇa, our eternal friend. We are poor and very unfortunate. Please make us fortunate.**

*prabhupāda lokera jīvana,*

**Everyone is your disciple, an object of your mercy. Some know it, others do not.**

*hā hā prabhu koro doyā,*

**We know you are the most merciful; still, we are beseeching your mercy, for we are easily affected by** ***māyā*****.**

*deho more pada-chāyā,*

**The opportunity to increase our service—that is our cherished desire and the only source of happiness.**

*ebe jaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana*

**There is no one throughout the three worlds who is more famous because you are perfectly representing the all-famous Supreme Personality of Godhead by bringing the lost souls to His lotus feet.**

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to render some insignificant service by traveling and preaching. Although I am not at all qualified, the strength of your mercy continues to push me on.

There are many inconveniences and difficulties, such as the close interaction with materialistic people and erratic schedules, and at times we feel their negative influences considerably. Gradually, I hope to come to the correct understanding by seeing and accepting these obstacles as an opportunity to offer something of myself in devotion to your lotus feet.

I am unqualified in so many ways, yet I hope and pray I can improve to the stage where you will be pleased with my service.

All glories to you eternally!

*Aspiring to be a better servant at your lotus feet,*  
Candramauli Swami

## Candramukha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your divine mission! All glories to you, who have given us the most sublime process of devotional service, full of the potency to award love of Godhead! All glories to you, who are the very sublime process of devotional service personified, and also the love-of-Godhead distributor personified!

You have instructed us that if we feel indebted to you, we should preach vigorously. As I feel completely indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have no doubts that the service of preaching is the proper way to repay you. Therefore I pray to the dust of your lotus feet that I can serve you eternally by preaching the glories of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. But I also know that when I endeavor to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you give me even more mercy. In this way, in the attempt to reciprocate and “repay” you, I become even more indebted. Actually, I think this is all a transcendental snare—a snare of love! And I want be your prisoner and, in this way, attain the perfect liberation.

Since I can’t ever repay this debt to you, I offer myself to be your servant life after life, preaching the glories of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement eternally. Please grant this benediction to your most unworthy servant.

Candramukha Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

That I met you was not my choice—  
Now you’re my view and you’re my voice.  
Each thing I say would base or clip  
In every way from *śāstra’s* tip.  
A world of words you left behind.  
Once ships of fools and now not blind.  
A world of words to be our map,  
And words shall lift us from the trap.  
We sail towards that lotus land  
And take along so many more.  
We frail and small, your vision grand,  
Now help us all to reach that shore.  
How can I take them to that place  
Where none are lost and love is true,  
No deed is done that is disgrace,  
And all things run to One in Blue?  
This day we think of where we stand,  
This day we talk of hearts and goals,  
This day we move with lamps and fan,  
This day we rake our dimming coals,  
Again to live that dream afresh,  
Again to rise beyond the day,  
To push beyond this fearful flesh,  
Again to want what you would say.  
That I met you no merit mine;  
Now you’re my guide throughout all time.  
Each thing I say may help them see  
This fortune tall that’s taken me.  
We work with words to reach to hearts,  
But only grace gives it a start.  
We work with words and make them deeds;  
Our lives then can be fertile seeds.  
Not sail alone, for many come;  
Not fail alone in darkest night.  
Each other need and not to shun;  
Your mercy then makes all things right.  
Empower those who’ve won inside  
Against the force of selfish want,  
And take us too one day to ride—  
Mercy, we say, comes from your font.  
This day we think of where we stand,  
This day we talk of hearts and goals,  
This day we move with lamps and fan,  
This day we rake our dimming coals,  
Again to live that dream afresh,  
Again to rise beyond the day,  
To push beyond this fearful flesh,  
Again to want what you would say.  
Could any guess, when I met you,  
What changes it would bring me through?  
Each day I see another man  
Is speaking where I used to stand.  
How much I longed to leave behind  
That pain and darkness that would bind!  
How many times I must have dreamed  
That there was some way to be free!  
I also sail, but I want not  
To reach there and bring no one more.  
Let me take courage and take thought  
From friends whose steps are deep and sure.  
I thank you from my deepest heart:  
Lost in confusion was my life,  
Now all is clear and to the mark—  
Your gift has led me out from strife.  
This day we think of where we stand,  
This day we talk of hearts and goals,  
This day we move with lamps and fan,  
This day we rake our dimming coals,  
Again to live that dream afresh,  
Again to rise beyond the day,  
To push beyond this fearful flesh,  
Again to want what you would say.

Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

To His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedānta Svāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda on the 107th anniversary of his appearance day in this world.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, most precious of all spiritual gems, kindly accept my eternal obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Whether considered by direct experience or scriptural evidence, clearly Your Divine Grace is the most wonderfully qualified spiritual master.

Through direct experience we...

- Appreciate how Your Divine Grace saved us from lives of sin and waste,
- Relish your rich *kṛṣṇa-bhajanas,*
- Behold your Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities,
- Taste your nectarean *prasādam,*
- Treasure your wisdom,
- Savor the sandalwood paste of your kindness,
- Marvel at your management genius,
- Embrace your loyal Vaiṣṇava children and grandchildren,
- Organize ISKCON Vedic colleges to educate thousands.

By scriptural evidence we...

- Confirm that you are a true *gosvāmī* who controls his senses—*vāco vegaṁ manasaḥ krodha-vegaṁ jihvā-vegam udaropastha-vegam/ etān vegān yo viṣaheta dhīraḥ sarvām apīmāṁ pṛthivīṁ sa śiṣyāt.* (*Upadeśāmṛta* 1)
- Understand that you are a bona fide guru coming in the line of disciplic succession called the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya.
- Honor you as much as God—*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit/ na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ.* (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.17.27)
- Realize that you are directly empowered by the Lord in the *nārade śakti ‘bhakti’* category as a preacher of *bhakti*—*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe* \[*nāma*\]*pravartana.*(*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya* 7.11)
- Recognize that you are the *senāpati-bhakta* predicted by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who said, “Even if the sinners reject religion or flee to foreign countries, still they will get the mercy. I will send *mor senāpati bhakta* to go there and deliver them.” (*Mor* means “My,” *senāpati* means “a military field commander,” and *bhakta* means “a devotee.” So Lord Caitanya is here pledging that He will empower His own devotee to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world. The quote is from the *Caitanya-maṅgala,* by Locana dāsa Ṭhākura—*Sūtra-Khaṇḍa,* Chapter 1; Lord Caitanya is speaking to His associates in Goloka.)

Dear Gurudeva, kindly purify this lowly beggar and engage me in your service eternally.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Dhanvantari Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my humble and prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

Although your presence is so outstanding and alive on each day of my life, it is always on the day that I have to write your Vyāsa-pūjā offering that I am filled with such a great emotion. Therefore, in this state of mind, I’m coming to you today, when the most blessed of all of the events, your Vyāsa-pūjā, is celebrated, to offer you some words in praise to your saintly personality.

Certainly you are endowed with all the delightful qualities of a pure soul, but it is your magnanimity that I would like to point out this time. Due to this quality, the world today has a hope of achieving pure spiritual knowledge, free from impersonalism and sentimentalism. Your instructions carry the hope of a new world order that is gradually taking shape because it is being realized by an increasing number of intelligent men and women—and this is only happening due to your magnanimity.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Thank you for encouraging me to compose yet another offering for you this year.

Our whole Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is depending on this enthusiasm. Just like I went to your country. At the age of seventy years, nobody goes out of home. But there was enthusiasm, “Yes, I must go.” And because I went, there is something. \[*Nectar of Devotion* lectures, Vṛndāvana, 24 October 1972\]

Yes, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is depending on enthusiasm—your unceasing enthusiasm, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

You have revealed the attitude of the enthusiastic worker in this way: “My Lord is so great, so kind, so beautiful, so wonderful. So I must speak something about my Lord.”

No one can ever doubt your boundless passion for glorifying your Lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

In your *Nectar of Devotion* you have defined enthusiasm as “endeavor executed with intelligence in Kṛṣṇa consciousness”; and elsewhere you have described intelligence as “the form-direction of Supersoul.” We understand the Supersoul to be Hṛṣīkeśa, master of the senses, and Bhokta, the supreme enjoyer of His potencies. Fully realized souls are expert in utilizing all varieties of the inferior energy for the purpose of the superior energy, or in other words, for the service of the master of the energies. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, proved yourself to be the most enthusiastic, intelligent, expert, and practical of all because you simply did what had to be done. Others, likewise, became enthused to assist you because you set the ideal example of selfless service.

“Utility is the principle.”

“Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the practical side of yoga.”

“Philosophy is the highest, but even higher than philosophy is practice of philosophy.”

“Kṛṣṇa Consciousness means practical living, not something utopian or idealistic and vague. We simply do the needful, whatever pleases Kṛṣṇa most, that’s all.”

“My Guru Maharaja used to say, ‘Anything material, if it is used for the service of Kṛṣṇa, it is a mistake to give it up.’...You should take it to heart as a guiding principle that somehow or other we always please Kṛṣṇa by doing what is practical and necessary, according to time and place.”

These instructions of yours, like most others, especially come alive by referencing them to the context of your personal example. This is natural, for you are the perfect *ācārya*. How refreshing was your descent on the contemporary spiritual scene! You were the straight-shooter amongst the hodge-podgers, the down-to-earth amongst the airy, and the sensible amongst the far-fetched. You practiced what you preached and expected the same of us, especially in regards to our personal vows.

You taught us about the different grades of servants (in ascending value):

one who refuses to follow  
one who follows irreverently  
one who follows with enthusiasm  
one who anticipates the order

Besides these, there are many who work with apparent enthusiasm and spontaneity but are still attached to their own concocted idea of duty. Like the mundane philanthropist, the servant of all is really servant of none. You have noted in your *Gītā* commentary that “after doing something whimsically \[one\] should not offer the result to the Supreme Lord. That sort of duty is not in the devotional service of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One should act according to the order of Kṛṣṇa. This is a very important point. That order of Kṛṣṇa comes through disciplic succession from the bona fide spiritual master.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you demonstrated your complete comprehension of your Guru Mahārāja’s mood and direction by your practical service to him. You understood the order of the day and executed it with all of your faculties. While others could only speculate and romanticize on the meaning of Lord Caitanya’s words—*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma/ sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  \[*CB Antya-khaṇḍa* 4.126\] (was it just an inspirational slogan?)—you took the order of your Guru Mahārāja in a most practical way: “So we have to simply take instruction from guru, and if we execute that to our heart and soul, that is success. That is practical. I have no personal qualification, but I simply tried to satisfy my guru. That’s all.”

You set the most sublime example of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s revolutionary statement: “I am prepared to do anything for the service of Mahāprabhu, for the propagation of His mission. The grace of Mahāprabhu should be distributed throughout the length and breadth of the whole world. And for that, anything should be done. No stone unturned. Physical connection is no connection. We shall try to save our internal purity, that is all.” Out of your immense compassion, you extended Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s principle of *yukta-vairāgya* to the extreme. Although others have since then tried to follow your lead, alas, the requisite purity of purpose is almost impossible to duplicate. You, however, were continually able to “catch the big fish without getting wet.”

You took risks against all odds and expected that criticism might follow from even your intimate spiritual family members, but your amazing success enabled you to ultimately boldly proclaim, “No one is allowed to speak anything. All mouths locked tight!”

When your men encountered some trouble in Japan, you illustrated the folly of finding fault with the empowered *ācārya* by relating the story of the fortunate loser: “One man said that ‘I have lost fifty thousand this year.’ His friend said, ‘You are still fortunate, because you had fifty thousand, but I have no fifty paisa even.’” Your efforts always produced noteworthy results, while your critics had nothing to show.

You finally summed up all the criticism with a single Prabhupāda-ism: “But for all that, I am expanding; and for all their strictness, they are doing nothing!” Which is exactly what I would have done with my life—nothing—had it not been for your unlimited enthusiasm, intelligence, expertise, and realization. Please continue to extract some service from this otherwise useless soul.

*Your ever-dependent son,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As I write this offering, I have before me a favorite photograph I have of Your Divine Grace. You are sitting in a garden on a thin mat on the grass, hand in beadbag, looking down. Your posture and demeanor are the essence of humility.

No ordinary individual would ever suspect that the man in this photo had changed the course of history and had millions of dollars at his disposal and thousands of surrendered souls at his beck and call.

I always like to meditate on what your mood might have been as you sat there with head bowed. I get a glimpse of that mood when you talk about the humble heart of a devotee. Below are some of the things you have said, especially regarding all of your accomplishments and the devotees who have given their lives to you:

So whatever I demand from you, it is for Krishna’s sake. Personally I am humble servant of Krishna as you are also, but I am deputed to accept your service just to transfer it to Krishna as via media. I shall try to do this service to you and Krishna throughout my life, and I am so proud to have such assistants as you are to help me in my mission to push on the Krishna Consciousness Movement.” \[Letter, 1 May 1969\].

*Sarvasya cāhaṁ hṛdi sanniviṣṭo mattaḥ smṛtir jñānam apohanaṁ ca*. So always think like that, that...Do not try to take credit yourself. We should always remember that it is Kṛṣṇa’s credit. He wants to do something at a certain time, and some of his servant is authorized to do that. That’s all. Otherwise, whole credit goes to Kṛṣṇa. That is Kṛṣṇa consciousness. \[Lecture, 19 January 1975\]

Therefore sometimes, when people say in India, “Swamiji, you have done wonderful.” And yes, I do not know. I’m not a magician. But, so far I am confident that I did not adulterate the words of Kṛṣṇa. That’s all. That’s my credit. I did not like to take the position of Kṛṣṇa. I remained a servant of Kṛṣṇa, and I spoke what Kṛṣṇa said. That’s all. This is my secret. So everyone can do that. There is nothing magic. \[Lecture, 19 May 1972\]

My Spiritual Master has given me the instruction to spread this movement all over the world and you are my good disciples, are helping me do this. Without your good help I could not have done anything, so practically you can take all the credit for spreading this Krishna Consciousness movement and fulfilling the prophecy of Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu. \[Letter, 26 August 1972\]

I am very pleased that you are doing nicely. But don’t be proud. Always remain humble, meek, that “I am nothing. I cannot...” That will be nice....Just like even *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* author, he says, *purīṣera kīṭa haite muñi se laghiṣṭha:* “I am lower than the worm in the stool.” *Jagāi mādhāi haite muñi se pāpiṣṭha:* “I am more sinner than Jagāi-Mādhāi.” In this way...That is not artificial. A Vaiṣṇava must think like that, that “I am rotten. I have no value.” Don’t be proud. Then the things will go on nicely. And as soon as you become proud, then *māyā*—“Yes, you are God. Come on. I will kick you on your face. Come on.” \[laughter\]...Don’t become like that. Always remain humble servant and you will be happy. \[Lecture, 3 August 1975\]

Lord Caitanya exclaimed that the humility of Rūpa and Sanātana “could even melt stone.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 16.263) Such is the power of a pure devotee. I can personally attest to that, for I am seeing how your humility, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is magically melting my stonelike heart. Hardened and frigid by lifetimes of sin, this heart had for so long kept me standing erect with head held high in the sky. But by the soothing and unremitting warmth of your loving embrace, I could no longer maintain my stance and miraculously came tumbling down, my head finally taking shelter at your lotus feet.

As I contemplate your photograph, I muse that I will certainly never fully fathom the depth of devotion you have for your guru, your disciples, the conditioned souls, and your eternal Lords, Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. But I have faith that if I can just be blessed by you to keep my head down, meditating deeply on your penetrating words and praying fervently for purity in thought and deed, then perhaps some day I will get a glimpse this humble service mood that you so perfectly portray.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpresaka Swami

We offer our most humble obeisances to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who has kindly given us the book *Kṛṣṇa: The Supreme Personality of Godhead*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to follow your instructions, take your ample association through books, CDs, ISKCON, temple worship, *japa, saṅkīrtana*. We hope to become like a match amongst dry wood, an impetus for burning love for Kṛṣṇa wherever we go. It is your mercy.

This last year we saw many miracles confirming how by the mercy of guru even blind men can see the stars in the sky, dumb men can recite poetry, and a dog can swim across the unlimited ocean.

By the mercy of Vaiśeṣika Prabhu and others a thought came into our head: “How can we distribute full sets of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*?” He told us the technique: “Buy lots of books. Stack them up where you can see them. And distribute them.” So we tried it. With struggle we got thirty sets of *Bhāgavatams* from Spain to Peru. They arrived in December, and by February they were all gone and the devotees were ordering another thirty sets. It’s a miracle. Your devotees are all little miracles.

Although we are still rather incompetent at selling books in the street, we have been trying to lecture in the universities, help the Bhaktivedanta Institute, do some academic preaching. In that capacity we were invited to participate in a national conference called Science and Technology in Ancient India, organized by the University of Kolkata, your hometown. We thought that this was an extraordinary honor, but when we got there we were even more surprised to learn that we were to be the chief guest. On inauguration day the vice chancellor of Kolkata University, the president of the West Bengal Science Association, Prof. S. K. Mukherjee, as well as Śrī P. C. Chundar, former Indian Union Minister of Education, were on our left and right, respectively. All were wearing nice suits and ties. We were dressed in simple *dhotī* and *kurtā* and feeling somewhat like a street dog sitting on a platform. Of course, Kolkata University is a communist world, and the members of the learned audience were staring at us skeptically: “Why is he here? What is he going to do, sing a song? This is a science congress.” But we were feeling well trained by you. We have done this lecture over and over again at different universities, before both simple students and learned professors of physics, and we’ve even discussed the topics with Nobel Laureates. (Still, we were nervous—“God help us! Prabhupāda, help us!”) But after we’d spoken just a few minutes, instead of glaring at us the audience began to take out paper and take notes on the lecture, and in the end the organizers of the conference asked us to expand the presentation to twice the size for the final publication.

We explained how Western science starts with the proposition that matter is real and then goes up to explain God as a by-product of matter, whereas the Bhāgavata philosophy of nature starts with Kṛṣṇa as real and comes down to explain matter as ephemeral. We used many examples from physics, chemistry, psychology, karate, theater. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for allowing us to put a “one” in front of our many, many “zeros.”

We don’t know what will happen this year. We feel more and more like a small, fragile electric light bulb illuminating the darkness. We need to be connected to you at every moment or the whole world becomes material, meaningless. Please *continue* to help us. We have a little more distance to travel on this rough road while you hold our hand.

*Your infant child,*  
Hanumatpresaka Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Lately I have been doing some research about the days in America when Śrīla Prabhupāda had begun his preaching and was now trying to publish his books. It was 1966, and some young men had come to him and begun to take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They had only the barest ideas of the philosophy. But the Swami was wonderful, *prasādam* was great, and chanting got you higher than LSD. And by hearing the transcendental sound of the *Bhāgavatam* and the holy name, delivered in the line of pure devotees, these new American followers were becoming purified. Śrīla Prabhupāda, as he had prayed to Kṛṣṇa on the ship, was acting as Kṛṣṇa’s servant—and now, through Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇa was unfolding a strange and wonderful drama.

In a little storefront on the Lower East Side, in the midst of hippy dropout acidheads, in the East Coast venue for America’s 1960’s freak show—a psychedelic zoo—Śrīla Prabhupāda was preaching Kṛṣṇa’s message of pure devotional service, and—Jhārikhaṇḍa revisited!—creatures who’d been living like animals were dancing and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.

There was *kīrtana*, there were lectures, there was *prasādam*—but where were the books? He had three—the three volumes, reddish brown, of his original *Bhāgavatam*. He had *Easy Journey to Other Planets*—a small booklet with a pale-green cover, brought over with the *Bhāgavatams* from India. And with his usual persistence, determination, and faith in the words of his Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Prabhupāda was working on more.

As his new followers “turned on” with the *mahā-mantra* and “tuned in” to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda—authentically more “dropped out” than everyone—was writing *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is.* On a vintage typewriter placed atop a yellow tin suitcase that served as a desk, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in his apartment one flight up in the back, typed with two fingers—at night, during the day, whenever he wasn’t leading *kīrtana,* or giving class, or cooking and serving *prasādam* with his own hands to his new recruits, or preaching to them, or meeting new people, or writing letters, or taking his daily walk or honoring *prasadam* or accepting his astonishingly few hours of rest, or dealing with yet another crisis in the one little storefront that constituted his “International Society.”

And if anyone were to ask, in his closet lay a bundle of manuscripts wrapped in saffron cloth—the next canto of the *Bhāgavatam.*

Those were the days when $100 was nearly all ISKCON had. And Śrīla Prabhupāda went up to Long Island with Gargamuni and for $150 bought two old mimeograph machines. (Asking price: $150 apiece.) *Back to Godhead!*

Gargamuni became the first printer, a professor-turned-seeker and a freelance writer for comic books became the first editors, and a hundred copies of BTG, cranked out on the greasy old machines, joined *The East Village Other* and the acid-is-the-answer underground papers in the East Village “head shops.”

Meanwhile, Śrīla Prabhupāda kept typing, and one day Gargamuni, looking in a shop window, saw a dictaphone. He didn’t know what it was, but it looked like something maybe the Swami could use. Gargamuni bought it on the spot. He brought it back and presented it to Śrīla Prabhupāda and started to explain how to use it (the man in the shop had taken an hour or so to explain all those buttons), but somehow—a dictaphone?—Śrīla Prabhupāda already knew. And now Śrīla Prabhupāda began writing his books by dictating.

But who could transcribe? No one. Brahmānanda tried. Hayagrīva tried. Others tried. But the work was tedious, Prabhupāda’s accent was hard to follow, everyone had other things to do. And the work started piling up.

And then Neal showed up, a college student from Antioch on a work-study program, ready to live with a religious group for some time and study it while doing some service.

“Can you type?” a devotee asked him.

Sure.

“We have all this dictation...”

“Oh, I do that sort of thing at college.”

Bingo!

Getting the book edited was another saga. Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote to Rāyarāma, “Howard wants to do it but he has no time to finish it or to type it. You are also engaged in various ways and I do not know how to make it ready. Both you and Howard want to edit it but nobody takes charge to finish the job quickly say within a month.”

But meanwhile he had Brahmānanda looking for a publisher.

Allen Ginsberg’s publishers weren’t interested. Brahmānanda wrote to others, and, publisher after publisher, they all turned him down.

And then one day a letter came from someone at the Macmillan Company, asking to buy some books. Śrīla Prabhupāda told Brahmānanda, “Deliver them personally. And tell him we have a *Bhagavad-gītā* to publish.” Which is how Brahmānanda wound up in an office at Macmillan, face to face with—nobody, a mere clerk in the accounting department, a clerk who had sent that letter only to buy books for himself.

And while Brahmānanda sat there, utterly frustrated, suddenly the chief editor walked in. *The chief editor?* “Mr. Wade,” Brahmānanda said, “we have a *Bhagavad-gītā* to publish.”

“A *Bhagavad-gītā*? We need a *Bhagavad-gītā.* Can you bring it tomorrow? We’ll publish it.” Sight unseen!

Shortly thereafter, Śrīla Prabhupāda had a stroke, and went back to India. But “Although I am practically on the path of death, still I cannot forget about my publications.”

Soon Śrīla Prabhupāda was back in America, and then *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* was in print, then *Teachings of Lord Caitanya.* And the story goes on from there, the story of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s determination and persistence, his vision, his steadfast faith in the words of his spiritual master: “Print books.” It becomes the story of thousands of books sitting on Second Avenue, no one able to sell them, and then the story of a few copies miraculously sold, and then hundreds and thousands and millions. The story becomes a wealth of stories, stories of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers, books in hand, getting books out to people, tens of thousands of people, millions of people, all over the world, the books working their wonder, bringing people to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa and His devotees.

In 1972 Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote to me personally, “These books and magazines are our most important propaganda weapons to defeat the ignorance of maya’s army, and the more we produce such literature and sell them profusely all over the world, the more we shall deliver the world from the suicide course.”

May remembering such instructions from Śrīla Prabhupāda, and remembering his personal example, inspire us always.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jayādvaita Swami

## Kadamba Kānana Swami

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I came to this movement because I was convinced by your books.

It was high in the Himālayas, in the village of the Dalai Lama, where I read your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and understood the difference between the limited happiness in the water in a hoofprint of a calf and the unlimited happiness in an ocean. You explained that the happiness of the impersonalist is actually the sensation of liberation from materiel existence; once achieved, there is no further development, whereas a devotee experiences that the happiness of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa is ever-increasing bliss, even continuing to increase in the spiritual world. That convinced me. I could understand that devotional service was the bigger concept and thus superior and therefore had to be the truth. At that point all the Tibetan Lamas I was observing in that village lost their aura of mysticism, and I decided to follow the *Bhagavad-gītā* and to offer a flower to a picture of Kṛṣṇa I had seen in a local Hindu temple. I sang the *mahā-mantra* out loud while walking there—I didn’t care what people thought, because after all I was 10, 000 km away from home. Just then a boy from my hometown came around the corner. As soon as I followed the direction of the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* I walked the transcendental path, and Kṛṣṇa was reciprocated with my desire to surrender. Now everyone back home knew that I was a Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Then I went to Vṛndāvana. It was evening and I arrived on foot, walking along accompanied by many cows toward the large arched gate that had been constructed over the road in your honor. I knew I had come home.

At that time you were no longer physically present on the planet, and in Vṛndāvana I saw your *samādhi*. It was only a small simple structure, with your picture on a simple *vyāsāsana,* but there was no doubt that you were personally there. And I learned from the devotees to rise early in the morning and to sing for you in your *samādhi* and then circumambulate it three times. It was there that it was deeply impressed upon me that this entire International Society for Krishna Consciousness was created by your great efforts. Then we would daily chant our *japa* in your house, which was carefully kept as it was in your presence. It gave me a further glimpse into your life. In those days your letters had not yet been published, but in Vṛndāvana they had a large collection, and a new letter was placed in front of you on your desk every day for us to read. Those hours and hours in your *samādhi* and in your house thoroughly made me want to be your servant, a “Prabhupāda man.”

As time went on I was given some responsibility in the management of the Krishna-Balarām, temple and eventually my responsibility included making sure that the ringing of the bell was going on. Oh, how important was your bell! Had you not on many occasions personally stressed that it should ring on the hour and half hour and during the *ārati*? Had you not called for secretaries, managers, and all when the bell was not ringing on time? Did you not say that the management of this temple will be judged by the ringing of this bell? I took this holy order on my head and for many years struggled to make that bell ring on time. That bell made me your servant; it made me responsible for just a little bit of your mission. I had the good fortune that many of your intimate servants paid attention to me and taught me what was important to you. I read your books, all the biographies as soon as they came out, your letters and conversations, and I listened to your lectures—all to know more about Your Divine Grace.

Then your glorious disciple Śrīpād Jayādvaita Swami initiated me and showered me with mercy. In due course of time he gave me *sannyāsa* and at some point suggested that I would initiate disciples. My spiritual master is not a man of light decision, and he maintained the idea with conviction. I felt unworthy, but also flattered, and at the same time I knew that I would really have to grow to live up to such an exalted idea. Now, with the blessings of your GBC body, it has come to pass. Now there is no limit to the responsibility I have.

In one of your lectures, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you say that this movement “can turn crows into swans.” At that point all the devotees, even the children, call out “*Jaya!*” In my own lectures I have sometimes introduced a variation on the theme, speaking about a crow who did not actually make the transformation to become such a swan but rather painted his feathers white to pose as one. I now pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you may kindly make some arrangement to turn me into a genuine swan. From my side, I solemnly promise to make a better endeavor than ever to become a better servant of my spiritual master and Your Divine Grace.

*Servant of your servant,*  
Kadamba Kānana Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances. All glories to you on this occasion of your most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā!

On this occasion of the 107th anniversary of your appearance, I would like to submit the following offering at your lotus feet.

While considering the theme for this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I was looking for that event during the last year that made me full of Śrīla Prabhupāda consciousness. I thought of the reunion festival in Mumbai. For me, that was the most intense experience of consciousness of you. I was swimming in an ocean of memories.

The occasion was the 25th anniversary of the opening of the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī temple. There were almost a hundred of your disciples present who had witnessed your transcendental activities in Bombay. Some of your disciples had come to Bombay for the first time since they were here with you. Together, we had almost one weeks’ relaxed time in different settings to reminisce about your glorious pastimes in Bombay.

The devotees were there, but we were missing you. “Where is Śrīla Prabhupāda? Where is Śrīla Prabhupāda? Here is Śyāmasundara! Śrīla Prabhupāda must also be here. Here is Gurudās! Śrīla Prabhupāda must also be here. Here is Brahmānanda! Śrīla Prabhupāda must also be here!”

You were conspicuous by your absence.

Just by seeing my Godbrothers and Godsisters in Bombay, I experienced a flood of memories of you. As each one spoke, it was like going thirty years back into the past. It was just like being with you directly in Bombay. As each devotee began to speak their memories, you appeared.

There were lighter moments full of humor and laughter. And yet there were speakers who choked up and spoke with faltering voices full of tears. The audience was equally surcharged with emotions. Your memories have been deeply carved into their hearts. The love of your followers is genuine and eternal —spiritual in nature. They will carry these memories beyond this life—birth after birth—eternally. We are all eternally indebted to you. May you empower us to do as you did.

All the while, as we were hearing the nectar of your memories, we were being reminded of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī. You had prayed to Them and promised Them, “My dear Rādhā-Rasabihārī, please remain here, and I shall build a beautiful temple for You.” You are so much intimately connected with Kṛṣṇa that one who remembers you remembers Kṛṣṇa.

I remembered that Bombay was my spiritual birthplace. I was conceived at your first Bombay Festival at Cross Maidan in 1971. And I was born in 1972, during the festival the following year at Hare Krishna Land, Juhu, Bombay.

I was a student studying in town called Sangli. I decided to complete my graduation in Bombay. I could have continued in Sangli, but there was a dictation from within to go to Bombay. It was the voice of the Lord within, who was directing me to go and take shelter of your lotus feet in Bombay.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **19.151)**

When I came to your Hare Kṛṣṇa festival in Bombay in 1971, I was confused. I had no sense of direction. I was full of doubts and questions. By hearing from you, and by associating with your devotees, I became free from misconceptions. The dynamic of that association worked like an alum, which settles the sediment at the bottom of a flask, making the water clear at the top. By my coming into contact with you and your devotees, the impurities in my mind began to settle and dissolve. Everything became crystal clear for the first time in my life.

*arjuna uvāca*  
*naṣṭo mohaḥ smṛtir labdhā tvat-prasādān mayācyuta*  
*sthito ’smi gata-sandehaḥ kariṣye vacanaṁ tava*

**“Arjuna said: My dear Kṛṣṇa, O infallible one, my illusion is now gone. I have regained my memory by Your mercy. I am now firm and free from doubt and am prepared to act according to Your instructions.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **18.73)**

I concluded that you were my eternal spiritual master and that the devotees are the kind of people I would like to associate with. By your grace and causeless mercy, I have remained fixed in this determination until this day. There is nothing better to be had.

I walked away from that festival with the treasure of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. I kept chanting for one year, all by myself. I attained the lotus feet of the Lord of my heart, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rasabihārī, the following year at the pandal at Hare Krishna Land.

What can I say? You are a “Goloka man.”

I thank the Lord for your coming into my life and giving me eternal life full of bliss and knowledge. On the occasion of your appearance day, your Vyāsa-pūjā, I would like to express my gratitude from the core of my heart for accepting me as your disciple and engaging me as one of the soldiers of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army.

*Your eternally indebted servant,*  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my humble obeisances! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you taught us that in Bengali cooking sweets come last, only after so many *śuktās, sāgs, subjis,* fried things, rice, dahl, *cāpāṭis,* salads, chutneys, etc. Now, after so many years in ISKCON since I joined in London in 1971, a special sweet has at last come!
