This year, 2003, after the Māyāpur Festival and at the very beginning of our ISKCON 108-nation *mahā-harināma saṅkīrtana* through Kolkata, I was especially fortunate to have my first *darśana* of your birthplace in Tollygunge! Śrīla Prabhupāda’s birthplace *kī jaya*! I took some earth to eat every day from now on. On your actual birthday 1896, your birthplace was on the banks of the “Ādi Gaṅgā,” in a specially constructed temporary maternity room under a jackfruit tree. A young jackfruit tree is also now growing there, on the same property kindly donated to ISKCON by your devoted relatives. The poetic-sounding Ādi Gaṅgā unfortunately now looks like a big sewer. But when we Kolkata devotees build a beautiful memorial temple for you, then perhaps the Ādi Gaṅgā will return in pristine glory to bask at your lotus feet. The *mahā-harināma* was most successful, and we ended up in a big empty sports stadium for a nice *prasādam* feast. If we do this annually, then next year perhaps we can arrange for the stadium to be full of thousands of chanting devotees.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have always been a demonic doubter and have instinctively distrusted organized religions, gurus, and their cult followers. In my heart I knew that only if someone was actually like Lord Jesus would he be able to pass my test. I was a loner and certainly never came to you for your popularity. When you were coming to visit the temple in London in 1971, all your disciples were so excited that you were coming very soon and called your name out in their excitement. I kept my doubts to myself but thought, “What is all this fuss? Let me first see how you look and behave before I get carried away with such sentiments.” Of course, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you passed all my concocted tests, sailing through them as the swanlike *paramahaṁsa*. I could find no fault in you. You confirmed yourself in my eyes as the selfsame divine author whom I had first met privately in the pages of your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. You had managed to reveal to me that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. If we distribute your books widely, you will surely still go on stealing the yearning hearts of all. Śrīla Prabhupāda, is it too late for me to become your full-time book distributor? Sometimes I think it would be the best thing for me to do in my old age.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, one thing that really helped me become convinced about your being Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee was your absorption in and enjoyment of the *kīrtanas*. Even though you were ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya,* you would always quickly get lost in the *kīrtanas* or *bhajans*. In 1970 my truest friend gave me a 6 x 4 photo of you, which was the first photo I ever saw of you. Your hands were upraised and you danced amongst your followers, leaning backward, your shining face in an upward gaze. That picture spoke a million words, even though I rejected it out of false ego. I have never seen that photo again, but would dearly love to. Especially at the Māyāpur Festivals in ’76 and ’77 I was so happy to be with you after the daily morning *guru-pūjā,* before your class, when you would encourage and watch your disciples dance in all kinds of exuberant ways in front of your marble *vyāsāsana* under the Lotus Building. Especially your Guru-kṛpā and Madhudvīṣa Mahārājas would prance and whirl wildly round each other like tribal warriors just to please you. I could see in your face that you really loved the transcendental fun. It was all spontaneous, and at the heart of it was the holy name you had given us and the magnanimous sanction of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava and Lord Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu. At the height of the ecstasy you would pick up the flowers at your feet and throw them out at your buffoonlike Western disciples, who then became even more elated and jubilant. Those flowers were their well-earned reward from your lotus hands. May this sweet memory of you always keep me taking shelter in all the *kīrtanas*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda! My *anarthas* still seem unfathomable, even after so many years of trying to chant properly! Please by your special mercy give Your beloved Lordships Gaura-Nitāi a hint that I should one day be eligible to get a special taste of the holy name to pull me out of my dark entanglement in Māyā’s clutches. Then maybe I will be able to really please you.

*kabe ha’be bolo se-dina āmār*  
*(āmār) aparādha ghuci’, śuddha nāme ruci,*  
*kṛpā-bale ha’be hṛdoye sañcār*

**“When, oh when will that day be mine? When my offenses ceasing, taste for the name increasing, when in my heart will your mercy shine, when, oh when will that day be mine?” (“*****Kabe Ha’be Bolo*****,” by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura )**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, until that time please bless me with the ability to bring many young educated young Africans to your lotus feet.

Your gift I can never repay—but please do not let me go astray.

*Your fallen servant,*  
Mahāviṣṇu Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda came to give the world Kṛṣṇa consciousness. What precisely does Kṛṣṇa consciousness mean? Śrīla Prabhupāda has explained the importance of understanding this:

Therefore, one has to seek out such a bona fide spiritual master and, under him, learn what Kṛṣṇa consciousness is, for Kṛṣṇa consciousness will certainly drive away all nescience, as the sun drives away darkness. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.16, purport\]

To understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we can examine some of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s definitions. Śrīla Prabhupāda originally took the definition of Kṛṣṇa consciousness from a line of a verse by Rāmānanda Rāya, quoted in Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *Padyāvalī: kṛṣṇa-bhakti-rasa-bhāvitā*—absorbed in the mellows of executing devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. (*Padyāvalī* 14, as quoted in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 8.70)

To be in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, one must be absorbed in pure devotional service and experience *rasa,* or transcendental mellows, by thinking of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

In Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *Kṛṣṇa* book he writes: “The art of focusing one’s attention on the Supreme and giving one’s love to Him is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Kṛṣṇa* book, Preface)

Researching Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, I was amazed to see the extent and depth of the definitions given for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* alone we can find a wealth of understanding of these words:

**Constantly thinking of Lord Kṛṣṇa.** “Thus a devotee of Kṛṣṇa can conquer both sleep and ignorance simply by thinking of Kṛṣṇa constantly. This is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness, or *samādhi*.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 1.24, purport)

**For the benefit of Lord Kṛṣṇa.** “Activity in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, or acting for the benefit of Kṛṣṇa without expectation of sense gratification, is the highest transcendental quality of work.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.40, purport)

**Full dependence on the good will of Kṛṣṇa.** “This transcendental position is achieved in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness when one is fully dependent on the good will of Kṛṣṇa.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.45, purport)

**Directly into communion with Kṛṣṇa.** “In Kṛṣṇa consciousness, one comes directly into communion with Kṛṣṇa, and thus all directions from Kṛṣṇa may be understood in that transcendental state.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.53, purport)

**Kṛṣṇa is everything.** “The *sthita-dhīr muni* is always in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for he has exhausted all his business of creative speculation. He is called *praśānta-niḥśeṣa-mano-rathāntara* (*Stotra-ratna* 43), or one who has surpassed the stage of mental speculations and has come to the conclusion that Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, or Vāsudeva, is everything (*vāsudevaḥ sarvam iti sa mahātmā su-durlabhaḥ*). (Bg 2.56purport)

**Knowledge of how to use everything in the service of the Lord.** “On the other hand, a person in Kṛṣṇa consciousness knows how to use everything in the service of the Lord; therefore he does not become a victim of material consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.63, purport)

**Only concerned with the satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa.** “Although the person in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness may apparently be on the sensual plane, because of his being Kṛṣṇa conscious he has no attachment to sensual activities. The Kṛṣṇa conscious person is concerned only with the satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa, and nothing else.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.64, purport)

**Engaging all the senses in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa.** “One can curb the forces of sense gratification only by means of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, or engaging all the senses in the transcendental loving service of the Lord.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.68, purport)

**Perfect stage of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.** “To understand one’s actual position as the eternal servitor of Kṛṣṇa, without falsely claiming this material body to be oneself and without falsely claiming proprietorship over anything in the world, is the perfect stage of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 2.71purport)

**Desiring everything for Kṛṣṇa.** “If, therefore, lust is transformed into love for the Supreme, or transformed into Kṛṣṇa consciousness—or, in other words, desiring everything for Kṛṣṇa—then both lust and wrath can be spiritualized.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 3.37purport)

**Simply meant to serve the Lord.** “The living entities are parts and parcels of the Lord, and therefore they are simply meant to serve the Lord. This consciousness is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 3.41purport)

**Direct connection with the Supreme.** “In Kṛṣṇa consciousness the soul makes direct connection with the Supreme Personality of Godhead.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 3.42purport)

**Deliberate spiritual intelligence.** “Thus knowing oneself to be transcendental to the material senses, mind, and intelligence, O mighty-armed Arjuna, one should steady the mind by deliberate spiritual intelligence \[Kṛṣṇa consciousness\] and thus—by spiritual strength—conquer this insatiable enemy known as lust.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 3.43purport)

**Does everything for Kṛṣṇa.** “This freedom from the bondage of actions is possible only in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, when one is doing everything for Kṛṣṇa. A Kṛṣṇa conscious person acts out of pure love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and therefore he has no attraction for the results of the action.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.20purport)

**Engaged in the satisfaction of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s senses.** “In material consciousness, the senses are engaged in sense gratification, but in Kṛṣṇa consciousness the senses are engaged in the satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa’s senses. Therefore, the Kṛṣṇa conscious person is always free, even though he appears to be engaged in affairs of the senses.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.8–9purport)

**The body is the property of Kṛṣṇa.** “The conclusion is that a person without Kṛṣṇa consciousness acts according to the concept of the material body and senses, but a person in Kṛṣṇa consciousness acts according to the knowledge that the body is the property of Kṛṣṇa and should therefore be engaged in the service of Kṛṣṇa.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.10, purport)

**He himself belongs to Kṛṣṇa.** “‘A person acting in Kṛṣṇa consciousness (or, in other words, in the service of Kṛṣṇa) with his body, mind, intelligence, and words is a liberated person even within the material world, although he may be engaged in many so-called material activities.’ He has no false ego, for he does not believe that he is this material body, or that he possesses the body. He knows that he is not this body and that this body does not belong to him. He himself belongs to Kṛṣṇa, and the body too belongs to Kṛṣṇa. When he applies everything produced of the body, mind, intelligence, words, life, wealth, etc.—whatever he may have within his possession—to Kṛṣṇa’s service, he is at once dovetailed with Kṛṣṇa. He is one with Kṛṣṇa and is devoid of the false ego that leads one to believe that he is the body, etc. This is the perfect stage of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.11, purport)

**Steady consciousness.** “This knowledge elevates him to the station of knowing the complete science of the Absolute Truth, namely Brahman, Paramātmā, and Bhagavān. He thus knows his constitutional position perfectly well, without falsely trying to become one with the Supreme in all respects. This is called Brahman realization, or self-realization. Such steady consciousness is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.20purport)

**Everyone is subordinate to Lord Kṛṣṇa.** “This is the sense of Kṛṣṇa consciousness: Lord Kṛṣṇa is the supreme predominator, and all living entities, including the great demigods, are His subordinates.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 5.29, purport)

**Engaged in the transcendental loving service of the Lord.** “Kṛṣṇa consciousness means, concisely, to be always engaged in the transcendental loving service of the Lord.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 6.10purport)

**Mind always absorbed in Kṛṣṇa’s activities.** “Therefore a person working in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the perfect yogi, because his mind is always absorbed in Kṛṣṇa’s activities (*sa vai manaḥ kṛṣṇa-padāravindayoḥ*). (*Bhagavad-gītā* 6.15purport)

**Serving Kṛṣṇa with purified senses. “**Serving Kṛṣṇa with purified senses is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 6.26purport)

**Nothing can exist without Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa is the Lord of everything.** “A person in Kṛṣṇa consciousness certainly sees Lord Kṛṣṇa everywhere, and he sees everything in Kṛṣṇa. Such a person may appear to see all separate manifestations of the material nature, but in each and every instance he is conscious of Kṛṣṇa, knowing that everything is a manifestation of Kṛṣṇa’s energy. Nothing can exist without Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa is the Lord of everything—this is the basic principle of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 6.30purport)

**Steady concentration of the mind upon Kṛṣṇa.** “At the end of the Sixth Chapter, it has been clearly stated that the steady concentration of the mind upon Kṛṣṇa, or in other words Kṛṣṇa consciousness, is the highest form of all yoga.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 7.1)

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla PrabhupādaPlease accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In glorifying your divine personality on your divine appearance day, I would like to reflect on your example and your presents to me, and also offer a personal prayer to you.

**Your Glorious Example**

Your glorious example of one hundred percent surrender has forever captured my rebellious heart. Who could not be moved by your prayer on board the ship *Jaladuta,* where you totally surrender yourself to the plan of the Lord? “O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.”

This hundred percent surrender to the Lord takes its practical shape in your surrender to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s orders. You are the perfect example of following your own spiritual master.

You said that this determination to follow Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura in unconditional surrender was kindled by reading Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41. For your pleasure I would like to quote this purport.

The best kind of intelligence I can have is intelligence used in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Intelligence is defined as “fixed” when it is intent upon my spiritual master’s instructions, such as chanting the name of Kṛṣṇa, remembering His activities, and performing service to His lotus feet. My spiritual master’s instructions are my *sādhana* and my life, both in the beginning stages of *bhakti* as well in *bhakti’s* perfectional stage. I desire only to follow his instructions. I accept nothing else as my life’s work, even in dreams. Whether I am happy or distressed, whether the material world remains or is destroyed, I don’t care. There is no loss for me. I simply must carry out the orders of my spiritual master. Fixedness upon his orders is determined intelligence and devotional service, and only by being fixed on his orders can such determined intelligence prosper.

Often in your life, when devotees or strangers expressed their admiration for your wonderful achievements, you humbly referred back to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, describing everything that happened simply as his mercy.

Even to this day I remain totally impressed by your deep desire to fulfill the desires of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. It is clear that you have endeavored in a very loving and successful way to fulfill Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s orders and wishes. ISKCON the way you conceived it is a visible manifestation of your burning desire. It is your loving offering to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Nothing less.

**Your Presents**

In my life, you have gifted me with two types of presents, the visible and the internal.

Your visible presents are your temples, with the spiritual programs that you installed within them as the spiritual heart, so to say, and your numerous other projects. And of course, most notably your transcendental books, in which you are still accessible. Also, your most important visible presents for me are your dedicated devotees, who carry the mission of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which you have inculcated into their hearts. I can simply bow down to these presents and take advantage as much as possible.

The other type of present is more difficult to describe because of being more internal. The most important of these presents is the holy name, who is Kṛṣṇa Himself. Outwardly He may appear as a visible present, since when we chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra it is clearly visible to others, both inside and outside the temples.

However, as I chant more and more I begin to get a small understanding of how everything is encapsulated in the holy name. The holy name always unfolds deeper dimensions and leads me on to new realizations.

Another internal present you have given me is the security and the inner peace accompanied by the sweet hope of attaining full Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the future. As soon as I reflect peacefully I can see that you have actually given me the kingdom of God. You came with an invitation:

The Lord says, “Take to My devotional service and come quickly back to Godhead, back home.’ No one should remain in this temporary world, full as it is with miseries. Everyone should attach himself to the bosom of the Supreme Personality of Godhead so that he can be eternally happy. The devotional service of the Supreme Lord is the only process by which all problems of all classes of men can be solved. Everyone should therefore take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and make his life perfect. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 9.33, purport\]

Even though I lament for being still so conditioned and so inexperienced in transcendental matters, I know I am on the path “back to Godhead” if I simply remain your dedicated servant. This is wonderful. My life has become a pilgrimage to an exalted goal. I may be going through rough terrain—at times the feet may sometimes hurt and the belly maybe full of hunger—but there is great hope inside. All these difficulties are worth undertaking because eventually we will reach the destination that will release us from all the difficulties of material life by bestowing the treasures of Kṛṣṇa consciousness upon us.

My life before was different. It was a troublesome journey to the karma stations of life, ending at the place of execution—my sure death. Now it is a pilgrimage back home, back to Godhead. Yes, this pilgrimage will also go through the death station—but we have reason to believe that this death will be a pleasant surprise and that afterwards the journey will simply continue onwards.

**A Prayer**

It is my understanding that when the *ācārya* leaves our external vision, the disciple needs to give him a home in his heart. Otherwise there is separation. Of course, this should have happened already, during your presence, but in my case I was extremely immature and young. I very much wish now to construct a wonderful place for you in my heart. I know that *bhakti* can come only from the pure devotee, who has *bhakti* in his heart. You are that great personality whom Lord Kṛṣṇa has sent to me to rescue me, and you will always remain so.

I pray that I can grow up more and more and follow your glorious example to become a one-hundred-percent surrendered disciple. I pray that all external desires, which give miseries and frustration to me, will soon leave me so that there is only this one desire left. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful, please accept me as I am and give me the strength to become like you want me to become: a faithful, dedicated, and humble servant of yours.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please shower your mercy on this fallen soul. My life in this world is difficult without your confirmed shelter. My character is weak and my mind fickle; please throw the lifeline of your instructions to me and save my life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my time is running out, but I continue to remain distracted. Please use me to do something useful in your glorious mission.

I am standing before the closed door of the palace of *bhakti,* to which you hold the key. Your mercy is my only hope.

Genuinely desiring your shelter,  
*Your most insignificant disciple,*  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Śrīdhar Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble, timid obeisances to your divine self.

What can I say on this auspicious day? Śrīla Prabhupāda, I love you. I love you because you have shown me the path to salvation.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Boy, was I ever in ever in darkness! I shudder to think of my wretched life before you showed me the path and gave me a chance to purify myself. You took so much trouble and time to show me, and others like me, this divine path of *bhakti-yoga*. Therefore I love you.

I love you because you gave me hundreds of books and articles to destroy my dense ignorance with the torchlight of knowledge. You encouraged me to read them, follow them, and distribute them. Your mercy is boundless.

I love you because you revealed your ecstasy while singing *bhajans* and in leading *kīrtanas.* You attracted so many wonderful musicians and trained them in *kīrtana* for my pleasure and purification (listening to Kṛṣṇa glorified).

I love you because you gave me so many of your recorded lectures so I can listen every morning to at least one or two. In this way you are always with me. Since you left in ’77, I have found you again. Please, please don’t ever leave me now.

I love you because you opened and are opening so many temples where I can visit and remember you, melt before the beautiful Deities, and relish the association of dancing enthusiastic devotees (your devotees all).

I love you because you gave me Jayānanda (as my *vartma-pradarśaka*). You gave me Goswami Mahārāja, my dear friend and mentor. You gave me so many others who continue to inspire me. Especially you gave me the wonderful second-generation devotees who are so humble to tolerate our (the first generation’s) mistakes. Having no chance to meet you incarnate, they are pure in their desire to know you. It is my only joy to help them know you. It is greater than any pleasure I can dream.

I love you because you gave me Rādhā-Rasabihārī, who are infinitely merciful. They are so kind to me I sometimes become stunned when I think of my good fortune. You introduced me to Their Lordships. You have kept me at Their lotus feet for so many years now. I don’t know what is your magic. I want to learn how to be an acceptable guru, but your magic is too far beyond my comprehension.

Now you have given me Narasiṁhadeva. I love my Deities. My personal *pūjā*. I didn’t know this pleasure before you. Your mercy is all I am made of.

Especially you suffered in an extension of your life when you first came to America. You said you should have died but Kṛṣṇa extended your visa. I see this as perhaps simply a *līlā* to help people like me. Therefore I go on in love with you and in your love for me. It is really a struggle for me, but I feel I can do anything when I love you.

*Your loving servant,*  
Śrīdhar Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Somehow you have done it again, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Just when I was feeling secure in my position, smug in my conviction that I was one of the few “chosen ones” who could be confident that I had your mercy, you have very forcefully obliged me to reevaluate myself. Why is it that I am so ungrateful? Why can’t I properly appreciate the wonderful gift you have given me? Why do I, again and again, trouble you to become personally involved in the thankless task of trying to improve my character? You sometimes said the effort was “like trying to wash coal.”

I must confess that in this reevaluation process you have inaugurated in me, I have uncovered a grand storehouse of defects. Somehow I always knew that these defects were there, but my mind had been assuring me that because I was one of your important senior disciples these defects were not really significant. Well, now by your unalloyed kindness you have shown me very clearly just how displeased you are with my inability—or better to say, my refusal—to rid myself or *all* the dirty things in my heart.

I am sorry that I have demonstrated so clearly to you, and to myself, and to all objective observers, that I am, after all these years, still not a pure devotee of our sweet Lord, Śrī Krsna. How disappointed you must be with me! I really don’t know what to do except cry. Cry out of agony that I have failed you so blatantly, cry out of gratitude that with all my defects you still have not forsaken me, cry to continue to be allowed to render some insignificant service to you.

I could say, “Now I promise to do better.” I guess I should say like that, but I feel that these words must seem very hollow to you.

Therefore allow me to close by saying that you are really an amazing person, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We never had any idea that such a wonderful person as you could be found anywhere. We worship you with great awe and reverence, and we want the whole world to begin to have the opportunity to realize your extraordinary transcendental personality.

*Your troublesome servant,*  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umāpati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**The Perfection That Fulfills All Desires**

“My only wish is to have my consciousness purified by the words emanating from his lotus mouth. Attachment to his lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires.”

Every morning we offer *ārati* to Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we sing these beautiful words. We meditate on their meaning, and they run through our heads during the day, but do we ever stop and wonder whether anyone believes them?

Now maybe it is because I am active on the Internet that I have to deal with so many people who call themselves devotees but refuse to purify their consciousness with the words emanating from the lotus mouth of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

“But I still accept Śrīla Prabhupāda as my spiritual father,” they say, or “He was a great person.” All true of course, but the essence is missing. After all, we do not need absolute faith in a father or a great person.

So I began to wonder how people can come so close to Kṛṣṇa consciousness but miss the central point: surrender to the spiritual master.

*tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā*  
*upadekṣyanti te jñānaṁ jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

**“Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized souls can impart knowledge unto you because they have seen the truth.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **4.34)**

“Yes,” they say, “Śrīla Prabhupāda is a great self-realized soul, but there are some things he does not understand about the material world.”

Understand the material world? Lord Kṛṣṇa tells us how. The material world, the Lord says, is like a great banyan tree.

*na rūpam asyeha tathopalabhyate*  
*nānto na cādir na ca sampratiṣṭhā*  
*aśvattham enaṁ su-virūḍha-mūlam*  
*asaṅga-śastreṇa dṛḍhena chittvā*

**“The real form of this tree cannot be perceived in this world. No one can understand where it ends, where it begins, or where its foundation is. But with determination one must cut down this strongly rooted tree with the weapon of detachment.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **15.3)**

And Lord Kṛṣṇa adds that only after we cut down this tree can we go back to Godhead:

*tataḥ padaṁ tat parimārgitavyaṁ*  
*yasmin gatā na nivartanti bhūyaḥ*  
*tam eva cādyaṁ puruṣaṁ prapadye*  
*yataḥ pravṛttiḥ prasṛtā purāṇī*

**“Thereafter, one must seek that place from which, having gone, one never returns, and there surrender to that Supreme Personality of Godhead from whom everything began and from whom everything has extended since time immemorial.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **15.4)**

But what does it mean to cut down the tree? Surely it does not mean that we must destroy the material world. Many great souls have already cut down the tree, but the material world is still here. It can only mean that we must destroy our material conceptions. *Bhagavad-gītā* tells us to do it by cutting our material attachments, and Śrīla Prabhupāda helps us:

Therefore one must learn detachment by discussion of spiritual science based on authoritative scriptures, and one must hear from persons who are actually in knowledge. \[purport to the above\]

Simple enough, but there is a catch: you have to accept the scriptures and the knowledgeable persons as authorities. And here is where people back down.

Again, why? Another passage comes to mind:

By Him even the great sages and demigods are placed into illusion, as one is bewildered by the illusory representations of water seen in fire, or land seen on water. Only because of Him do the material universes, temporarily manifested by the reactions of the three modes of nature, appear factual, although they are unreal. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.1\]

Here is the problem, and it affects even great sages and demigods: Something false looks real.

How can this be? What about that beautiful girl outside my window? Is she not real? Yes, the girl is real, but the idea that she is meant for my enjoyment is false, although it may seem real to me. The idea that she will be free if she has many lovers is also false, though it is a social concept that seems real to many people today, including some who are approaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

It is hard to admit that something I see as real can be false, that I can be so misled. And the seemingly real solution? Why, it must be Śrīla Prabhupāda who is misled...but only about the material world, of course. He can see clearly into the spiritual world, infinitely far away, but he cannot see what is happening right next to him. In short, they say Śrīla Prabhupāda is still bewildered by the banyan tree even though he has cut it down.

An absurd conclusion, but it seems real to many. If you don’t believe me, I can send you some of the e-mails I get. And they are all too real.

Now let us ask ourselves: Are we preaching against this false notion? Are we making sure people understand that every word coming from the lotus mouth of Śrīla Prabhupāda is true and will purify our consciousness? When we tell stories about Śrīla Prabhupāda, do we make people understand that he is not just a spiritual father or a great teacher or a spiritual pioneer? He is Prabhupāda, the master at whose feet other masters sit. Attachment to his lotus feet will fulfill all our desires, but we cannot reach this attachment if we stay attached to something false.

Can there be a greater fool than one who comes so close to going back to Godhead but stays in the material world because he or she cannot accept the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda?

“Of course I want to go back to the spiritual world,” they say, but how will they do it without Śrīla Prabhupāda? Attachment to his lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

It seems such a short time on one hand, while eternity on the other, that you appeared in my world and so dramatically interrupted my mundane routine to bring new life to every aspect of my existence. Leaving everything I knew behind to serve your mission, guided by your vision, in serving your *vapu* (form)—a whole new realm of beauty and charm revealed itself from behind the veil of what we previously considered to be real.

By the touch of your mercy and your vision, everything shifted. No longer was life an endless maze but a clear path back to home. No longer a world of hostility and strangers, but a field in need of cultivation. You touched our lives with a vitality so real, so fine, that the dull dead existence that was all we previously knew transformed into a place where all speech resembled a song, all motion a dance, and all was inspired and orchestrated by the holy name, nondifferent from the flute song of Kṛṣṇa.

How you nurtured the naive mentality we mixed into our spiritual lives! With such patient determination you prepared us for the inevitable. Yet, we were so accustomed to denying and evading the truth, we never really deeply and properly accepted or conceived of the way your mercy would expand in our lives, our hearts, and our world.

Traumatic indeed was the day the news reverberated through the world that you had returned home, leaving your inheritance in the hands of your children and grandchildren. What lessons awaited us in maturing!

Being especially slow in the grieving process, it has taken me many years and lots of tears to finally close the only happy chapter in life I had ever known, to soberly open the next. Separation from Kṛṣṇa, we say, is the highest ecstasy, yet I could never relate to it.

Separation from our beloved Gurudeva was too painful. Until now, I never really accepted it.

Now in retrospect I see how many offshoots of the branch of Lord Caitanya’s tree are but various manifestations of devotees’ attempts to come to grips with your disappearance pastime. Understanding this, I feel more compassion for them by your grace. I can now relate better to their condition.

Indeed, during those years you physically walked and talked with us, I failed to enter deeply and fully into communion with you. You were, to me, the one going ahead, leading the way, waiting patiently for us to learn to follow you properly.

As fulfilling as those years were, and remain to be, we needed to enter the deeper realm of loving service in separation, to associate with you in the eternal holy *dhāma,* which lies both far above the world we know, and deeper within our own hearts.

In that self-luminous spiritual reality, darkness has no access, even death cannot enter.

Between that land of the living and this mundane world where everything must die, there is a connection. This deeper and more substantial reality, jewels of Lord Caitanya’s teachings—I would never have grasped or attempted without your most personal and merciful intervention.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for weaning your infant followers from the sentimental feelings and emotions we held on to before. In letting go of our clinging grip of your mortal form, you have led us into a deeper communion. From that seed you planted, a sweet and fragrant flower has sprouted. As it grows, the lotus of the *dhāma* will support the kind of community for which our hearts forever yearn. With this insight, gleaned from the nurturing instructions you offered me this year, I have come to accept your disappearance pastime as a means to appreciate your appearance day with the joy born of deeper communion with you.

And now for the first time, I can wholeheartedly say —Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your continuing love and instructions and appearance days, even in the deepest realm of separation.

*Your servant,*  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most sincere obeisances so far, this time at your merciful lotus feet.

It is said that on the day of your appearance you descended with more mercy than Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Therefore devotees celebrate your Vyāsa-pūjā to rejuvenate and enrich their spiritual lives by begging more of that extraordinary mercy from you. They hope that the spirit of preaching may remain throughout the year, and your appearance time nourishes that hope like a waxing moon. It is a period of nourishment and growth. And as healing herbs get nourishment from increasing moonshine, so the devotees’ hope for increased preaching spirit is nourished at this time.

Then later in the year the devotees celebrate your divine disappearance in the mood of separation and lamentation, looking for your kind mercy more intensely in your absence. They hope that their devotional service may survive and they may remain steady. But your disappearance time is a period of wilting and drying up, like the period of the waning moon. Just as such a time dehydrates plants, your disappearance time drains out all energy and enthusiasm for performing dynamic devotional service. Then the devotees have no other shelter than your divine Bhaktivedanta purports for their rescue.

If I cultivate full and exclusive faith in your words, which are equally as powerful as the pure holy name, I will definitely cross over this miserable material world.

What I mean to say, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that I am getting old and am facing ups and downs in my enthusiasm to preach due to Kali’s influence. While your teachings are very transparent and are transcendental to Kali’s influence, nonetheless I seem to be more susceptible to this influence in your physical absence. And as time passes I am becoming more frustrated. But what can I do except take shelter of your instruction to cooperate and tolerate any inconvenience for your pleasure?

When I read your biography, which shows your unwavering dedication to transcendental truth by your taking absolute shelter of the holy name while tolerating millions of times more inconveniences than what I am experiencing, I drop the idea of complaining to you. Thus I feel my enthusiasm rejuvenated simply by my thinking of you. Please forgive my mental offenses. I selfishly beg most of your mercy on this auspicious day of your divine appearance, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your mercy-beggar disciple,*  
Vedavyāsapriya Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Agartala

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet.

I am the temple president of Agartala (Tripura), and I speak on behalf of all the devotees of the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Temple of ISKCON in Agartala.

When I started my devotional service in this temple, about seven years ago, I felt like I was in the desert. But after the installation of your *mūrti,* I felt at home. Your wonderful presence in the temple has inspired me and all the devotees to better perform our devotional service. It gave us the opportunity to introduce you to all people as the real, authentic, bona fide spiritual master. By your mercy we also got the opportunity to purify ourselves through serving your lotus feet.

Your Divine Grace has taught us how to increase our love for Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and there are no words sufficient to thank you appropriately. You have given us the formula for perfect happiness through your constant recommendation to chant the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Through your perfect teaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you have introduced us poor fallen souls to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Your spiritual realizations and your knowledge are inconceivable, and the work you have done for us is incredible.

Personally, managing a small temple with about ten devotees, I have experienced many difficulties; therefore I can understand your greatness, just as a son can understand the sacrifices of the father when he also becomes a father. Particularly in India a great deal of effort must be expended to dispel spiritual misconceptions, because most of the people have been misguided by several famous false gurus.

Your purity, your lucidity, and your perfect knowledge are opening our minds so that we may in the future become qualified to pick people up from the darkness of ignorance. But to attain that level of purity we need to undergo more purification. Even after twenty years of service in ISKCON I have realized that my mind is not pure enough, and I pray to your lotus feet to help me remove all my material attachments. Please help me become more humble and totally surrendered to Guru and Gaurāṅga. I have realized how Śrī Guru and Śrī Kṛṣṇa are merciful to me, because they are engaging me in devotional service even with all my faults. Therefore I am inspired to reciprocate with better service, but still I am handicapped by my ignorance and mistakes.

You have told us, “Books are the basis, purity is the strength, preaching is the essence, and utility is the principle.” Please, our beloved spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda, help us follow that dictum fully, and help us always remember that we are in this body and on this planet only temporarily and that we are not this body but pure spiritual souls. Thank you, thank you, thank you, a thousand million times thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your devotees in the Agartala* yātrā, *Tripura, India.*  
(written by Premadatta dāsa)

## Amravati

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

By your causeless mercy and that of your dear disciple Lokanāth Swami Mahārāja, we now have a beautiful ISKCON Temple in Amravati, with stunningly beautiful Deities of Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, we feel joy to offer at your lotus feet a small token of our love and appreciation for you. We have acquired five acres of land near Amravati, at a place called Kondinyapur, which is the birthplace of Śrīmatī Rukmiṇī-devī. This has been possible only because of your mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have done so much for us and the world at large that we will never ever be able to repay you, but we are making some small endeavors to please you somehow. Please accept our humble gift to you this day.

We beg you to bless us so that we can utilize the new land to build a nice temple project that will cultivate devotees and help spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the region.

*Your grand-disciple servants at ISKCON Amravati, India.*

## Arizona

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our Commitment to You**

We are on a path, you’ll be happy to know,  
To expand, revive, build strength, and grow.  
To broaden our impact and give everyone hope,  
To present *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* on a much wider scope.  
Education and training for all under our care,  
As well as ourselves, or it wouldn’t be fair.  
Come up to the mark, let there be no doubt,  
It’s worth the sacrifice, kindly stick it out.  
Before it’s too late, let’s set things in place,  
Before we get old and our brain starts to waste.  
Let us all become empowered and work as a team,  
From ladies to lads, it’s no longer just a dream.  
Training is missing from the head down to the feet,  
Let’s educate our devotees, not let them deplete.  
Providing for their future and becoming their friend,  
A strong community of preachers we’ll have in the end.  
More books will go out in a manner most pleasing,  
The public’s respect we will be achieving.  
Variegated outreach in all types of venues,  
From yoga to courses to Govinda’s menus.  
Putting old wine in new bottles and attracting that way,  
With proper guidance and caring, they may choose to stay.  
Your purest of teachings we won’t water down,  
The chanting of *harinām* shall be heard in every town.  
Assessment and critique are sometimes missing on top,  
But if we’re your humble servants all lethargy will stop.  
Times are changing for the good and it’s certainly refreshing,  
With nothing left to hide we now ask for your blessings.  
You’re here in our midst, seeing all from above,  
Giving mercy and guidance and most of all love.  
You expect the most from us and we’re ready to give,  
That’s our only goal, the only reason we live.  
We are your fallen servants, but we try nonetheless,  
For outside of your service there is no happiness.  
There’s great joy to be found with this family we’re in;  
Our love is shown to you when we stay through thick and thin.

*Your servants in Arizona,*  
(written by Sandāmiṇī-devī dāsī)

## Austin

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to your Divine Grace!

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
who are so dear  
to their Lordships  
Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara,  
How unfortunate we are  
to have never had the chance  
to see Lord Caitanya  
and you, His dear friend, chant and dance!  
How unfortunate we are  
to have lost the anxiety  
to receive you in person,  
To have never had the ecstasy  
of bathing your lotus feet,  
To have never had the satisfaction  
of tasting the remnants  
from your own hands!  
We are most fortunate, though,  
to have met your dear devotees,  
who tell us about you and  
your wonderful qualities.  
We are most fortunate  
to hear from them  
your personal instructions  
and to see manifest in them  
your wonderful pastimes.  
We are blessed to remember  
in separation  
your causeless compassion,  
saving the most fallen  
from utter destruction,  
generation after generation.

O most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we lack the purity and the resolve to give our best to you. But we love you and know that by taking shelter of all your instructions, someday we may be rid of all desires but the desire to satisfy you in full through your sweet and caring devotees.

You say in the Fourth Canto of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that we should serve the spiritual master in full surrender with the motive to satisfy the supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa. Please give us the intelligence and strength to understand and execute your desire to please the Lord in a way as to satisfy you in full. If you please, please destroy all our ignorance so we can see you face to face every moment.

In spite of all our disqualifications, we offer our very selves at your lotus feet and “with all the humility at our command” request you to accept us and make us dance as you please. Hare Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please accept this humble offering, for without your saving glance we don’t stand any chance.

*Your servants in Austin, Texas, USA.*  
(written by Śacīdulāl dāsa)

## Baltimore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. On this most blessed day of your divine appearance, what can we who are most fallen offer you, the most confidential servitor of the Lord? We have no spiritual or material qualifications of any substantial value. Rather, it is *you* who have given *us* everything:

The *saṅkīrtana* of the transcendental holy name of the Lord as given by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which is the *yuga-dharma* and through which we can genuinely taste the nectar of love of God and finally go back to Godhead.

The most exalted and exquisite philosophy of the original all-attractive Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who is the Absolute Truth and cause of all causes and yet revels in eternal pastimes of intimate love with His devotees in the spiritual world.

Your powerful books, through which we can remain absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness ourselves and which we can distribute to all the other fallen souls to get your association and receive the seed of devotion.

Wonderful *prasādam,* by which we can liberate ourselves and create “*prasadam* bodies,” as well as purify the hearts of the masses of conditioned souls (you once called Kṛṣṇa consciousness the “kitchen religion”).

Beautiful Deities and temples, wherein we can worship the *arcā-vigraha* form of the Lord, thus seeing Śrī Kṛṣṇa face to face and beginning our practice of regulated service.

Ecstatic festivals to celebrate the divine appearance and activities of the Lord and His pure devotees.

The bona fide disciplic succession of *ācāryas* in the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya, which you represented with relentless enthusiasm and conviction, and through which you were empowered to preach according to time, place, and circumstance.

The regulative principles of freedom (no meat-eating, no intoxication, no illicit sex, and no gambling), by which we can be freed from sinful activities and their consequences.

Although there is much more that you have given us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, taken as a whole all you have given is your very self, your own heart, which is nondifferent from Śrī Kṛṣṇa because of your intense love for Him. And just as one worships the Ganges by offering Ganges water in return, we can please you only by offering what you have given us back to you with love and devotion. This is our only qualification.

On this most auspicious day let us all resolve to endeavor to make this offering to you more and more pleasing with each passing day of our lives and to finally offer you a world where all the conditioned souls recognize the transcendental gifts you have bestowed on humankind and make their way back home, back to Godhead.

*Your servants at ISKCON Baltimore, Maryland, USA.*  
(written by Sunanda dasa)

## Beed

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga! All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day!

Beed is a small city in the state of Maharashtra, between Solapur and Nagpur, with a population of 285,000. *Beed,* an Arabic word, means “water.” This place was previously flooded with water, but now the few devotees here are trying to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the form of the holy names, books, and *prasādam,* to pour some drops of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy on this small city of Beed.

Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda are very beautiful Deities. Their body looks like pure milk. Thousands and thousands of visitors are attracted by their beauty. When we are in front of the Deities, we do not want to move, and again and again we want to see Their beautiful forms daily. During the feast programs thousands of people come to get their *darśana*.

This temple was constructed and donated to ISKCON by Śrīmān Ramesh Sharda. His close and intimate friend Śrīmān Shyam Varma assisted him. They are both very kind-hearted and generous. They have given Kṛṣṇa a place to reside, and Kṛṣṇa will bless them one day by giving them residence in the spiritual word.

The temple has ten resident *brahmacārīs,* and the new team of devotees are trying to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda to the best of their ability.

The temple-opening festival was very successful. His Holiness Lokanāth Mahārāja, His Grace Rādhā-kuṇḍa Prabhu, and His Grace Abhinanda Prabhu attended the festival. We had devotees coming from ten different directions—twenty buses full of devotees came from different parts of Maharashtra, and over 20,000 people attended the festival.

A huge Śobha-yātrā procession was performed, with several horses, chariots, bands. During the ceremony small Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda were carried on a small but beautifully decorated cart; there were scout boys and hundreds of ladies carrying sacred pots, and flags of various colors flew. A young boy and girl dressed up as Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa sat on a chariot and depicted the pastimes of the Lord. Rangolis decorated the roads with various auspicious graffiti. A small Ratha-yātrā, with Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā in a mini-cart, was followed by a huge *harināma-saṅkīrtana* party, and all the devotees enthusiastically danced. The procession ended after four hours with colorful fireworks, and then *prasādam* was distributed to everyone without discrimination.

The *kalaśa-sthāpana*, *mahā-yajña,* and *mahā-abhiśeka* took place early in the morning. During the *mahā-ārati* everybody was eager to see their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda in their beautiful new dress. We felt that all the guests who got *darśana* were fully satisfied.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us and give us sincerity and intelligence to keep going in our spiritual duties so that Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda may also bless us.

*Your servants in Beed, Maharashtra, India.*

## Belfast

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Your preaching mission to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world has been effective and successful. The proof is that, although you never visited Ireland, nevertheless there is a Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa temple here in Belfast. The *pūjā* is going on steadily, day by day and year by year. Local men and women are becoming serious in their spiritual practices and are taking Vaiṣṇava initiation. Books are being distributed, and the holy names of Kṛṣṇa are heard on the streets of the city center. Many souls are receiving *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* at our temple and at outreach programs.

Our temple is only one among many the world over that has been established by your sincere followers. All of them testify to your matchless spiritual potency, which flows down through the *paramparā* and the lotus feet of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

But for us, this temple in Belfast is our special shelter. We offer you our deepest and most heartfelt thanks for engaging us as your workers, your servants.

Thank you for giving us Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava, whose divine presence is our constant inspiration to continue, despite obstacles, and to co-operate despite our tendency to disunite.

Your desire that the holy name be chanted in every town and village was accompanied by a desire that Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa be worshiped in the prominent cities of the Western world, so that fallen souls could begin to understand Kṛṣṇa and His beauty and be inspired to give up their degraded ways.

Your Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhava here in Belfast are your very special mercy to us and all the people of this city. Each day they are radiating so much spiritual benevolence and opulence, way beyond our comprehension. We are extremely fortunate. We bow down at your lotus feet, summoning all the gratitude we have, and offer it to you. You have saved us and given us true spiritual life.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

We beg to remain,  
*Your servants at ISKCON Belfast, Northern Ireland.*

## Bhakti-latā Purī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances to the golden dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you!

I always thank you for all the surrender you showed during your whole life in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I have been one of the fortunate receivers of your infinite and causeless mercy.

And though I must admit that as I have encountered the many opportunities you have presented to me for devotional service, the shoes you intended for me to wear have been somewhat bigger than my possibilities could ever reach to, these challenges I have been glad to face, for they substitute for so many other, materialistic challenges, which are not worthy to be mentioned.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you define and spread it is the best for Kṛṣṇa and therefore for everyone.

I also wanted to tell you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I am trying to present in the “tray of my life” a particular offering to you, namely, a Vaiṣṇava couple, my wife and I, who would become a real Vaiṣṇava family if you bless us so we could become parents. I am hopeful that as a Kṛṣṇa conscious family we could better help this small farm community become established.

Here in Bhakti-latā Purī we are trying to offer devotees a worthy and exciting Kṛṣṇa conscious rural lifestyle. Many of your sons and daughters are helping us. Today, I also want to thank you for being the personified inspiration for Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami, who tirelessly serves your mission and inspires others to do likewise.

I aspire to at least not present any hindrance to your plans, although ultimately nothing can be a real hindrance to your universal mission.

*Your servants at Bhakti-latā Purī, the ISKCON farm community near Buenos Aires, Argentina.*  
(written by Daityeśa dāsa)

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

Year after year we come together on this day and try to glorify you. We should be able to glorify you more and more every year. We should appreciate more and more what we have received from you. One of your prominent disciples, Śivarāma Swami, compiled a book called *Śikṣā-guru,* in which he is seriously analyzes your unique position. From his objective analysis we have come to realize that what you have done is unparalleled.

Only now do we understand a little of what it meant physically for you to go to America. How hopeless it must have seemed to launch such a risky venture! By accepting this challenge you gave an example of how we should follow the order of the spiritual master and make it our heart and soul. You not only started something glorious, but you determinedly built the ISKCON institution up over years for the benefit of all future Vaiṣṇavas. You created the opportunity for millions of conditioned souls to receive the mercy of Lord Caitanya.

Recently I asked the devotees in a class if they wanted to meet you. All of them raised their hands. Though we can not physically meet you now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can meet you at any time because in the form of your *mūrtis* and your pictures you are always giving your personal *darśana,* and more importantly your are always speaking to us and teaching us through your books. If we cannot appreciate this, how could we appreciate your personal presence? What does it mean to appreciate what we have received from you? At least it means to take care of this legacy, to make sure that it remains as pure and strong as when you gave it to us. It is our duty to take care of everything you have given, are giving, and will give us in the future. This means first of all maintaining the spiritual health of the community of the devotees, because if the devotees are spiritually healthy and strong, then everything we need will be provided, and anything can happen. If not, than we have only empty buildings in an empty institution. You personally gave us an example of how we should take care of the devotees, cooperate with the devotees, and follow the devotees. You knew that especially in this age it would not be easy. But still it is our duty to make a serious effort every year, every day, and every hour.

We beg for your special and causeless mercy so that the devotees in this small community will be worthy members of the wonderful family you have created.  
*Your insignificant servants in Budapest, Hungary.*

## Buenos Aires

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, the founder/*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā!

On this most auspicious day, in which humanity became so fortunate due to your transcendental appearance—the appearance of a devotee in the highest *paramahaṁsa* stage—I, your grand-disciple, will attempt to offer homage to you, representing the temple of Nava Sutala Loka Mandir, in Buenos Aires, Argentina.

A hundred thousand million thanks for your untiring dedication to the mission of His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, and for your majestic success in accomplishing that mission, namely the spreading of the *saṅkīrtana* movement in the West and throughout the whole world. *Golokera prema-dhana, harināma-saṅkīrtana:* this *saṅkīrtana* movement comes directly from Goloka Vṛndāvana, just as you did, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a brilliant citizen of the spiritual world.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.11says:

*tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ’ṅkitāni yat*  
*śṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ*

**“That literature which is full of descriptions of the transcendental glories of the name, fame, forms, pastimes, etc., of the unlimited Supreme Lord is a different creation, full of transcendental words directed toward bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization. Such transcendental literatures, even though imperfectly composed, are heard, sung and accepted by purified men who are thoroughly honest.”**

This verse predicts your advent and the matchless gift you left us in the form of your purports full of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.6says:

*sa vai puṁsāṁ paro dharmo yato bhaktir adhokṣaje*  
*ahaituky apratihatā yayātmā suprasīdati*

**“The supreme occupation (*****dharma*****) for all humanity is that by which men can attain to loving devotional service unto the transcendent Lord. Such devotional service must be unmotivated and uninterrupted in order to completely satisfy the self.”**

In the purport you reveal the exclusiveness and supremacy of devotional service, which gives one real life and pleases one to total satisfaction.

Praying unto your divine lotus feet, all the devotees and aspiring devotees in this recondite place at the southern extreme of South America, far away from Māyāpur *dhāma*, ask that we may, through your empowerment, become Kṛṣṇa conscious and be flooded with the *prema-bhakti* you left to us as our inheritance.

All glories, all glories, all glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the greatest Vaiṣṇava, who made your advent in this obscure Kali-yuga to give us the nectar we are always anxious for, pure love for Kṛṣṇa!

*Your insignificant servants in Buenos Aires, Argentina.*  
(written by Jagad Bandhu dāsa)

## Canberra

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our Prabhupāda chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa,  
And the auspicious moon rose for us all.  
Our Prabhupāda chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa,  
And the Lord revealed himself to us all in His name.  
Our Prabhupāda chanted Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa,  
And we saw humility, tolerance, and self-effacement.  
Our Prabhupāda chanted Hare Hare,  
And we developed the desire to serve life after life.  
Our Prabhupāda chanted Hare Rāma,  
And we finally realized it: “I am fallen.”  
Our Prabhupāda chanted Hare Rāma,  
And we longed for the day we would cry like him for Kṛṣṇa.  
Our Prabhupāda chanted Rāma Rāma,  
And nothing seemed to make any sense without Kṛṣṇa.  
Our Prabhupāda chanted Hare Hare,  
And Kṛṣṇa became the Lord of our lives.

How lucky we are that he came into our lives and chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa!

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants in ISKCON Canberra, Australia.*

## Chicago

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

To our most dear spiritual master, Paramahaṁsa Ṭhākura Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*  
*tāhā pravartāilā tumi,—ei ta ‘pramāṇa’*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti dhara tumi,—ithe nāhi āna*

**“Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement. You have spread the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore, it is evident that you have been empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. There is no doubt about it.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya-līlā*** **7.11–12).**

We humbly borrow these words spoken by Vallabha Bhaṭṭa to Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself over five hundred years ago as part of our offering, because they also perfectly apply to Your Divine Grace.

Similarly, we would like to borrow from your own words in the purport to same passage (verse 12), wherein you explain the attributes of the most advanced devotee. We can understand through your character and accomplishments that you embody all these attributes. You may be referred to as *paramahaṁsa-ṭhākura,* the most advanced devotee, who, as described, acts as *ācārya,* directly representing Lord Kṛṣṇa by spreading His name and fame.

You are an incarnation of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, and it also can be said that you are personally embracing Lord Kṛṣṇa. You are the spiritual master of the entire world, the true *ācārya* who presents Kṛṣṇa to everyone. You are the spiritual master of all the *varṇas* and all the *āśramas,* a fact clearly revealed in the same purport.

On this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we would like to offer you a simple *aṣṭaka,* eight verses in praise of your wonderful accomplishments and your all-important instructions to us, the fallen conditioned souls who have been so kindly blessed with the opportunity to hear from you. These verses are based on your own words, words we have come to know as our only shelter, words you pour like incessant rain upon the blazing material fire within our hearts. We have no words of our own, except to say thank you for all you have done, and to please accept this humble offering.

**Eight Verses in Praise of the Accomplishments and Instructions of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda**

The very pleasing and peaceful atmosphere one can find within  
The International Society for Krishna Consciousness  
is the result of the pure chanting of the founder-*ācārya,*  
His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda’s aim is to provide human society  
with a common platform of clear theistic knowledge and practice.

He has broadcast all over the world the essence of all faiths,  
the eternal wisdom of the Vaiṣṇava disciplic succession.  
I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Through the congregational chanting of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s holy names,  
the dormant love of God in every soul can be awakened.  
This *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu  
has been delivered to every country by Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust is the backbone of the movement.  
The printing and the distribution of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books  
directly bring all who are involved to the platform of hearing  
the highest message chanted from the lips of the pure devotee.

I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
The one hundred eight temples established by Śrīla Prabhupāda  
conduct devotional service continuously day and night.  
By our worshiping Lord Kṛṣṇa according to the proper standards,  
the teachings of the six Gosvāmīs are developing nicely.

I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
By protecting the cows and the *brāhmaṇas* on self-sufficient farms,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda has taught faith in Lord Kṛṣṇa as the center.  
The disciples live in Vaikuṇṭha, not the material world—  
a harmonious life according to the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda has built magnificent centers in the *dhāmas*  
for all to come and further their progress in devotional life.  
These are places for the whole planet to gather under one roof  
and deeply discuss the pastimes of the Lord and His devotees.

I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Faithful followers of Śrīla Prabhupāda keep the principles  
of rising early every day and chanting sixteen rounds on beads.  
They remain free of material contamination and thus  
make visible progress in going back home, back to Godhead.

I offer my most respectful and most grateful obeisances  
unto the shelter of my soul, the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

With humble submission to your instructions and increasing enthusiasm to serve your lotus feet,  
*Your disciples and grand-disciples at New Seva-kuñja, ISKCON’s Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī Mandir, in Chicago, Illinois, USA.*

## Coimbatore and Kerala

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we hold you in awe and reverence every day as we read your books or preach about your wonderful movement. Thinking of you is so soothing to the subtle body, which is torn with various anxieties. Your fatherly figure, words, and shelter are giving us genuine comfort from the vicissitudes of this material world. This is real shelter. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for sheltering us and giving yourself to this world. We can understand that the only way we can repay you is by following your instructions carefully and getting more souls into the house that you have built for us.

Here in Coimbatore, Tamil Nadu, we are endeavoring to satisfy you in small measures, through baby steps. Although considering the massive projects some of your followers have launched elsewhere our efforts could be seen as insignificant, we are biting only as much as we can chew.

Your wonderful devotees here serve Their Lordships Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā-devī by preaching in colleges, distributing your books, and conducting courses and classes through The Bhaktivedanta Academy for corporations, schools, and other institutions. The congregation is increasing very fast through programs in houses and important local venues.

The honorable Prime Minister of India was here with us for the inauguration of our facility and praised Your Divine Grace’s contribution. It was a historical moment. We have since stepped up our book distribution, college preaching, and other programs as people pour in to see Their Lordships. The next phase of the project is on the anvil.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, at present we are working extensively with our project in Guruvayur, Kerala, which got embroiled in legal problems with the neighbors. Land-grabbing is common in Kerala, but we stood our ground and our opponents have learned a lesson, and serious negotiations are on for a settlement. We hope that with your mercy we will be able to overcome this difficulty and build a facility for preaching to the elite educated crowd in Kerala, which has the highest literacy rate in India. The land of Śaṅkarācārya will taste the mercy of Lord Caitanya when your mission is firmly established in their bastion, Guruvayur.

We sincerely seek your blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so that we may be engaged in your service in this wonderful mission and overcome all challenges successfully. Anything is possible with your mercy.

*Your servants at ISKCON’s temples in Coimbatore and Kerala, India.*  
Sarvaiśvarya dāsa

## Columbus

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our mist humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

You once gave the instruction that there should be a temple in Columbus and that it should never close. Today, the Columbus *yātrā* is unique because it is composed mostly of your grand-disciples. Our lives have been forever changed through your timeless mercy, and the growth of our spiritual lives is sweetly monitored by your dear disciples, as is the growth of our community. We pray that we will always have faith in your instructions and therefore continue to assist you in preaching Kṛṣṇa’s glories and spreading the cult of devotion. Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our lives would have no meaning.

*Your grateful servants in Columbus, Ohio, USA.*

## Czech Republic

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On the behalf of the devotees in the Czech *yātrā,* I would like to offer our deep gratitude for all the transcendental gifts you have bestowed upon us. Especially your books, without whom there for sure wouldn’t be any *yātrā* here now. Your books comprise your most important gift to us, and we pray that we will utilize it properly. So many times you said book distribution is the foremost preaching activity, and so far, by your mercy, this *yātrā* has been blessed by having many nice devotees eager perform this service, and we pray this will continue and increase.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, on your last visit to London, when you asked to see the German *saṅkīrtana* devotees, at that time on the top of the world list, they all excitedly sat in front of you, waiting for you to speak. There was of course some expectation that you would say something favorable about their book distribution. But you simply asked, “Do you know our philosophy?”

As always, you went “straight to the point,” cutting the pride and injecting the needed lesson.

You very much wanted that we learn your books nicely. Not just parrotlike repetition, but deep understanding within the heart. Let us not be allured into reading all the volumes and volumes of new books and in this way put your books on the back shelf. Please give us inspiration to study your books, lectures, conversations, and letters every day, since this is the only way we will be able to keep our priorities right.

I’m sure that all sincere devotees really want to follow you truly. Unfortunately, we find ourselves again and again using you to justify our own ideas. Please let us, by your mercy, throw away our independent ideas and exchange them for your ideas. Then we can use you in a bona fide way, in a way that you wanted to be used—to serve Kṛṣṇa. You didn’t write your books to decorate some bookshelf. You wanted this knowledge to be used to “kick the Māyāvādīs on their faces with shoes.” You have compared your books to bombs meant to kill the demoniac mentality in society. You wanted warfare with Māyā. These statements of yours may not seem very “nice.” I personally was a little shocked by them when joining this movement. However, the potency and attraction coming from you—heavy and sharp but sweet and irresistible—haven’t been seen much since you left us. We should always remember that a Vaiṣṇava doesn’t die but dies to live, and living spreads the holy name around.

So now it is our duty to make your words heard by truly representing you and your teachings as you have received them from your beloved Guru Mahārāja, the rest of the *paramparā,* and ultimately from Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the original guru. You are here as much as you always were for those who want to see you. Anyway, who could actually have your intimate association when you were present? A few leaders and servants. For a *mātājī* like me, we might receive a glance or a few words maybe. Anything more was unlikely, and the same was true for the majority of men as well. It seems that even long intimate association with you couldn’t save some devotees from falling down. But some who never even met you are still around because they have truly understood your mission.

*Vāṇī* is more important than *vapu,* and since you stated that “everything is in my books,” we should take it seriously, not wanting to bother you with our shortcomings regarding mismanagement and problems arising from our failure to understand your books. You have obviously more important things to do elsewhere. Still you are totally available to us anytime we care to associate with your books.

As the Czech *yātrā* keeps on growing, we are of course also facing an increasing numbers of *gṛhasthas,* a fact that leads us to deal with issues of social development, roles of parents, etc. You have given many instructions about the importance of ladies being good mothers, wives, grandmothers, etc. Unfortunately, we are not always eager to fulfill these roles because modern society is minimizing their importance, and in this way havoc is created. You also said that you wanted everyone to be a guru, and this sounds better to someone who is trained in career thinking. Foolishly we forget that the mother is the first guru of the child and that we have that important role and responsibility to form the very beginning of a new society, where the majority of work is still to be done.

Before you left you said that you had only accomplished fifty percent of what you wanted to do, the last fifty percent consisting of education of children as well as farms with *gṛhastha* communities. This work you left for us to do. There hasn’t been a success on a grand scale yet, but there have been some individual successes. If your ideas on these subjects are to be realized, a lot of responsibility will naturally fall to the ladies.

You once wrote:

\[Y\]es, actually I want that every woman in the Society should be married. But what is this training to become wives and mothers? No school is required for that, simply association....A woman’s real business is to look after household affairs, keep everything neat and clean, and if there is sufficient milk supply available, she should always be engaged in churning butter, making yogurt, curd, so many nice varieties, simply from milk. The woman should be cleaning, sewing, like that.
