How important festivals are being done in many a town and city.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have awakened us and made us see  
That without what you taught us, our lives would be empty.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is time for the entire world to see  
How you converted *karmī* men to *brahmacārī*.

(written by Mīnākṣī-devī dāsī)

## Rio de Janeiro

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to enlighten our path and to give us transcendental vision. If not for your impact upon our lives, we would not be able to see things as they actually are (sometimes we still can’t).

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

Even though we try to repay you for all the transcendental good fortune you have so kindly bestowed upon us, every effort in this regard will be vain. The reason for this is that any success one may have in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is due to your potency. In fact, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are acting through the devotees who sincerely try to serve you.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliver of all fallen souls, whose mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

How is it possible for a blind man to repay the one who gave him eyesight?

How is it possible for a dumb man to repay the one who gave him the gift of speaking?

How is it possible for a lame man to repay the one who made him walk?

The burning desire to repay you, to be loyal to your mission, to keep alive the fire of devotional service in our hearts—maintaining this strong desire is the only means we have to reciprocate with you for everything you have done and are doing for us.

Although we are aware of our limitations, we are imbued with the same missionary mood that brought you to our lives: our mission is to carry out your mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which is also Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission.

*Your servants at the ISKCON center in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil.*

## St. Louis

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the supreme friend of all living entities, sent Śrīla Prabhupāda to this world for the elevation of all conditioned souls. Śrīla Prabhupāda took the order of his divine master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, seriously, and thus thousands have had the opportunity to hear the absolute truth.

The same message is still being vibrated through Śrīla Prabhupāda’s institution, ISKCON. Without an institution, the organized dissemination of *hari-kathā* would not take place. May the Lord of the heart enlighten the living entities to come forward and engage in the service of His pure devotee.

Once a local resident of Vṛndāvana noticed that many *sādhus* preached but when they disappeared everything collapsed. However, this person observed that even though Śrīla Prabhupāda has not been physically present for over twenty-five years, still his institution continues. This makes Śrīla Prabhupāda very special among all *sādhus*.

ISKCON has continued after the disappearance of Śrīla Prabhupāda because of the efforts of devotees who have surrendered to his instructions. ISKCON will continue to grow as long as there are faithful devotees ready to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda’s directives. Devotees who follow Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions can solve the difficulties ISKCON is facing.

So, on this holiest of days we pray at the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda that we gain the spiritual strength to follow his instructions. For the following of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions opens the way back to Godhead not only for us but also for thousands of others.

*The aspiring servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda at the ISKCON temple in St. Louis, Missouri, USA*.  
(written by Bhīma dāsa)

## Scotland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on the auspicious anniversary of your appearance day! All glories to your ISKCON and the preaching of the Lord’s glories throughout the world!

As we reflect on another year that has passed, we consider how we have been of service to Your Divine Grace and whether you are pleased with our endeavors. In the presence of such an exalted personality, our attempts seem to fade into insignificance, and thoughts of how much more we could have and should have done come to mind. By your transcendental potency, practically every country in the world has a “room of your house,” in different shapes and forms. From large palatial mansions, to farms, to small preaching centers, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is alive and well by your divine inspiration. Your sincere disciples carry your hopes and instructions with them, and with co-operation from your grandchildren they continue your mission of spreading the holy name to every town and village.

In Scotland it is our hope that we are playing our part in this propagation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and that you are pleased by that. We are small in number, but that does not stop us from endeavoring to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you would like. Of course, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your teachings are the focal point of these endeavors. By your grace Kṛṣṇa consciousness was made available to us, and it is now up to us to play our part in your preaching machine. Your teachings are the directives and laws by which all human society should live, and the service of understanding this, taking it to heart, practicing it ourselves, and assisting others to do so is of prime importance. Such a vast wealth of knowledge has been left to us by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that if we dedicate our lives to studying and distributing it, our short lifetimes will be very meaningful and successful.

We hope that we can offer more and more service to Your Divine Grace and that you will continue to bless us with your favor and mercy. The mercy of Your Divine Grace is all we need for successful Kṛṣṇa conscious life because you are so dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa. “By the mercy of the spiritual master, one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement.” It is our desire in the coming year to fill up our new warehouse with your books, thus allowing us to distribute them all over our preaching area. *Kṛṣṇa* books are there—20,000. *Perfect Questions Perfect Answers* are on their way—250,000. *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*—25,000. *The Science of Self-Realization*—40,000. A few other BBT titles are also there in the warehouse, and we have plans to get a few more. We also hope to introduce distribution of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* sets, and negotiations between us, the BBT, and the printer are underway. We trust that this will be pleasing to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because what other kind of offering can we really make? Preaching here in Scotland has its austerities, but we keep struggling through them, remembering that our aim is to please you.

Please forgive our many shortcomings and at times unbearable conditioning, for we really do need and want your mercy. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your transcendental mission! All glories to the preaching of the holy names and your transcendental book distribution!

*Your insignificant servants in Scotland.*

## Seattle

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In your merciful letter of January 16, 1975, to Kṛṣṇāveśa dāsī you wrote:

Our movement is based on love and trust, so if we do not co-operate, then how is that love and trust? Follow all of the rules and regulations very strictly without deviation, chant 16 rounds, attend class and mangala arati and then everything will be alright.

In the years since you gave this instruction, we have come to experience and begun to understand the great power inherent in your much-emphasized formula of cooperation among devotees.

In recent years we have been blessed to be serving together in your ISKCON Seattle *yātrā*. Your beloved disciple Govinda dāsī has told many stories of her visit here with you in 1968, and of how you planted the seed of the tree of *bhakti* here, now seemingly so long ago. In the interim between then and now, the seed you planted began to grow into a strong young sapling, but in the course of time that tree was sadly burned by the forest fire of dissension among devotees and stood in desolation, a charred skeleton. Any traveler passing on his way through the forest might have given that tree up for dead, and had it not been for the healing touch of a certain divine resuscitant, it may well have been so. But lo and behold, before our very eyes the blackened remains began to be gently showered by the redeeming rain of cooperation among godly Vaiṣṇavas, and it magically began to revive. Soon small spring-green buds of new leaves began to appear in little clusters dotted along the trunk and branches. Those buds grew into strong leaves and newly grown twigs and boughs. Now the tree stands in full bloom, decorated with beautiful fruits and flowers to be offered at the lotus feet of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā–Nīla-Mādhava.

Where did this divine intervention come from? We can only surmise that it originated in the spiritual realm with Your Divine Grace. But outwardly our *bhakti* tree was apparently revived through the blood, prayers, tears, labors, and battle cries of many personalities that make up our diverse *yātrā.* Some of these personalities, such as temple president and local GBC Hari Vilāsa Prabhu, along with his superexcellent wife Pārijāta-devī dāsī, are visible agents in this transformation. Yet there are many other empowered catalysts among the greater devotee community who have stimulated and reinforced positive growth and progress in our transcendental tree.

Today our community has grown to include many steady and loving devotees. The temple has been renovated, and construction plans have been drawn up for a new temple building. In the fall of 2002, My Sweet Lord’s Kitchen was opened in the University District as a memorial dedicated to George Harrison. Already this center is attracting throngs of students, seekers, media, and devotees, who are treated to free *prasādam,* ecstatic *kīrtana*s, and classes on *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*.

Lord Caitanya has bestowed some amazing *ajñāta-sukṛti* upon some of the most successful materialistic corporations of the Unites States—most notably Microsoft—by attracting the cream of India’s youth to work for them in the greater Seattle area. These young people are very clearly the Lord’s latest secret weapon for infiltrating the strongholds of *māyā* and revolutionizing mundane society. Appearing as the best of material successes, their personal devotional qualities continually astonish us. Where they have come from, we can only wonder. Only one thing is clear. They have been sent by Kṛṣṇa to help push on your movement.

Many new devotees from these ranks are taking shelter of your lotus feet in Seattle, and they are beginning to take initiation from your senior disciples. Just as you brought your dancing white elephants to India in the seventies to inspire the Indian population to take pride in its spiritual heritage, so now you have brought the Indians to dance in our American streets, distribute your books here, and assume their rightful and natural role as spiritual ambassadors, preaching in towns and villages outside their native land (*bhārata-bhūmite haila manuṣya-janma yāra/ janma sārthaka kari kara para-upakāra: Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 9.41). Holding *Bhagavad-gītā* classes in their homes, to which they invite more and more people from their workplaces; taking time to study your books and learn to distribute them; taking time to learn how to play the *mṛdaṅga* and *karatālas*; going out regularly on *hari-nāma saṅkīrtana*, book distribution, and *prasādam* distribution; organizing temple functions and retreats at venues such as Microsoft and Bastyr University—these young Indian-born *bhaktas* have created a new bloom of *bhakti* that is very invigorating to the community and is a constant inspiration to the older devotees.

In addition to these auspicious circumstances, various *sannyāsīs* and other senior Vaiṣṇavas have graced large preaching events here, such as our Festival of India, and provided direction in our activities of *sādhana* and preaching. We pray that you continue to bless our blossoming *yātrā* with your divine presence and instructions through the mercy of these and other advanced souls.

Just recently, through a sudden and inexplicable illness, we lost our Bhakta dāsa. He was one of the unseen agents working tirelessly through many nights to ensure that the annual Ratha-yātrā and Festival of India happened each year. At the time of his impending death and hospitalization, this community rallied together to offer him our love, prayers, and support. Although we were shocked and saddened at his departure, it was amazing to sense the spirit of fellowship that manifested during this crisis.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you started your movement from nothing, and it went forward when Kṛṣṇa sent you your first disciples. Ordinary people could not have accomplished for you what those devotees accomplished. They were clearly cut out for the mission of assisting you. We think, therefore, that wherever we see your mission being carried forward today, we must recognize the special qualifications of the devotees involved. Wherever there are devotees who know the art of cooperating to show their love for you, and who can inspire others to do likewise by their example, all victory will reign for ISKCON. All inauspiciousness will disappear; all philosophical misconceptions will be rectified; all facility will be provided. Alone, none of us can push on your movement, but together we can grow a luxurious *bhakti* tree even from a petrified stump—and we can be practically assured of not failing you.

*Your aspiring servants in Seattle, Washington, USA.*  
(written by Kṛṣṇāveśa-devī dāsī)

## Silicon Valley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*Śāstra* proclaims that achieving a human body is *sudurlabham*, “very rare.” And Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu told Sanātana Gosvāmī that of all those who have attained a rare human body, only a small number are followers of the Vedic principles. “Among these,” Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “almost half simply give lip service while committing all kinds of sinful activities against the principles.” He continued by saying, “Of those who do follow the Vedic knowledge, most follow the process of fruitive activity; and out of many sincere fruitive actors, there may be one who is actually wise.” And finally, He declared that, “Out of many millions of such wise men, one may actually become liberated, and out of many millions of such liberated persons, a pure devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa is very difficult to find.”(*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 19.144–148)

Indeed, in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* Mahātmā Vidura says that the association of pure devotees is *durāpā*, or “rarely obtainable.” In fact, he says that “Persons whose austerity is meager can hardly obtain the service of the pure devotees who are progressing on the path back to the kingdom of God.”

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda are that pure devotee whom Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu described to Sanātana Gosvāmī. You are the one that Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura proclaims to be *sākṣād-dharitva,* or the direct representative of the Lord. You have the power to attract everyone to Kṛṣṇa because you carry the Lord within your heart.

We are astounded at our good fortune that you moved among us. But it is even more amazing how you continue to attract those who never personally met or saw you. Maitreya Ṛṣi has revealed to Mahātmā Vidura that the pure devotees of the Lord mercifully leave the world with their writings, which sing of the Lord, “the Pious Actor”; Maitreya says that these writings are “so nicely arranged that the actual purpose of the ear is served just by being near them”(*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.6.37). This is true about your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda. People all over the world continue to be enlightened by the alluring fragrance of your personality emanating from your purports. Your books are so nicely arranged that if someone is fortunate enough to read one word or one line from them, his or her perfection in life is practically assured. This is your extraordinary potency.

We therefore voluntarily and happily surrender to you in the mood of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, who has written the prayer *vaiṣṇava ṭhākura tomāra kukura baliyā jānaha more: “*O Vaiṣṇava Ṭhākura, please accept me as your dog.” We, the members of ISKCON of Silicon Valley, implore you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this holy day of your divine appearance, that we may become your faithful dogs and that you will accept us. As a dog waits outside the door of his master for some food remnants, we’ll accept for our nourishment only the scraps of mercy that you may throw to us. Without these scraps, we will prefer to starve. Just as a faithful dog lives for a chance to please his master, we too, hoping to please you and to expand your benevolent mission in Silicon Valley, have gathered together.

Now that the temple congregation is growing, we’ve begun to regularly offer to the Deities the following prayer, written by your disciple Śrīmān Akrūranātha Prabhu:

Dear Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madanamohana, and Śrī Śrī Lakṣmī-Nṛsiṁha-deva. You are always favorably disposed to your confidential servant Śrīla Prabhupāda, by whose wishes You have kindly consented to reside in Silicon Valley and to accept our inadequate worship. In order to expand Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON preaching mission here, we are trying to locate and purchase a new ISKCON temple property. Please guide us in this endeavor and make our efforts successful, so that we may improve our standard of worshiping You, establish a better base from which to broadcast Your message, and accommodate a growing community of faithful devotees. Actually, everything and everyone belongs to You, but the fallen souls in this world have forgotten this important fact. At Śrīla Prabhupāda’s request, we are trying to remember this fact, and to remind others also. Without Your blessings, there is no hope of success, but with Your blessings there is no possibility of failure. Therefore, while we strive to engage in Your devotional service by installing Your Lordships in a suitable new temple, we are entirely dependent on Your mercy. Please help us.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our lord and master and our life and soul birth after birth. We pray to dedicate ourselves more fully to your instructions. May we dive deeply into the chanting of the holy names of the Lord and take up your work of showing mercy to all living beings by distributing the holy names.

All glories to you!

*Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.*  
(written by Vaiśeṣika dāsa)

## Slovak Republic

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances!

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Our Most Beautiful Śrīla Prabhupāda,**

In the essay “Truth and Beauty,” you wrote that the Absolute Truth is most beautiful. Kṛṣṇa is so beautiful that He attracts everybody, including Himself. But how to approach Him? Where to find him? Which way to move?

So out of your kindness you have appeared as a link to Kṛṣṇa. You are appearing with your enchanting beauty, which originates in your unalloyed love for and devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You are appearing as a light, which leads us from the dark room to the sun. Whatever you do, whatever you touch, whatever comes in contact with you is becoming beautiful. By your magic our stonelike hearts are changing, although Kṛṣṇa is as far away from us as you are.

You are the personification of “higher taste.” It is said that nothing is as sublime as transcendental knowledge—knowledge about pure devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You are the embodiment of it. You are the embodiment of sublimity. Your whole life story, described in your biography, *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta,* is *Bhagavad-gītā* in practice—nondifferent from any *śāstra,* bringing us the inexhaustible taste. You are so high, we are so low; nevertheless you consider yourself *our* servant, bringing transcendental bliss, calling “Come and taste it! Do not rot in this dark condition! Do not stop your progress until you reach *prema-bhakti*—love of Kṛṣṇa!”

You are our most beautiful and most attractive, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Day after day we pray to be able to perceive beauty, which emanates from you.

You are our only hope.

*ye me bhakta-janāḥ pārtha na me bhaktāś ca te janāḥ*  
*mad-bhaktānāṁ ca ye bhaktās te me bhakta-tamā matāḥ*

**\[Lord Kṛṣṇa told Arjuna:\] “Those who are My direct devotees are actually not My devotees, but those who are the devotees of My servant are factually My devotees. (*****Ādi Purāṇa*****)**

*Your servants in ISKCON’s Slovak yātrā, including New Ekacakra dhāma and Govinda’s restaurants, the Slovak Republic.*

## Slovenia

Dear respected Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet. Please accept our insignificant attempt to serve Your Divine Grace. All glories to the previous *ācāryas,* whose desires your have fulfilled by spreading the glories of Lord Caitanya throughout the whole world. All glories to Their Lordships Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Lord Nityānanda Avadhūta, Lord Advaita, Śrī Gadādhara, and all of Lord Gaura’s associates, headed by Śrī Śrīvāsa, whose perfect representative you are.

How to glorify you? Our ability to understand your glories is totally absent. We see only some insignificant amount of your glories, so how to glorify your properly? You are fully transcendental and an intimate servant of Lord Gaurāṅga. Therefore we pray to Lord Gaurāṅga to give us the proper intelligence so we can properly glorify you. Your glorification is the perfect remedy for all the problems of material existence.

You are most the most worshipable *ācārya,* the founder-*ācārya* of our International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. Not only are you ISKCON’s founder, but you are also the power of ISKCON, and only by your mercy are we insignificant servants of your servant able to do some service to Lord Caitanya. Our services are full of errors, and our intelligence is covered by the three modes of material nature, but you accept every service as something pleasing to you, and you offer this to Lord Gaurāṅga. So only by your mercy has some wonderful preaching been done. And we are able to advance in spite of being completely unqualified to perform devotional service. You have mercifully sent your representatives to save us, and you have given us the shelter of your lotus feet. You have given us a new life and hope. You have purified our contaminated intelligence so that we can understand life’s purpose. Your spiritual power is unlimited.

Your are the destroyer of the bad influences of the Age of Kali. You are the true general of Lord Caitanya’s army. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura predicted that the Russians, English, and other Westerners would chant the holy names in Māyāpur. This was a really amazing prediction! But the impossible became possible by Your Divine Grace’s desire, compassion, and full surrender to the lotus feet your Guru Mahārāja and the previous *ācāryas*. All around the world there are now devotees of Kṛṣṇa. Please let us be your soldiers in your transcendental army.

You are the perfect example of a devotee and also of an *ācārya*. You perfectly followed what you preached. You have shown everything by your perfect example. Please give us the strength to follow the spiritual *sādhana* properly.

*Mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim:* a dumb person speaks and a lame person crosses mountains. How is this wonderful thing possible? Only by the mercy of Gurudeva. By your mercy we are celebrating the twentieth anniversary of ISKCON in Slovenia. In this twentieth year, many wonderful services have been performed and devotees have distributed many hundreds of thousands of your books. How is this possible? Only by Your Divine Grace’s desire to save the suffering souls from the clutches of the illusory energy and give them shelter at Lord Gaurāṅga’s lotus feet.

*gaurāṅgera duṭi pada, jār dhana sampada,*  
*se jāne bhakati-rasa-sār*

**“Anyone who has accepted the two lotus feet of Lord Caitanya as his only asset can understand the true essence of devotional service.”**

Devotional service is very difficult to understand. We see how easily devotees get bewildered by the illusory energy. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give your mercy to all of us, as well as to those who have rendered so much service to Lord Gaurāṅga and somehow gotten bewildered. Please let them again find shelter at your lotus feet and serve Lord Gaurāṅga.

Our only hope is to take shelter of you. You have taken full shelter of Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet, so all the confidential knowledge of devotional service is known to you. And therefore you are our eternal guide and well-wisher.

Your books are our torchlights to see the proper way; your nectarean words are giving us the strength to fight against the illusory energy. The dust of your lotus feet is our sole shelter. We do not want to call ourselves by material identifications, for we are proud to be your followers, Prabhupādānugas.

In the twenty years of this *yātrā* we have performed so many programs, but we could not always take full advantage of the preaching opportunities Lord Kṛṣṇa gave us to assist you in your strong desire to spread Lord Gaurāṅga’s glories. Please give us the intelligence and strength we need to truly call ourselves servants of your servants by spreading the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and the glories of Lord Gaurāṅga all around Slovenia.

Please give us the strength to properly honor the devotees so they feel cared-for and happy in your society. In a cooperative mood we can make nice offerings to your lotus feet, so our prayers to Your Divine Grace are to protect us from the influences of Kali that cause unnecessary quarrels and disharmony.

Please give us the strength to be able to inspire more and more people to join your wonderful ISKCON so that we can truly manifest “the house where the whole world can live.” So many suffering people need your mercy. Please empower us to be able to serve you properly.

You are mercifully present in your worshipable form in our temple. Please give us the proper vision so we can fully understand your presence and take advantage of this boon.

Thank you for sending Their Lordships Śrī Pañca-tattva to our New Yogapīṭha Temple so we can serve them and take advantage of Their transcendental presence.

We offer all our services to your lotus feet and beg for your mercy. Thank you for everything.  
*Your humble servants in Slovenia.*

## Solapur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Kṛṣṇa. Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this auspicious occasion of your appearance. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We, your small and insignificant devotees of ISKCON Solapur, are struggling to preach your mission. We are an extension center of ISKCON Pandharpur. We are only two *brahmacārīs* and one *vānaprastha* living in the *brahmacārī āśrama*. A small but enthusiastic congregation comes daily to join us in prayer.

Today is April 28, 2003. The sounds of the power looms churning out textiles in the factory next-door to Śrī Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā and Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Śacīnandana are *akhaṇḍa-kīrtana*—they go on twenty-four hours a day. They only stop during the frequent power failures. Would that our devotion to your lotus feet were also as fixed up. And please let our slips into the three modes of material nature, causing power failures in our *bhakti,* also cease. Let our consciousness flow unceasingly to your lotus feet, like rivers flowing to the ocean.

*tvayi me ’nanya-viṣayā matir madhu-pate ’sakṛt*  
*ratim udvahatād addhā gaṅgevaugham udanvati*

**“O Lord of Madhu, as the Ganges forever flows to the sea without hindrance, let my attraction be constantly drawn unto You without being diverted to anyone else.” (a prayer by Queen Kuntī,** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.8.42)**

April 29, 2003: Last night something very favorable happened here in ISKCON Solapur. Your dear devotee His Grace Sādhu-saṅga Prabhu donated an excellent *sat-saṅga* hall. The ribbon-cutting ceremony was performed by your dear devotee His Holiness Lokanāth Swami, the zonal secretary of our extension center. More than a hundred devotees attended the function.

This morning devotees peacefully chanted *japa* after *maṅgala-ārati*. Gone were the sounds of the textile mills with their grinding of gears and slamming of silks. Instead, devotees sat around your photo chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. You wore a special smile.

We are not constructing a temple here, but we can see the temples nicely coming up in the hearts of your devotees. The Deity worship is now done completely by your congregational devotees, leaving the *brahmacārīs* free to preach. The devotees here enthusiastically participate in festivals, with wonderful *abhiṣekas, kīrtanas*, lectures, and dramas. Let the festivals in their hearts increase more and more until their joy surges forth and floods all of Solapur with *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*!

We have rekindled the eternal flame of relishable Vaiṣṇava relationships with the devotees of ISKCON Pandharpur. ISKCON Pandharpur temple president His Grace Sanaka-Sanātana Prabhu is encouraging us and supporting us. Devotees from Solapur and Pandharpur now regularly visit one another to help with their respective preaching programs. This is another temple of the heart that is growing with your mercy—the temple of cooperation between devotees.

You said to your disciples, “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate together to keep this institution together after I am gone.”

On behalf of the devotees of ISKCON Solapur, I pledge on this most sacred day of your Vyāsa-pūjā to always strive to become the servant of the servant of the servant of all your devotees and to endeavor to be an example in this regard, with the aim of encouraging our other devotees to continue to fully take shelter of your lotus feet.

Please accept this insignificant offering of love. We beg for your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your devotees at ISKCON Solapur, India.*  
(written by Ekalavya dāsa)

## Stockholm

My dear spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was born in this age of darkness and madness and was wandering in the weeping forest of sadness all the time. One day, disgusted and tired, I sat down to think about the meaning of life. Then you appeared in your shining and transcendental figure. You opened my blind eyes and showed me the divine lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. You taught me how to listen to the ecstatic *kīrtanas* of the devotees of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. You purified my lusty tongue by giving me the *mahā-mantra* and *mahā-prasādam*. And then, Your Divine Grace, you kindly glanced over my dusty heart and I understood the meaning of life—devotional service.

You also gave me the most precious gift one can get—the *saṅga* of *sādhus*. Now I have the opportunity to listen to the many souls who have had the great good fortune of receiving Your Divine Grace’s love and mercy.

But now, even though I have so many reasons to be happy, I still have sorrow in my heart. It seems impossible to find the proper words to glorify you, as your glories are beyond the reach of my mind. And if I find them, I am afraid that from my unqualified mouth they will sound like the croaking of a frog.

My dear spiritual master, even if you are no longer present in your physical body among us, you are still guarding us in our little peaceful temple in Stockholm. And it is amazing how you reciprocate with the devotees. So many of them can feel your presence among us, showing us the way back home, back to Godhead.

In the end, I just want to pray to you: Please never ever leave us, and please give us the ability to understand that you are *our ever well-wisher*. And please forgive this offender.

*Your servants in Stockholm, Sweden.*  
(written by Bhaktin Manuela)

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

A real devotee, a real disciple, should be simple: free from all duplicity, pretension, and deceit.

Since we are conditioned souls, our nature is to always take shelter of falsity. To assume or to project by some contrivance or other a *persona* we believe will confer some distinction or superior status: something that will set us apart as unique...*special*. Our perverse and abiding conceit is such that even on the spiritual path, ironically, the urge to falsity remains a potent driving force. Therefore Lord Caitanya himself describes the rareness of finding someone in this world of an *unalloyed* pure devotional character. Someone who is not trying to prove anything, or to be anything: someone existing simply, contentedly, as he really is—a humble, unassuming servant: a servant...of a servant...of the Lord’s supreme servant.

Reflecting the innate desire to be special and apart, spiritualists—and even ordinary devotees—inevitably fall prey to some self-promotional indulgence. They cannot refrain, by some artifice or subtle contrivance, from trying to assume a certain deservingness of acclaim or special appreciation. In contrast, Śrīla Prabhupāda *was* actually unique and distinct in being completely free from all of that. His special-ness and his greatness lay in his very lack of pretension. He was only ever himself, his natural self, unaffected, unadorned, honest, humble, simple, and real. Never did he succumb to what for him would have been any amount of opportunity to promote himself personally as unique or superior. The distinctive feature and overriding accomplishment of Śrīla Prabhupāda was his consistency in seeing himself as nothing more, of being nothing more, and of presenting himself as nothing more than a simple and dedicated servant of his own dear spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

He did, however, have a great and even defiant pride in claiming his due as such: his due as a loyal disciple of a great spiritual master and a sincere servant of his eternal transcendental order. Once, reflecting on someone else’s claim to some mystic experience (regarding which he was generally disdainful and dismissive) he remarked, in effect, that

Not that these things may not happen, but, at least in my own case, I have never had any such experience. People say like this to impress others. Therefore they are claiming...the most learned, the most advanced. But it is seen that amongst all of them only I have accomplished something. I do not consider myself advanced. And they \[referring to the Rādhā-kuṇḍa *bābājīs*) also do not consider me to be advanced. But I have always had one hundred percent faith in the instruction of my spiritual master. Therefore it has been successful to some extent.

By teaching and demonstrating this principle: that the only thing that ever counts for anything is a disciple’s loyalty, fidelity, and humble, simple-hearted acceptance of his guru’s order—life and soul—Śrīla Prabhupāda not only reduced the pride, pretense, and spiritual make-show of others to foolishness, he also inspired within us all, fallen as we obviously are, an optimism and even a confidence in the possibility of our own success. It is tough having to be unique; it is so hard to be someone special; impossible to become *advanced*! But the perspective that Śrīla Prabhupāda brought, and conveyed to us by his own example, means we need no longer suffer the insecurities of self-doubt, nor consequently the stress of pretending, or of straining and striving for what cannot be. All we require, simply, is to become a good disciple. And while that notion may still present its obvious challenges, at least the idea is accessible: success is, at least, conceivable. It does in fact raise a great hope, a hope within all of us that we can still succeed and that therefore, despite our numerous failings, one day, Kṛṣṇa willing, all will be well again.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, like the gold standard, has created a measure against which all other things may be assessed and judged. It is his integrity as a disciple, his demonstration of a simple-hearted and pure devotion for his spiritual master that has saved us. Saved us from being fooled, from being ourselves foolish. Saved us from doubt and despair. Given us hope and a clear resolve. Beyond that, he has also inspired within us a great joy and a fierce and even defiant pride in him as our own spiritual master. And by dint of his unbounded integrity as a disciple he has created such a paradigm of virtue as to enable us to revel in an utter disdain for anyone or anything that is not reflective of him. These things, through his freedom from duplicity and absolute adherence to the principle of guru and disciple, he has given us.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, though it may only be as a matter of principle that we accept you as our guru, and though, what to speak of love, we may have very little sincerity even for serving you, still, what we do have is a kind of raw enthusiasm for you, and also some small degree of appreciation for what you have given us. These things, little enough as they are, confer at least some meaning and hope on our otherwise void existence. I pray to Kṛṣṇa that I may guard and nurture these sentiments, rough and ill-formed as they may be; that they may grow more and more and that, one day, they may swell into the kind of great love that you have for your spiritual master. I pray that I may become like you—a real devotee, a real disciple, free from all pretension, duplicity, and deceit: Simple.

*Your humble servants at ISKCON Sydney, Australia.*

## Szeged

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet! All glories to you!

Your appearance day should be important for all devotees. In fact, we should keep contemplating your transcendental qualities and the special nature of your divine mercy each and every day. Neglecting this, would we be able to understand and know you?

Today is the most auspicious day of the year, for we devote all our attention to you. If only we could always devote the same attention!

I have still not realized the amount of luck—or rather mercy—I encountered by getting to know your books. And I am sure many other devotees think the same way. Had we not met those books or those devotees spreading your transcendental mercy, we would never have known about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One thing is sure: without being connected to you, we would have been completely lost in the dark swamps of ignorance and illusion, without the faintest hope of escape.

In your books you explain in several places that ignorance means “chewing the chewed.” Yes, most probably that would be the case with me. For many, many births we have been tasting the same old chewing gum, which actually has no taste at all. How many plans have we made to try to achieve happiness, and how many times have we tried to avoid the merciless pounding of the material energy? We have most probably done so many millions of times, until we were finally woken up by you from this illusion and given a goal of life that is the only source of happiness.

It is still inconceivable to me what you have given me. It is all too special and pure compared to my polluted mind.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if we really want to know you, then we first have to know your teachings. By neglecting your teachings, we also neglect our responsibility toward you, and we will never understand the greatest desire of your heart, which is preaching. By preaching we help others to become just as fortunate as we have become by encountering you. By spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness we change people’s hearts, but we change ours as well. Isn’t it wonderful? We can do two things at the same time. And this is the real purification of the heart. It is a hard job, since we must start the changes in our own hearts first, but...no pain, no gain.

Every devotee who considers himself just a little serious takes part in spreading your mission. This is our insignia, in addition to your books and the holy name. Success in preaching is a long process, and we must be very patient.

Devotees have been present here in Szeged since 1991, and they have been trying hard to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is like being at war, since we have actually proclaimed war on the material energy. We have fought a lot of battles and lost many of these. Devotees have been coming and going, moving in and out; there were times when we were few and other times when we were many, but the only thing that has never changed is that we have always wanted to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Places, times, and methods may change, but commitment never should.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue showering your causeless mercy upon us so that we will be qualified to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness and eventually win the final battle.

*Your servants in Szeged, Hungary.*  
(written by Jayaśrī-devī dāsī)

## Tampa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for coming to the West. We cannot measure our good fortune from meeting you. Those who surrender to you and take up your mission to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every single person in the world are the happiest people in the world.

To fulfill your mission, the devotees in Tampa, Florida, including Hasyakāri, Jaya Gopāl, Madan Mohan, Harināmdev, Prasant, Sanjiv, and their devoted wives have purchased a wonderful temple and property for the residence of our Deities, Rādhā-Vṛndāvanacandra. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us so much enthusiasm to preach.

Just hearing about your pastimes on this planet knocks the material world right out of our heads and drowns us in love and compassion for all living entities. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like the atom bomb, annihilating in seconds the bogus philosophies of the mental speculators. Thank you so much. Without hearing your words, we would be continuing to go through millions and millions of lifetimes of speculation. How hellish! You broke all our anxieties and confusions to pieces and put us on a supersonic elevator (ISKCON) to the spiritual world, Goloka Vṛndāvana. How you did this will be analyzed by pundits forever.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you were to appear before us today, would you be happy with us and what we are doing in your mission? I would say yes and no. Yes because ISKCON has expanded tremendously due to the work of so many of your serious, dedicated disciples and grand-disciples. And I would say no because of big mistakes we made that have hindered the united effort of all devotees to fulfill your mission. As you often said, it takes great transcendental intelligence to manage ISKCON. It is not done by a material formula. When you arrived in America in 1965 you expressed shock at the powerful influence of the modes of passion and ignorance, and you prayed to Kṛṣṇa to make your words effective in changing the people. Kṛṣṇa empowered you because of your full surrender, and we also can be empowered to do wonderful service like you, if we fully surrender to you, our Jagad Guru, our Founder-*ācārya,* our savior of the fallen, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Let us always remember: the nectar and happiness of life is in the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrīla Prabhupāda. If we are not feeling happy in spiritual life, it must mean we are not “watering the root,” which means pleasing Kṛṣṇa. But this can be easily achieved by distribution of *Bhagavad-gītā*, an essential part of the *saṅkīrtana* of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Haribol!

*Your servants in Tampa, Florida.*  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Tokyo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you!

You came to us  
in ’65;  
you spoke the truth,  
no compromise.  
“This life is short,  
we won’t live long;  
call to the Lord,  
we must be strong.”  
No chance for us,  
the human race;  
you’re pulling us out  
with Kṛṣṇa’s grace.  
Birth, death, disease,  
also old age;  
come on—get out  
of Māyā’s cage.  
We’re lost in the world,  
Māyā’s playground;  
you’re giving us hope,  
spiritual sound.  
Secret weapon:  
taste sensation;  
Kṛṣṇa’s mercy  
meditation.  
Ancient wisdom  
to help us fight  
against Māyā’s hold—  
you give us light.  
Two hundred sets  
of *Bhāgavatams,*  
with purports too:  
the *bṛhat* drum.  
Study your books,  
do not be fools,  
chant Kṛṣṇa’s name.  
follow the rules.  
Happy we are  
that you came west,  
the Lord’s servant  
doing his best.

*From your humble servants in Tokyo, Japan.*

## Towaco

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord, because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Kṛṣṇa\].”**

Our dearmost eternal father Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious day of your appearance, please accept our prostrated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet, which are most powerful in purifying us from the contamination of this material world.

Although I was born in the holy land of India in a Vaiṣṇava family, I was completely misinformed, misdirected, and misguided about spiritual life. You have saved not only me but millions like me from becoming degraded in the material whirlpool of hellish conditioned life and ignorance.

By your pure Bhaktivedanta purports and your personal direction, and by personally exemplifying the teachings, you have made Vedic literatures such as the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* so sweet and practical that any sincere person can relish them and apply them in his life.

The scriptures say, “Please wake up and try to understand the boon you have in this human form of life! The path of spiritual realization is very difficult, and it is sharp like a razor’s edge. This is the opinion of the learned transcendental scholars.” Because of your compassion and kindness, you have made this transcendental path very easy for simple-hearted, faithful persons.

You have taught us how, without our realizing it, Māyā is alluring us and deluding us and entrapping us in the material world of repeated birth and death, causing us to suffer like anything. You are the only one who clearly showed the fine points distinguishing Māyāvāda philosophy from *bhakti* and how dangerous Māyāvāda is. You have made the path of *bhakti* very easy to understand and execute in a very pleasing way—chanting, dancing, feasting, and then philosophy—and shown that this is our permanent asset.

On this most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā day of yours, we pray with all sincerity that you kindly bless us to serve wholeheartedly your mission and your eternal society ISKCON with our body, mind, and words.

*Your eternal servants, the community of devotees at ISKCON of New Jersey, USA.*  
(written by Śamīka Ṛṣi dāsa)

## Tyumen

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your Divine Grace! Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet on this auspicious day of your appearance!

By the mercy of your sincere devotees I have gotten an opportunity to offer you this modest offering. Among your great followers who powerfully spread your mission through this wonderful Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, I don’t feel myself able to really describe your fame. But devotees are so merciful that they allowed me to approach your lotus feet.

My fate is a good example of your genius, your ability to transform demons into the followers of Lord Caitanya. You became the last shelter for such a fallen soul as I when you came to me in the form of the Deity. Then you allowed to get the mercy of your genuine representative and his servants, surrounding me with spiritual care and giving me the ability to perform devotional service. Now I feel very happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I have the opportunity to serve you in my *prabhu-datta-deśa* (which in 1994 was named Prabhupāda-deśa). I feel that you and the Lord had some plan for me, and for a long time I sincerely prayed for it to be revealed to me. Thank you very much! I have no more material desire. I pray that you give me the ability to give your compassion to all people in this region of Russia. By your mercy I understand more and more the tears in your heart when you saw this mad civilization.

I have a strong desire in my heart to dedicate my life to your mission. The devotees of Prabhupāda-deśa can do much service for your mission. There are serious leaders here who have taken responsibility for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this region. The devotees meet together and discuss devotional service and the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They strive to accept Lord Caitanya’s order to become a guru and set free those who live near. We are very much inspired to develop the Bhakti-vṛkṣa program, practicing the mood of cooperation based on deep spiritual relationships. In this activity, which needs sincerity and purity, we completely depend on your mercy.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us become a real spiritual family near your paternal lotus feet! Since you want Kṛṣṇa consciousness to spread here, no power in this world can stop this movement. No doubt, if the people see the Vaiṣṇavas’ real devotion to Kṛṣṇa, to each other, and to all living entities, they will easily accept the holy name and make Lord Caitanya vary happy.

On this wonderful day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I sing your glories with great inspiration: *ebe jaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana,* may your fame spread all over the three worlds. *Jaya* Prabhupāda!!!

*Your servants in Tyumen, Russia.*  
(written by Vasuśreṣṭha dāsa)

## Veracruz

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our must humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

*bhaktisiddhānta-śiśyāya bhaktivedānta-nāmine*  
*prasannāya praśāntāya tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“Let me offer my obeisances unto my Guru Mahārāja, who is a disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who is always calm and joyful, and who bears the name Bhaktivedanta.”**

**Dear to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord**

You have come to this material world to fulfill the mission of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord.

You are very dear to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord, and therefore He sent you to this world on the anniversary of the very next day after His own appearance in this material world.

If someone takes up the service of the Supreme Lord under the guidance of your divine words, you free him from fear in this material world.

You are a real *paramahaṁsa* because you have spread the chanting of the holy name of the Supreme Lord all over the world, and whoever seriously takes up this chanting becomes free from fear.

Seeing your perfect example, many have become inspired to render you service with their body, mind, and words, and they want to surrender to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord

You are the real self-effulgent *pracāraka* because whoever came to you wanted to render service to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord.

You are the real *parivrājakācārya* because as long as you possibly could you traveled everywhere, glorifying Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord.

On this day please let us offer our body, mind, and words in your service, and bless us to be fixed in your service and the service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord. Please be merciful to all of us, because we do not have any other shelter in this material world except you, our dear lord and master.

*Your aspiring servants at ISKCON Veracruz, Mexico.*

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Today, on this most auspicious occasion, I would like first of all to inform you that I am very proud to be your disciple and am very happy too. I feel so fortunate and especially so grateful to you for having accepted me as your disciple.

This year, coming back from Śrīdhām Māyāpur, I took a flight from Calcutta to Delhi. I happened to be seated next to an elegant middle-aged Indian lady. As soon as the plane took off, she took out from her bag a small *Bhagavad-gītā* in *devanāgarī* and begun reciting each verse by heart in a soft voice, and while doing so she was kissing every page as she turned it. I thought, “Wow, this lady is really learned and so devotional!” Then the hostess came and asked everyone if they would like a vegetarian or nonvegetarian dinner; when she came to us I politely refused any meal (as usual), but the lady asked for non-veg! I was surprised: such vast knowledge of Sanskrit and show of devotion, but no real understanding! Right there I had the proof that without having had the association (even for a moment) with a pure devotee of the Lord, it is not possible to develop any good qualities, even if one is very advanced academically.

As I looked out the small window at the blue sky, I reflected upon my good fortune. Actually, I don’t know why this good fortune happened to me, one among billions of souls, nor do I know why I still have this good fortune, because I am still part of your family after many years, even if I am not special at all and I am no better than anyone else. I therefore definitely conclude that I am a living proof of your greatness, of your causeless mercy and overwhelming spiritual power. And you saved many, many others who, like me, were wondering lonely in this world, without any purpose in life.

Simply by spending some time with us—your sons and daughters, grandsons and granddaughters—people can detect that you are not an ordinary great personality, nor an ordinary transcendentalist, guru, or master. You are different. You are very special because you infused your teachings with life—you taught us to live the *Bhagavad-gītā,* not only to read its Sanskrit verses. You inspired us to apply Kṛṣṇa’s teachings to Arjuna in our everyday lives. In fact, you have made a revolution in our way of life because you were able to change all our habits—our eating, sleeping, cleansing, speaking, dressing, etc. But most of all you changed our thoughts; you redirected our feelings and emotions.

What else can I say? I was tricked by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! You asked me to come and chant, dance, and feast, and I did it, without realizing that you were monopolizing my life. In fact, now I can’t do anything without relating it to you: when I speak, I cannot help but glorify your qualities; when I hear some sound, I find myself wishing to hear your voice; when I eat, I offer my food to you; when I pray, you are in my prayers and also in my innermost desires; when I cry, I call for you to console me; when I am peaceful, it is because I know you are near me to protect me. Indeed my last thought before going to sleep at night is for you and my first one when I wake up is also for you.

Whenever I come to know of some great feat accomplished by some great personality in the field of science, art, business, humanitarianism, etc., immediately my mind goes to you and everyone else becomes dimmed by your personality—all accomplishments become tiny compared to yours. Your qualities have no comparison with anyone else’s; in fact, as the *Bhāgavatam* says, all the best qualities of the demigods are present in you.

In this way you have monopolized my thoughts, my feelings, and my actions, and I am most happy that you have done it. Actually, I am praying today that you keep monopolizing my existence and that whenever you desire you will please call me back to you, call me for some more service, call me to your soft lotus feet and monopolize my next life, too.

*All the fortunate souls who serve Śrīla Prabhupāda at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-ramaṇa temple and Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa, Italy.*  
(written by Āli-Kṛṣṇa-devī dāsī)

## Vṛndāvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have spread the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu throughout the world.

Vṛndāvana is your home, and home is where your heart is. The proof is that you chose to remain here at your *samādhi* so we could continue to have your association by serving you intimately here. As your servants in Vṛndāvana, we have a serious responsibility to maintain your home carefully, according to your directions. We are trying to put into effect your vision of ISKCON Vṛndāvana and offer you a garland of achievements this year, praying that you will accept this effort by the community.

There is excellent attendance of the entire morning program by resident devotees and guests.

The management is making all efforts to see that the devotees are fully engaged in service.

There is a regular, well-attended evening program with *Bhagavad-gītā* classes and *ārati*.

Your rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple are peacefully occupied, and the water supply is connected.

An International Brahmacārī Training College is being organized to train young men.

ISKCON’s first Bhakti Vaibhava course was a success, with thirty students attending.

The Kārttika Festival brought together five thousand devotees from all over the world.

Food for Life is distributing more than one thousand plates of *kicharī* every day in front of the temple.

A welcoming committee now greets visitors and is available to assist them during their stay.

*Iṣṭagoṣṭhīs* are held regularly to increase love and trust among the devotee community.

Gurukula and temple devotees are communicating and coordinating.

The temple managers are working closely to follow the directions you gave for Vṛndāvana.

Twenty-four hour *kīrtana* has been going on steadily, enlivening the entire atmosphere.

The temple bell is always rung on time.

The Bhaktivedanta Swami Gurukula has 110 students enrolled for next semester.

The accounts department is functioning systematically and efficiently.

Legal cases are being cleared up by your mercy.

The temple grounds are clean and attractive.

The Bhaktivedanta Swami Goshala is producing enough milk for Their Lordships and the Gurukula children. Pure cow ghee is made from the extra milk.

The cows are in good health and the bulls are working. No cows or bulls are ever sold.

The municipality has built a road to your Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana Trust *āśrama* behind the temple.

After Janmāṣṭamī, India’s prime minister, deputy prime minister, president, and vice president received Śrī Kṛṣṇa *prasādam* from the Krishna-Balarām temple.

A new temple at the holy place of Vṛnda-kuṇḍa, at Nandagaon, was inaugurated in February, and *prasādam* was distributed to ten thousand villagers.

Food for Life has organized *prasādam* distribution and Kṛṣṇa conscious educational programs in surrounding villages.

There is regular preaching in colleges in nearby towns and villages.

The mailout department is distributing thousands of your books through the post.

All commercial activity has been removed around the temple area.

The temple interior is being restored to its original design and colors.

Twenty-five acres of land has been given for *go-seva*.

Five acres of land has been donated to construct a *vānaprastha āśrama*.

A serious effort is being made to keep pigeons out of the temple.

Your *samādhi* has been completely cleaned and polished, and repairs are being made.

The guesthouse has added fifteen rooms.

The temple sewer line is under repair and will be connected to the municipal line.

A new EPDX telephone system with facility for one hundred intercom extensions has been installed.

Zonal preaching is going on in many cities.

Book distribution is increasing daily around the temple grounds.

You have given us a great responsibility to maintain your home according to your standard. As you have said, “Charity begins at home,” so we are trying to give the most valuable gifts of the holy name, your books, and a transcendentally pleasing atmosphere to anyone who visits Vṛndāvana. It is a fact that since you established Śrī Krishna-Balarām Mandir, Vṛndāvana has become world famous. Even in the summertime the temple is crowded with pilgrims. Your vision for Vṛndāvana was so vast, you set the foundation for spiritualizing the entire world from the beacon light of Vṛndāvana. We pray for the intelligence to carry out your vision successfully so all living entities can come back home, back to Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma, by your causeless mercy.

*Your humble servants at Śrī Krishna-Balarām Mandir, Vṛndāvana, India.*

## Washington, D. C.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In your service we live in a city where political aggression sometimes causes people in other parts of the world to cry. Through arrogance, America has become the tiger who once received the mystic yogi’s mercy but now stares at the yogi desiring to eat him. Where is the gratitude? The tiger has come so far by the grace of the wise yogi—from a tiny mouse, to a cat, to a dog, to a tiger—but the yogi sees the mind and intent of the tiger. This is an explosive moment when change is imminent: an exploited colony called America, which in two hundred years has become an aggressively exploitative empire, may, by God’s will, again become a mouse. What do we, as devotees serving in the hub of this dominion, desire? How will we act? Who will we become? How will our actions impact the world?

O my Lord, as powerful as fire, O omnipotent one, now I offer You all obeisances, falling on the ground at Your feet. O my Lord, please lead me on the right path to reach You, and since You know all that I have done in the past, please free me from the reactions to my past sins so that there will be no hindrance to my progress. \[*Śrī Īśopaniṣad*, Mantra 18\]

O Prabhupāda! Forgetfulness of you or your instructions reduces us to tiny mice in an instant.

We can choose—gratitude or arrogance—to be used by you as tools of your compassion or to be used again and again by Māyā to learn the hard lessons.

O Prabhupāda! You are beyond any mystic yogi! We are baffled and amazed by your great power! You see our faults—your vision pierces through us! You see our arrogance, but your immense compassion sustains us and then transforms us—to one day try to love Kṛṣṇa and therefore all that belongs to Kṛṣṇa, as you love Him and all that expands from Him.

O Prabhupāda! As we gather together in our communities on this Vyāsa-pūjā day, let us not forget why you came here—to change our hearts, our lives, our consciousness. Let us not make you an icon with worship, trivialize you with anecdote, and forget you and why you came here.

As you said in your Vyāsa-pūjā offering of 1936:

Herein lies the secret of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā. When we meditate on the transcendental pastimes of the Absolute Godhead, we are proud to feel that we are His eternal servitors, and we become jubilant and dance with joy....

Gentlemen, had he not appeared before us to deliver us from the thralldom of this gross worldly delusion, surely we should have remained for lives and ages in the darkness of helpless captivity. Had he not appeared before us, we would not have been able to understand the eternal truth of the sublime teaching of Lord Caitanya....

O Prabhupāda! May we thus work to remain always in your company. Please empower us with consciousness of Kṛṣṇa that we may see and act for others as you see and act for us. O Prabhupāda! How can we repay you? How can we repay the debt that can never be repaid? May our only desire be to truly hear your instructions, and within those instructions to be electrified as conduits of your compassion passing from heaven to earth.

*Your servants in Washington, D.C., USA.*  
(written by Rukmiṇī-devī dāsī)

## Zurich

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept our respected obeisance at your shelter-giving lotus feet. Your glories are sung all over the three worlds, and we humbly join the great souls in our inadequate attempt to praise you. We can only thank you.

We are eternally grateful to you for establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness on this planet earth. Had you not come, how would we have walked, slept, talked, eaten—how would we have lived without that priceless knowledge you generously gave us? The passing years enable us to realize the extent of your compassion. We cannot read the first two volumes of your biography without shedding tears. Tears for what you undertook on our behalf and tears for what we don’t have, your loving soft heart.

On this blessed day of your appearance we are beggars crying for alms. Bless us with a drop of your compassion so that we can also possess such a valuable commodity. Endowed with that jewel of qualities, we will want to share Kṛṣṇa consciousness with the unfortunate suffering souls of this planet. And your preaching mission will go on. And Lord Caitanya will rain love of Kṛṣṇa on us all.

Prabhupāda, we are so helpless. When all-powerful Māyā tests us by putting us through various circumstances, we sometimes fall victim to her, like trees falling before the mighty winds of a hurricane. Yet you never forsake us. Your love for us revives us if we so desire. To follow your instructions constitutes our desire to earn your mercy and the mercy of the previous *ācāryas*. Our consistent devotional practices re-establish our lost connection with Kṛṣṇa and reawaken our dormant love for Him. Once a drop of love for Him is experienced, He, the lover of His devotees, reciprocates. Everything else pales in comparison.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have revealed the richness of the vast Vedic culture to us. You have magnanimously bestowed on us the knowledge of Kṛṣṇa. You have made us realize the fleeting happiness and emptiness of material life. Where would we be without your loving compassion?

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, our eternal well-wisher, on this blessed day we implore your pity. We are still floundering in the dangerous ocean of Kali. Tidal waves threaten to engulf us. Cast your merciful glance in our direction. We forever beg to remain your servants. We plead for your compassion.

*Your eternal disciples and grand-disciples at the ISKCON temple in Zurich, Switzerland.*

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Brazil)

My dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer millions of obeisances to you on this most sacred day, which marks your fully spiritual appearance on this planet.

Your dear disciples tell us of how your love and kindness knew no bounds. They tell us how your activities were all laden with pure *kṛṣṇa-prema*. We read your books and study your feats and see for ourselves this truth. This fills us with profound inspiration and enthusiasm; thus we happily give ourselves more and more to serving your lotus feet. As we advance we perceive that this service is itself another manifestation of your mercy. It is not something we are giving but yet another gift we are receiving from you.

Thus we can only continuously beg for your mercy, which you are freely giving out. Empower us to do your bidding, enlighten us to remain forever at your feet, strengthen us to always serve your servants.

*Your servants at the offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in Brazil.*  
(written by Giridhārī dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Chinese)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The essence of devotional service is love and devotion. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, anyone who has experienced the love and devotion you have demonstrated for Kṛṣṇa and all the living entities cannot help but immediately give up hiss petty selfishness; anyone once filled with pride will bow his head down and feel shame for his ignorance and vanity.

Just as you once said, everything you wanted to say is in your books. Your books contain your love and devotion. They contain the result of the hard work and intense endeavor you went through to benefit the conditioned souls.

Our gratitude and love toward you are expressed by our relationship with your books—by our carefully studying them, relishing the nectar of your instructions contained in them, and sharing them with others with great eagerness through translating, printing, and distributing them. And if to achieve such a goal we, your followers, must suffer some hardships, we will courageously accept such sufferings without regret and endure them as tokens of our love and gratitude.

*Your servants at the Chinese-language offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.*

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Life’s so short, we make it very hard,  
It’s simple for the pure in heart.  
There’s a purpose, a reason that we’re here,  
more than just playing a part.  
The mission at hand has been spoken:  
that every woman and man in all lands gets a book.  
The mission of man has been woven,  
in every village and town all around the world.  
There’s a conquest of cultural concern,  
a renaissance of humankind.  
East and West in chanting and your books,  
“The world will note the change, in time.”  
The mission at hand has been spoken:  
that every woman and man in all lands gets a book.  
The mission of man has been woven,  
in every village and town all around the world.  
I can’t serve my senses anymore.  
It’s only chewing the chewed.  
My life is meant for more than this,  
fulfilling the mission of Kṛṣṇa and you!  
There’s a place beyond this mundane world,  
free from lust, anger, and greed.  
One engaged in the mission life and soul  
will reach that place with certain guarantee.  
The mission at hand has been spoken:  
that every woman and man in all lands gets a book.  
The mission of man has been woven,  
in every village and town all around the world.

*Your servants at the offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in North America.*

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

The true greatness of a devotee can be seen by how many people he can convert into devotees. Therefore he is compared to a touchstone, which turns iron into gold. You are such a touchstone because every day you touch the hearts of hundreds of thousands of people by coming to them in the form of your books. Thus you gradually transform them into devotees. Through your books they are able to get your precious instructions and divine association.

People in this Age of Kali have hardly any qualifications for becoming devotees of Kṛṣṇa. But you create their good fortune with your books and send them on their way, back home, back to Godhead. To each one of these travelers you give the opportunity of engagement in devotional service. We can all take part in your program by publishing, translating, illustrating, printing, and, most importantly, distributing your books.

Here at Korsnas Gard in Sweden, we produce many titles for the BBT. But there hasn’t been very much book distribution going on. So the temple has felt a bit vacant and quiet at times. Until recently.

Last Sunday, Raivata Prabhu, one of your disciples who was intensely preaching from this temple some twenty years ago, arrived here with a group of new devotees. They’re all from Sweden and are ready to distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness by distributing the *mahā-mantra* and your books.

Although they have not yet moved in, we’re already feeling waves of ecstasy. They are all very eager to learn the art of book distribution. It’s wonderful to see the bright and smiling faces of these young new devotees looking forward to becoming members of your transcendental army.

A temple without book distribution is like a *kīrtana* in which the *mṛdaṅga* player beats only the small head of the *mṛdaṅga*. There is some sound but it doesn’t really create waves. Book distribution is like hitting the large side of the *mṛdaṅga,* which gives the whole *kīrtana* groove and drive.

There is nothing the world needs more than your books. They contain the truth as you have given it. Now we have to get out the door and distribute. The more we give, the more we get. Let’s liberate the whole world by giving everyone Kṛṣṇa consciousness!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us regain the ecstasy of the pioneer spirit. Please guide us and help us become successful instruments in your hands. Please empower us to print and distribute more and more of your books.

There is nothing the world needs more than your books.

We are eternally indebted to you because you are so kindly engaging us in your *saṅkīrtana* mission.

*Your servants at the offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust in Northern Europe.*

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead Magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

After fifty-nine years, your magazine continues to bring enlightenment and encouragement to the souls of this age. Although it is now in our humble hands, we know that it goes on only by your mercy and by the power of your devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa. It is just one of the many projects you began, one of your many tremendous endeavors in the service of the Lord. But its endurance inspires within us the conviction that your other programs will not only endure but expand more and more, bringing countless souls to Kṛṣṇa’s feet.

We thank you for the gift of service to your magazine and your mission. Please bless us with the intelligence to understand your vision for *Back to Godhead* and with the strength to carry it out.

*Your grateful servants at Back to Godhead magazine.*

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You are so much beloved of Lord Kṛṣṇa, such a confidential servant and dear friend, that by your mercy Lord Kṛṣṇa will bless any and all who come in contact with you. Your preaching is so fixed in the Absolute Truth that you are the embodiment of the words of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura: *cakhu-dān dilo jei...divya jñān hṛde prokāśito*. You open darkened eyes and fill hearts with transcendental knowledge. These qualities of enlightenment and compassion are not to be found in this material world except in a person who is completely and utterly surrendered to Kṛṣṇa. Your surrender to Kṛṣṇa is clearly manifest, and because this is so natural to you, you make it look easy. After all, it is simply chanting, dancing, taking *prasādam,* and discussing transcendental subject matter with whomever you come in contact. Very often you would remark, “So where is the difficulty?” Recently we were reminded of your slogan “Chant and be happy.” Sometimes it seems we forget the second part of this saying—that this process is for making us happy. We are bound up in the illusion that spiritual life is hard and uncompromising and requires a grim determination to reach a success that is like a material possession. What is overlooked in this attitude is that true spiritual advancement is marked by the freedom from unwanted things, a lifting of the burdens of false identification with material designations. Only such a consciousness will lift us above temporary feelings of happiness and distress and material considerations of poverty and wealth. Your example of enthusiasm for Kṛṣṇa consciousness in all these circumstances is the beacon of detachment from the material in pure surrender to Kṛṣṇa. This year marks the twenty-fifth year since the founding of the Bhaktivedanta Archives. We are fortunate that we are able to associate with both your *vāṇī* and *vapu* on a daily basis. Even though you have left our immediate vision, the sound vibration of your voice in your tape recordings and your form displayed on slides and negatives, as well as your original manuscripts and other items, are a sacred treasure that sustains us in your absence. We are hoping to preserve this mercy for all future generations, and the salvation that this will bring to the world will be our salvation. Your mercy is our only qualification.

*Your humble servants at The Bhaktivedanta Archives.*

## Bhaktivedanta Center

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and prostrated obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You nicely explained that the *jīvas,* as *taṭastha-śakti,* have a proclivity for change, i.e., the tendency to jump from phenomenon to phenomenon, sometimes evolving materially but more often than not devolving spiritually. Technology run amok, the offspring of greed, attests to this restlessness. We see the motto “Variety is the mother of enjoyment” at work even in attempts at spiritual life, where people move on from book to new book, from service to new service. The difference is that in spiritual life bona fide spiritual instructions are eternally significant, and therefore timely. To word this colloquially, the “same old” is actually the “good old”—always relevant, and always necessary.
