Along with identifying this tendency for the conditioned souls to constantly change, you expressed your hope that we wouldn’t change what you gave us and thereby ruin everything you worked for. As our loving and doting spiritual master and father, you repeatedly admonished us not to deviate and encouraged us to wholeheartedly try to assist you. You especially asked all your disciples to be cautious in spiritual life, living very carefully so that Māyā might not snatch them from Kṛṣṇa’s hand.

You related how once when your son was a child you and he were walking on the street and it appeared that the moon was moving with you. You asked us that if the moon can “go” with us in such a way, why can’t Kṛṣṇa also remain with us via sound vibration? You concluded that He can, provided we’re qualified to keep His company. Again, we eternally thank you for providing us with a context within which such a phenomenon is possible. Otherwise, how could we have known?

As children, we’d go to the movies, and often a pair of 3-D glasses were handed out to the patrons so that they could see the movie as it had been filmed—in 3-D. Similarly, because of your unparalleled compassion, you have allowed us to “see” both gross and subtle phenomena in this world, in effect letting us see life here as it is. And you have shown us that, without service to Kṛṣṇa via the transparent medium of yourself, life would be sorely one-dimensional.

Just like renters who pay month after month for temporary quarters in which to live, the *vikarmīs* enjoy for the moment, and in the end no “equity” accrues from their efforts. Their efforts are a temporary solution, with the results ending with the material body. However, the devotee, by investing in spiritual life, accrues the equity of spiritual advancement, and when the material body is finished, that investment leads to Kṛṣṇa.

You have shown us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that without serving Kṛṣṇa there is no success in this world. However, adding Kṛṣṇa’s service to the equation ensures success.

May we always try our level best to hear, understand, and implement your divine instructions. It is our desire to always remain in the shade of your lotus shadow.

*Your paltry servants at the Bhaktivedanta Center, Charlottesville, Virginia, USA.*  
(written by Amit Ācāra dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta College

*datto ’rkāya yathā dīpo gaṅgāyai jāhnavī-jalam*  
*tathā kṛtajñatā-mālā gurave sumanarddhaye*

**Just as a lamp is offered to the sun, and Ganges water to the Ganges, so we offer this garland of gratitude to our teacher, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is the abode of abundant, flowerlike good wishes for us.**

*japa kṛṣṇa hare japa rāma hare*  
*vraja-rāja-sutāt tava nānya-sakhaḥ*  
*iti śāsana-puṣpa-cayān kirasi*  
*prabhupāda guro mama te ’stu namaḥ*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher! Obeisances to you! You shower these flowerlike instructions everywhere: “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa! Chant Hare Rāma! Other than the son of the King of Vraja, no one is your friend.”**

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance in this world. In humble gratitude for your coming to teach us the wonderful truths of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we offer you these prayers of appreciation from the depths of our hearts.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: You never gave up endeavoring to increase your service, yet you seemed so relaxed, relying on divine empowerment. In your missionary struggle and in your kind glance upon me—a spider not even using its full, insignificant capacity—I see perseverance and confidence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: By perfect example you teach the surrender of a devoted disciple. Your profound dedication to the instructions of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura gives us life. No empty words, no broken promises. You fulfill your master’s innermost desire to spread Lord Caitanya’s movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: Your words—“If you love me, I will love you”—deeply impress my heart. You are a perfect *ācārya* on a compassionate mission, heavy with the weight of the *paramparā.* You present the most valuable jewels of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, not for business or mercantile exchange, but out of love. As an exemplary professor, you did not expect a salary, only loving exchanges, love for Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: How are you so merciful to me? You give me the holy name and the perfect process for attaining Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. You explain every detail. Under your guidance I can make it—yet I take you for granted, and my limited gratitude is never adequate.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: Dangling above a life of ignorance by your mercy, I am hanging on your words. As the manifestation of the *caitya-guru*, your words express the truth inside me: what I have always felt but could never name. Who could imagine that black ink, groups of letters, could be so full of love? In your words I find the form of my heart.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher, my guide and protector, the polestar in my heart: In all regards, you take my hand. Through life’s mayhem and the pandemonium within, you sustain and reach out to me. Your causeless, nonjudgmental patience guides me onto each stepping-stone in the turbulent river of material existence. Enclosed and enwrapped in your warm and protective care, I feel indebted. With gratitude, I pray to reciprocate, to become useful, worthy, and honest.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: My heart dances with joy when I hear your penetrating voice. I was living like an animal when you appeared and relieved my distress, saying “You are not this body, but a spirit soul, and without Kṛṣṇa consciousness you are no better than a cow or an ass.” O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are teaching me the value of life, and therefore I rejoice.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: So much love and affection you shower upon me! So much wisdom I receive through your dedicated followers! Indebted to you, I beg to follow you in your service as a preacher of *gaura-vāṇī.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: You rescued me when I was young. I met your disciples and read the First Canto. The descriptions of Lord Caitanya’s life captured my mind, and I read them again and again. The paintings, “windows to the spiritual world,” attracted my heart. In many respects I’m still a child being rescued and nourished by your Bhaktivedanta purports, which are as sweet and tasty as milk, *burfi,* and *kṣīra*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher: You have only my best interests at heart, and not considering my past, you offer full shelter. Being Lord Kṛṣṇa’s dear associate, you always know what is best for those under your shelter. You provide all means for us to qualify ourselves as your servants, and when we are properly educated and reformed, you will deliver us to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

*Your indebted servants and students at Bhaktivedanta College, Rāḍhadesh, Belgium.*

\[The special literary quality of this offering is that it is a composite of voices: Each college student contributed a paragraph, and their Sanskrit teacher, Rādhikā Ramaṇa dāsa, wrote the invocation.\]

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

**Education Redefined**

“ISKCON, FISKCON, just bring students.”  
Bring from where you can find.  
Let them have a chance to experience  
Education redefined.  
Colleges of technology are everywhere,  
Students entering in a line.  
What is the use? You boldly challenge—  
Education redefined.  
Schools as slaughterhouses are killing  
Their hearts, and souls, and minds.  
Let them understand Kṛṣṇa—  
Education redefined.  
Truthfulness, cleanliness, austerity, and mercy—  
Principles for humankind.  
Where are they teaching these values?  
Education redefined.  
You spoke of revolution, of changing the world;  
you spoke of a land beyond time.  
Teach them to serve the Lord of all Lords—  
Education re-defined.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us the key,  
The goal of education outlined.  
Please help us to honor your vision—  
Education re-defined.  
We pray for your mercy and shelter,  
For your guidance along the fine line,  
For your wisdom and purity and strength  
In presenting your “education redefined.”

*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School in Vṛndāvana, India.*  
(written by Ānanda Vṛndāvaneśvarī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Hospital

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept humble obeisances from all the devotees at Bhaktivedanta Hospital. All glories to you!

Bhaktivedanta Hospital is a miracle of your mercy. Actually, it is a house of many miracles, which are ever increasing. This special mercy of yours has given us an opportunity to work in a Kṛṣṇa conscious environment in the association of sincere devotees and simultaneously maintain our families. We are among the few souls who have this unique opportunity, due to your unlimited grace. We are participating in what is considered the second part of your mission, the establishment of *varṇāśrama-dharma*.

For us, your presence in the hospital is a reality. We are thoroughly convinced that you live here and actually run the hospital. Everybody comes in here by your desire, stays here as a patient or in some other role by your desire, and leaves this place only by your desire. You are the complete sovereign master of Bhaktivedanta Hospital. To those who have faith, this is a reality. To others, it is a mystery.

There are so many unlimited jewels that you created by the touch of your mercy. And many, many of them who are your disciples come to us for medical treatment or as visitors. Since we never got a chance to meet you personally, we always wonder what it would be like to be with you. It is only after Bhaktivedanta Hospital was established that we were awarded this unique fortune of serving your disciples at our doorstep. We have the unique blessing of serving your beloved disciples when they actually need it. And through the service we render them we perceive your presence and receive your association. Actually, we can see the unbelievable variegatedness of your personality when we see the variety of divine qualities in different disciples of yours. We officially and earnestly thank you for providing us with an opportunity to serve your disciples, and we pray to you that this opportunity keeps growing eternally for us.

So many patients naturally bow down to you in the hospital. They may not know their great fortune of having a chance to bow down to a pure devotee. But nonetheless, everyone who comes in is blessed with your mercy. Your presence gives them hope in life and confidence of getting cured. I have seen many people standing with folded hands in front of you and shedding tears. How can we ever fathom the limits of your mercy?

We just heard that many devotees all over the world have reconstructed their damaged spiritual lives just by hearing about Bhaktivedanta Hospital. This is simply amazing!!

His Grace Kulādri Prabhu was here with us this year in the month of March. He said, “After visiting the hospital, I am again feeling the excitement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness that I used to feel when Śrīla Prabhupāda was on this planet.” Her Grace Ūrmilā Mātājī said, “Bhaktivedanta Hospital has given me the hope that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission has unlimited scope for expansion.” His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami said, “Bhaktivedanta Hospital is one of the most important projects in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission.” His Grace Brahmānanda Prabhu said, “Whenever Śrīla Prabhupāda criticized construction of a hospital, he did not mean Bhaktivedanta Hospital. I am pretty convinced that Śrīla Prabhupāda is extremely happy with this hospital project.” His Holiness Devāmṛta Swami said, “There is a perfect Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere at Bhaktivedanta Hospital.” We feel that all these great souls are just expressing our good fortune and trying to give us an understanding of the spiritual depths of Bhaktivedanta Hospital.

Of late, in the month of April, a Vrajavāsī from Varṣāṇā named Murāri was in the hospital with his two kids, who were blinded by congenital cataract. Both children were operated on in the hospital and are able to see now for the first time in their lives. During one of the afternoons when Murāri was resting, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to him in a dream and asked him, “Are you feeling all right? Is everything fine with your kids? Are you hungry? Please take these sweets.” And you gave him a box of sweets. That convinced us that you are personally concerned and directly supervising the welfare of all the patients in the hospital. Due to your unlimited grace, we also have the opportunity to serve Vrajavāsīs in the hospital and at Varṣāṇā through the yearly Cataract Surgery Camps.

Rubina Khan, a twenty-four-year-old Muslim girl, attempted suicide and in the process suffered burns over ninety percent of her body. She was brought to the hospital in a very serious condition. Our spiritual counselor told her relatives to get the Koran and read to her, and our spiritual care nurse started reading your *Bhagavad-gītā* to her. After reciting the whole *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* to her, the spiritual care nurse went to take rest in her room, but within two hours Rubina Khan personally asked for that nurse to again read the *Bhagavad-gītā*. She heard the full *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* for a second time. On the third day, the difference between the body and the soul was explained to Rubina. On the fourth day she reached a critical stage. Her relatives said, “Please continue your spiritual care services. We will pray outside.” Rubina Khan passed away in the presence of the spiritual counsellor and the spiritual care nurse, hearing your *kīrtana,* with Tulasī leaf and Rādhā-kuṇḍa water in her mouth. Such is your unlimited, all-encompassing mercy, which knows no barriers of caste and creed. With your blessings we dream of establishing a Department of Spiritual Care in each and every hospital of the world. What is not possible if your mercy is there?

We pray to you earnestly that we ever remain grateful to you, in full awareness of all the mercy you have showered upon us, and that we not get lost in the whirlwind of positions, prestige, power, and politics. We pray to remain at your lotus feet as your humble servants. Let us always remain an insignificant part of your glorious mission.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda !

*Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Hospital, Mumbai, India.*

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our repeated humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The dust from your lotus feet can purify a man of all of his *anarthas* (unwanted habits). This dust especially comes in the form of your transcendental instructions. If we want to make advancement in spiritual life, we must make these instructions our heart and soul. These instructions can be found within your fully potent and living books, as well as within your lectures.

The availability of Your Divine Grace’s lectures are a special mercy for this technological age. The scientific enterprise has often resulted in horrible works meant to destroy the world. It would seem that technological advancement benefits a certain class of people while leaving another class unemployed and the environment polluted. Nevertheless, the same technological advancements have made it possible for everyone in the world to hear or read your transcendental words. Many great empowered personalities appeared in the past, but we cannot hear their divine instructions directly from their lips. Their instructions are always filtered through the minds of men with material motives. However, scientific advancement has made it possible to hear your words.

Similarly, we can appreciate your form by watching the fifty hours of video of Your Divine Grace.

Finally, you have left your divine books, which are full of transcendental instructions. The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* speaks of how it will bring about a revolution in the lives of men. We see now that your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is actually bringing about a revolution in the lives of men and women who were otherwise addicted to meat-eating, intoxicants, illicit sex, and gambling. This revolution has happened despite a powerful propaganda machine built out of television, billboards, and magazines featuring intelligently crafted advertisements designed to impel people to engage in sense gratification. Science and technology has made this terrible propaganda machine possible, but science and technology has also made it possible to publish and distribute millions of spiritual time bombs in the form of your transcendental books. Every one of these books will bring about a revolution. Each book has either changed the heart of someone or is waiting on someone’s bookshelf until Kṛṣṇa sends someone to pick it up and read it. It is said in the *śāstra* that in the Golden Age of Lord Caitanya everyone will become a *harijana,* a devotee of Hari. With millions of your books in print, it is not difficult to see how this will happen. We simply have to be patient and cooperate with your divine family.

In addition to showing how to use science and technology (even as they have been developed at present) in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa, making them *hari-sambandhi-vastu*, you also established the Bhaktivedanta Institute to revolutionize the very structure and method of modern science. Your aim was to develop a new science and technology, which would arise as a logical *next* step in the development of the materialists’ own science (*ājagara-vṛtti*), by the application of the *acintya-bhedābheda* principle. Thus you said:

Theory is not science. I can propose some theory, “It is like that.” But that is not science. Science means observation and experiment. That is science. You observe how the rules are working, and when you practically bring them into experiment, then it is science. If you simply theorize, that is not science. Mental speculation. It has no benefit. You can speculate, constructing a castle in the air. That is not a very good thing. **You should present something which will benefit the people, and practical. That is science**.” \[Conversation, 9 May 1975, Perth; emphasis added\]

This new material science (by *karmī* standards) and resulting technology would provide a much more environment-friendly use of material resources to fulfill people’s material needs, and it would also be fully compatible with the spirituality of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. To indicate how to do this, in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.34, you have also revealed, with stunning transcendental foresight and insight: “This verse is the potential basis of great scientific research work, for it explains how subtle forms are generated from the ethereal element, what their characteristics and actions are, and how the tangible elements, namely air, fire, water and earth, are manifested from the subtle form.” By your mercy we are now seeing the full import of this verse beginning to manifest in front of our eyes through the work we have been able to carry out in the most fundamental area of modern science, namely quantum mechanics. A Nobel Laureate in quantum physics has commented on the work of the Institute, remarking that “this is the way science will be done in the future.” Indeed, by your mercy, this work has also enabled the BI to pioneer the start of a brand-new field within science, called “consciousness studies,” which is *not* a matter-based study of consciousness (as the misguided *karmī* scholars think), but rather a consciousness-based study of matter. This field can therefore be scientific in the current sense of the word and yet be spiritually compatible.

All this scientific work is unfolding entirely by your guidance, vision, and protection and is a great fortune for the scientists of this world and for every one. Out of your causeless mercy, kindly allow us to continue to be part of your great pastimes, to eternally serve you with ever-increasing enthusiasm and humility.

Begging to be servants in your divine plan,

*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute, Berkeley, California, USA.*

## Bhaktivedanta Research Center (Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I would like to express my gratitude to you from my very heart. I would like the whole world to know about you: your life, your deeds, your teachings. You daily donated your life with generosity, compassion, and coherence as a service to God. Thus you lived to promote the supreme good for all creatures; this gave stimulus and infinite inspiration to countless people of good will. Your teachings on universal and eternal truths provide real solutions to existential problems related to birth and death in this deluding and deluded world—a world beset by conflict and suffering—and thus you have given a full sense to life.

It is stated in the Vedas: *yato vāco nivartante/ aprāpya manasā saha:* The mind and words cannot reach Brahman. Not mere thinking, nor mere talking, are enough for us to realize the Absolute. The conditioned mind, cloudy and distorted, uninterruptedly produces pairs of opposites (*rāga-dveṣa*); these would cause restlessness and unbroken suffering unless, by God’s mercy, we were given in life the chance to meet and serve the lotus feet of a pure devotee of the Lord. Only then is it possible to gradually gain awareness of the opportunity offered by the human form, namely, to go back to the spiritual world, our eternal abode. Serving your mission is my precious opportunity and my sweetest delight.

It is now twenty-seven years since I first met you; with ever-fresh wonder I realize that, regardless of the intervening time and quantity of events, your revered figure and your enlightened teachings are clearly impressed in my consciousness, pervading it completely.

Since I met you, I have so often looked for you in my heart. At times I simply evoke the memory of that first meeting, contemplating how those first teachings are deeply carved in my soul and constantly lead my choices, shaping and structuring my life up to now.

From time to time, while looking for a possible further meaning of that first moment, I recognize with joy that the teachings you gave me then, together with their practical application in daily life, have never stopped bearing fruit. This is my desire forever: May the glorious Lord of our hearts disclose to me the taste and the science for surrendering unto your feet, so that I can succeed in loving you and purely serving you.

Now and again I have reviewed my relationship with you throughout the years and analyzed the various moments of my spiritual consciousness at every crucial step in this life; I have thus become conscious of the enormous benefit of being perpetually linked to you through service and affection. This has allowed me to keep my balance in moments of both attraction and repulsion, and so to remain free from euphoria or depression. My lord and master, please allow me the mercy of feeling and understanding reality so that I can forever serve it without hesitation.

I have verified my level of sincerity toward you throughout the decades, at times feeling forced and reluctant, at other times courageous and free from masks, and so I discovered within me various kinds of uncertainties. Right then I would immediately go back to your teachings, finding them enlivening, dissipating the clouds and establishing the light of clear consciousness, allowing my rapid improvement both in thought and behavior. My never-ending gratitude goes to you for being my constant model of life.

In the morning when I wake I perceive your saintly presence within my heart, and my consciousness takes lightly to the air, completely satisfied; received daily, your mercy is limitless.

May Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the glorious Lord of the worlds, continue to inspire me and guide me in my future choices, enabling me to completely dedicate my life to your mission and your saintly personality in the company of good Vaiṣṇavas.

I offer my heart, my words, and my service unto you.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the Bhaktivedanta Research Center, Italy.*  
(written by Matsya-avatāra dāsa)

## Cleveland Nāma Haṭṭa

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances.

You certainly built this house in which we can all live peacefully! The mercy you have bestowed upon us is actually unfathomable. Still, we must endeavor for the rest of our eternal lives to show our appreciation for you. Kindly accept this song as an offering from our hearts to yours.

O dear Prabhupāda, we pledge our hearts to thee,  
For you have given us the key  
To life eternal, peace, joy and freedom and  
Because of you, pure devotees we shall be.  
You walked this earth in absolute reality,  
A true personification of love and humility.  
You asked nothing for yourself,  
But give life to all else,  
O renounced one, revered one, renowned one.  
So tell us, Śrī Guru,  
What can we do for you?  
What can one give for the gift of life?  
Just let us always be surrendered at your lotus feet,  
And surely then Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s favor we’ll meet.  
You take our hearts and mold them to Kṛṣṇa’s will.  
Now on them they will bear the seal  
Of Kṛṣṇa consciousness—that’s love of God.

All glories to Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your devotees at the Nāma Haṭṭa Program in Cleveland, Ohio, USA.*  
(written by Kṛṣṇānandiṇī-devī dāsī)

## College Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and humble obeisances on this sacred day of your Vyāsa-pūjā.

When a sincere seeker of truth begins to embark upon the spiritual quest, there seems to develop a thirst for cultivating one’s inner life. In the purport to Text 4 of the *Upadeśāmṛta,* you elaborate on this principle very nicely by quoting and commenting on *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā,* 22.107:

*nitya-siddha kṛṣṇa-prema ‘sādhya’ kabhu naya*  
*śravaṇādi-śuddha-citte karaye udaya*

**“Pure love for Kṛṣṇa is eternally established in the hearts of the living entities. It is not something to be gained from another source. When the heart is purified by hearing and chanting, this love naturally awakens.”**

You then comment on this verse,

Since Kṛṣṇa consciousness is inherent in every living entity, everyone should be given a chance to hear about Kṛṣṇa....Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not artificially imposed upon the heart; it is already there.

In this regard, Lord Caitanya has empowered you to build a sacred home in which all sincere seekers can take shelter of such a genuine inner life. And how is the life force maintained within such a home? Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī gives the ingredients in Text 4:

*dadāti pratigṛhṇāti guhyam ākhyāti pṛcchati*  
*bhuṅkte bhojayate caiva ṣaḍ-vidhaṁ prīti-lakṣaṇam*

**“Offering gifts in charity, accepting charitable gifts, revealing one’s mind in confidence, inquiring confidentially, accepting** ***prasādam,*** **and offering** ***prasādam*** **are the six symptoms of love shared by one devotee and another.”**

You then comment,

The life of the Kṛṣṇa conscious society is nourished by these six types of loving exchange among the members; therefore people must be given the chance to associate with the devotees of ISKCON because simply by reciprocating in the six ways mentioned above, an ordinary man can fully revive his dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You mention the word “nourished”; in this regard we can understand that we will be malnourished if these six symptoms are neglected. As a result, one will become spiritually depleted and feel a lack of substance along the path, and what to speak of giving the substance of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti* to others.

By your divine guidance, may we fully learn and take shelter of these loving exchanges along our day-to-day Kṛṣṇa conscious journey, and may we invite all those we meet to come and experience the sweet nourishment of *sādhu-saṅga*. Thus that inner life we are all seeking will naturally manifest as we tune into the eternal loving service to Śrī Guru, Śrī Gaurāṅga, and the Vaiṣṇavas. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being such a wonderful guardian as we endeavor to spread Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* mission.

*Your servants in College TSKP.*  
(written by Tuṣṭa Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

## Festival of India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances and this attempt to capture a pure expression of gratitude.

We certainly are indebted to you for your loving guidance and limitless patience.

We are all so often in a mad frenzy of passion, trying to fulfill our desire to serve you. From your perspective our activities are no more than those of playful children acting out imagined forms of happiness and distress. It is only by your divine grace that we can begin to comprehend the eternal privilege you have provided for every individual. Thank you.

The message of Kṛṣṇa is reaching the world by your mercy. You have sent the Absolute Truth to humanity at large and have so kindly given us the opportunity to be engaged in assisting you by delivering it to everyone. Certainly we are not worthy of this golden gift, but you have entrusted us with carrying out your will; therefore, you have offered us the option of becoming trustworthy. This is the power so often referred to in other terms.

You know who we are. Surely, you are aware of our weaknesses and shortcomings; nonetheless, you send us out into the world as your representatives, messengers of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. How wonderful it is that we are preserved and maintained by our feeble endeavors to please you!

Now, as for our specific function of serving your servants in the atmosphere of festive presentation, we can compare the struggle we face to that faced on an isolated battlefront. The opposition, our conditioning, is formidable and well aware of our failings. This contest is always one of inner turmoil. The enemy is our own weakness of heart. The external challenges are nothing compared with the undermining factor of being cowardly. We gather our strength, the strength that you have liberally afforded. We are armed and trained but without courage if we do not apply ourselves to adhering to your instructions.

By example, you have taught that our power increases in proportion to our ability to give our wealth of knowledge and by encouraging ourselves and others to step up to leadership.

We may even fall into the snare of illusion that informs us that we are abandoned and helpless. Are we hopelessly countering the villainous conspirators of demoniac nature alone? Who will we blame for this delusion? Leadership is the priceless inheritance that you have bestowed upon us, and yet out of misguided apprehension we seek to retreat. “I am not a leader!” we may declare, trying to cop out. But this responsibility of leadership you have given all of us, and all who will come in the future. We are by your definition not your “followers”; we must be your “leaders” and immediately give courage to all comers to become leaders also. There is no place for fear in the sampling of Vaikuṇṭha. If fear and intimidation and discouragement are detected, they must assertively be driven out, banished from our lives forever. It is our God-given, Kṛṣṇa-given pleasure/duty to serve you and your desires.

O pure devotee, fearless captain of our ship,  
steer us clear of hazards in our relationships.  
With the utmost obligation and affection,

*Your hopefully dauntless servants in the Festival of India.*  
(written by Phaṇi Bhūṣaṇa dāsa)

## Global Varṇāśrama Educational Social and Cultural Organization (GLOVESCO)

**Mission Statement**

To help promote and establish the universal concepts and principles of *varṇāśrama-dharma* for the benefit of all individuals in society. This can best be achieved within an agrarian-based social structure, with special emphasis on family, culture, education, cow protection, and devotion.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Last year we sent you our Mission Statement and our Mission Goals. Since then various individuals have endeavored to help promote the ideals set forth in our first offering. Some communication among GLOVESCO members has been on-going, and new members have signed up, but it seems we have accomplished little in this last year.

We remain convinced, however, that your vision for *varṇāśrama* communities, in particular rural communities, remains the guiding principle for a more stable and prosperous ISKCON—in fact, for a better global social and spiritual order. As you have often reminded us, the *varṇāśrama* system is given directly by Lord Kṛṣṇa: “According to the three modes of material nature and the work ascribed to them, the four divisions of human society are created by Me.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.13) It is therefore a most perfect and most scientific system. We somehow remain very slow in putting it into practice.

Devotees from South Africa, West Africa, North and South America, Europe, and Southeast Asia have been inspired by the humble efforts undertaken by members at GLOVESCO. We are happy to see that other individuals and groups of devotees are also taking your *varṇāśrama* mission more seriously, and we hope their efforts will also inspire others to become more involved in *varṇāśrama* development.

Part of *varṇāśrama* development is the *varṇāśrama* college. We are pleased to see that efforts are underway in North America (Los Angeles) to establish such a *varṇāśrama* college. We are also happy to report that similar efforts are underway here in Indonesia through the efforts of the Nārāyaṇa Smṛti Educational Foundation, based in Yogyakarta.

What follows are excerpts from letters we have received from devotees regarding the importance of *varṇāśrama* development and their involvement. We hope these excerpts will please you and devotees worldwide.

I am happy to read about your efforts to establish *varṇāśrama dharma* and to coordinate a worldwide cooperation between the devotees for this goal. It seems that the Lord put me into a situation where I am given big responsibility regarding developing *varṇāśrama dharma....*I was sure that somehow this cooperation will start....I would like to help to establish *varṇāśrama* worldwide if I can be of any use considering my circumstances. I am happy with the idea of *varṇāśrama* college as well. \[January 2003, from Gaura Śakti dāsa, Hungary\]

January 2003 will mark the beginning of classes at the Varṇāśrama College here in New Dvārakā (ISKCON of Los Angeles). The first class will be based on the first chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. It will include all the verses, purports, and classes given by His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. \[January 2003, Rabīndranātha dāsa, Los Angeles\]

I am doing my Masters in Business Administration....I am now seeing how the Globalised economy has worked to the detriment of most underdeveloped economies, with special reference to African economies, and that Varṇāśrama, which is based on localized economy, is more stable and sustainable, also with a moral and spiritual focus. I am really very impressed and interested in this vast and varied subject. \[February 2, 2003, from Prabhupāda-līlā dāsa, Nigeria, West Africa\]

I have had an excellent time with devotees at Gītā-nāgarī Baru. Though the visit was for less than 24 hrs, living in such an isolated place with simple people following KC practices as Śrīla Prabhupāda taught makes one watch his or her own self beyond comparison to any hatha yoga or psychic analysis program. \[March 5, 2003, from Gopīnātha dāsa, Secunderabad, India\]

This is from Bhaktin Jenna (she came through Yoga class). “Wow!!! What an incredible site! Thank you for sending it to me.” \[March 5, 2003, from Ānanda-devī dāsī, North Carolina, USA\]

Moreover, I am interested to become a Coordinator for the Philippines. Though I presently reside here in Cagayan de Oro City, Northern Mindanao, I frequently visit Manila, where we have a clearing office. Most of ITG-The Group’s clients are in Manila. Please let me know how I can become a Coordinator of Glovesco. \[March 19, 2003, from Bhakta Rūpa dāsa, the Philippines\]

I pray that we can be able to make this forum very active, as there is a great need to help one another to consolidate our ISKCON projects around the world through the wonderful Vedic social system that Śrīla Prabhupāda has introduced to us. We can actually change the world because we definitely have the necessary tools to do it, but we must start with ourselves first, individually and collectively. It takes a changed person to change another. \[March 26, 2003, from Varāha dāsa, Ivory Coast, West Africa\]

It is really nice to know that there is some information out there by ISKCON devotees that can help us understand the practical application of *varṇāśrama* more....It excites me to see that there are devotees working towards establishing a firm cultural foundation for generations to come. I can also see how it would make a great difference in our preaching if we could actually pull it off. \[April 13, 2003, from Bhakta Joe, Cape Town, South Africa\]

Hare Kṛṣṇa. I want to tell you that I am very happy because I am trying to make service in a web page named http://www.glovesco.org, and here we can serve your lotus feet in a nice way. We can help the cows, for example, and it is ecstatic. \[April 15, 2003, from Mādhava Prasāda-devī dāsī, Argentina\]

It has come to my attention that in today’s political and weather situations I believe our beloved leader (Śrīla Prabhupāda) was right...soon the cities will be destroyed, and unless we have our farms and cows and grains we will not survive. A farm is not just to provide food sufficiency in the household but is self-reliance and a sense of peace. A farm is not just a place but a way of life. It is a spiritual experience and a profound love of the land and love of deity and a joy. We can see God in the land and the inter-connectedness of all aspects of the world. And the joy and oneness of the world, rain and birds, and growing things. Farming is an act of worship. \[April 15, 2003, from, Carol, Arizona, North America\]

Your servants at the Global *varṇāśrama* Educational, Social, and Cultural Organization (GLOVESCO).  
(written by Bhakti Rāghava Swami)

## Hare Krishna School

**Pauṇḍraka Prabhupādas**

Undaunted, Prabhupāda, you struggled alone  
Godbrothers or followers here where you roamed.  
Then you met Wally, Bruce, Howard, and Keith;  
You preached the *Gītā,* Māyā to defeat.

Descriptions of tolerance, humility, and peace—  
You attract us now to your lotus feet.  
Unqualified, proud, and in disgrace,  
We stand before you with sullen face.

Openingdoors of spiritual perception,  
You shifted reality beyond conception.  
Kṛṣṇa is supreme, there is no doubt;  
Impersonal concoctions all tossed out.

Transforming lives with your mystic perfection  
Making effulgent disciples of every description,  
You preached the message of Mahāprabhu  
To a fallen audience so attached to you.

Your position is unique in the mission:  
To establish the Gauḍīya *siddhānta* conclusion.  
Many have come to claim the right  
To inherit from you your ISKCON might.

Parasites surfaced to feed on the remnants—  
Unwanted intrusions, a point of contention.  
Minimizing your position and undermining,  
Like flies looking for filth, always fault-finding.

Pauṇḍraka Prabhupādas will come and go,  
With their esoteric *līlā* and fancy show.  
Bewildering the innocent, philosophy unfounded,  
A soft alternative to those not grounded.

Emotional callings, irrational outbursts,  
Divergent teachings of the outcastes.  
Devotional service under the modes,  
Pridefully preaching esoteric codes.

A *sādhu*is peaceful, respectful, content—  
No need to hanker or lament.  
He accepts responsibility for all his actions,  
Sees Kṛṣṇa working in all situations.

Your message so clear and uncomplicated:  
Four regulative principles, as you stated;  
Chant the holy names sixteen rounds without fail  
Back home to Godhead you will sail.

On this day your followers strengthen  
In faith, trust, love, and association.  
ISKCON is yours: we’ll not tolerate  
Pauṇḍraka Prabhupādas at your gate.

Please continue to shower upon us all  
Your merciful glance, we seek nothing more.

*Your aspiring servants Hare Krishna School, Auckland, New Zealand.*  
(written by Prāṇa dāsa)

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, our spiritual master, our savior!

I wanted to tell you of a most unusual experience we had due to your mercy.

Jitendra, one of our oxen, was saved from the slaughterhouse about six years ago as a result of a group effort by the New Vṛndāvan cow department and Mr. Kamalesh Shah. Since his rescue from slaughter, Jitendra has lived a wonderful life on our farm, along with twenty-three other cows and oxen. He and his friends have access to seventy-five acres of pasture during the spring, summer, and fall, and a nice pole barn to shelter them in the winter and the heat of summer days. Even in the winter the cows are able to move freely from the barn to the pastures for exercise, which is different from most cold-climate barn setups.

The morning after Balabhadra arrived home from India, he saw Jitendra below the barn on the other side of the fence and went to investigate. There was Jitendra with his leg stretched out from his body and caught in the fence. Although the fence had been replaced with high tensile wire and was not barbed wire, Jitendra’s foot was badly cut. Balabhadra cut Jitendra out of the fence, and Jitendra lay on the ground unable to use his badly cut leg.

After his wound had been cleaned, the opinion of most was to let him lay there, and perhaps he would heal and get up and walk away. Since he weighed about two thousand pounds, it would take some heavy equipment to move him. The ground had thawed, which would make it difficult for such equipment to operate without getting stuck. Since we live in West Virginia, which is very hilly, moving him back to the barn, which was uphill, would have been quite a feat and also very traumatizing for him. We tried to make him comfortable with hay to sit upon, food, and water to drink. But he stopped eating almost from the beginning. Each day he struggled to get up, but the leg had no feeling or strength. So we waited a few days, praying he would get better. But every time we checked on him, he was not eating. He was also trying to move and therefore slipping down hill.

Balabhadra decided to move him. He first built a sled to put him on so that he could be pulled uphill. To build the sled took a day, since we had to be sure it would be strong enough and comfortable enough to cause the least amount of trauma and stress. When that was completed, we went to check on Jitendra the following morning. The weather had gotten cold again, and we wanted to move him in the early morning, while the ground was still hard and before it warmed up and began to thaw so the tractor would not get stuck and make things even worse. Jitendra had managed to get himself into a gully and was lying on his side in the gully’s freezing cold water.

I stayed with him chanting and holding his head in my arms trying to calm him. I felt so powerless, since I seemed unable to reach his consciousness. In the meantime Phil, a local employee of the temple, and a few devotees from the temple had been called to come and help us move Jitendra to the barn, where he would be safer and more comfortable.

First we maneuvered the sled under him and rolled him on to it, and then we pulled him out with the tractor. He was so big and heavy that it took all of us with all our strength to move him. Then ahead was the uphill climb to the barn, a distance of about three hundred yards. Phil had built a pulley system that he attached to the tractor and the sled. Balabhadra sat with Jitendra on the sled, and Phil began to drive the tractor uphill.

I ran back to the house to get some blankets for Jitendra, since he was shivering. But I couldn't move very fast because there were still high drifts of snow in some areas and I was already exhausted. When I succeeded in getting the blankets and made it back to the pasture, the sled was half way up to the barnyard. I prayed that the sled would hold together, since the barnyard was full of mud due to the previous cold weather and subsequent warm temperatures.

All the cows and oxen were all around Balabhadra and Jitendra, following them up to the top of the hill to the barn. When Jitendra had been down on the other side of the fence, they would come to the fence and moo and fuss, going back and forth along the fence line.

Then we maneuvered the tractor and pulled him into the manger part of the barn, again using the pulley system, which had been attached to the rafters of the barn. We made a bed of hay and rolled Jitendra off the sled onto the hay. At this point, after four hours of hard work, we were all exhausted. Jitendra did not look well; the trip was traumatic for him. For Jitendra, such a big ox, to be unable to walk was very frightening for him. The other cows and oxen calmed down the minute we got Jitendra in the manger. Balabhadra kept saying to me how it was so amazing how they calmed down, as if they knew we were doing all we could for Jitendra and he was being taken care of.

We got a tape recorder that played you chanting twenty-four hours a day the whole time Jitendra was with us. From the beginning, the prognosis was not good. He seemed to know that, for he did not want to eat and drink. We chanted to him and stayed with him as much as we could for ten days before he passed away. He was happier in the barn. The cows would visit him, and sometimes it even seemed that we disturbed him when we came to visit, for he was listening to you chanting and he was calm.

It was so difficult to be unable to reverse his fate. But on the positive side, he had ten days to meditate on transcendental sound vibrations and prepare for death. It was comforting to know that he was not terrified, as he was when he was in the gulley. We knew that it would take a miracle to change his fate, so the whole purpose in moving him was to give his soul the opportunity to enter into the next life peacefully and hopefully focused on Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Although his death was glorious and his life exceptional, I would be untruthful to say that his fate did not leave us with sadness and a great sense of loss. This experience makes me wonder, Is cow protection just as much for us humans as it is for the cows? Do we not benefit by being around these great souls and helping them on their journey? Do we not have the chance to develop patience, tolerance, and humility (all qualities necessary for advancement in spiritual life) by taking care of them?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, who can understand the extent of your mercy upon us, who before we met you had eaten many Jitendras? There is no way to measure your love for us, and in the measured breaths that we have, may we spend them all helping you bring your message of cow protection to this world.

*Your servants at the International Society for Cow Protection and the ISKCON Ministry for Cow Protection and Agriculture.*  
(written by Chayā-devī dāsī)

## ISKCON Prison Ministry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliverer of the fallen souls. His mercy turns the dumb into eloquent speakers and enables the lame to cross mountains.”**

On several occasions I have been challenged, even by those who call themselves devotees, “Who has started all this prison preaching? They committed the crimes. Let them pay.”

Apparently, we are not even welcome to give prisoners access to the tools of self-realization and Vaiṣṇava-saṅga. I can only thank Kṛṣṇa this was not your mood! You revealed your real mood in a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture you gave in Māyāpur in September of 1974:

So although Kṛṣṇa has created this material world for the condemned persons, still He wants to see that they are having their necessities of life...so that they may live prosperously....But at last, let them come back home, back to home, back to Godhead....

Just like the prisoners: they are condemned, and the government puts them into jail. So for their criminality they are punished within the jail. But the government does not want that these prisoners may remain perpetually within the jail....Sometimes the government arranges nice instruction, classes....We were invited by the jail authorities to speak \[in Ahmedabad\]....So I was given a place just in front of the room where Gandhi was imprisoned. That room is considered as sacred. So government makes such arrangement that “These prisoners may be corrected.”...

So here also, we are first-class, second-class, third-class prisoners. Therefore we find so many varieties of living condition. But we are all prisoners.

Who began this compassionate campaign to bless prisoners? You did, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Before you came to free the inmates of America you were preaching to prisoners in the Tihar jail in Delhi in 1962, and you wrote to the jail authority:

Dear Sri Puri,

Yesterday evening I have come to Vrindaban for a flying visit and shall return to Delhi on Monday 4/6/62 to attend the Jayal meeting on Tuesday 5/6/62 morning. I am glad to see that my Thursday lectures have brought some changes in the mind of the young offenders. If the lectures are continued I am sure to turn these offenders into saintly characters. The means which I have adopted is spiritual and it works \[more\] quickly than any material means. If you give me chance to speak to all the members of the Jayal, it is quite possible for me to turn them into ideal characters.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, since last year we have had zero setbacks and hundreds and thousands of victories. These letter excerpts are just a few:

**His Words Speak to Me**

Dear Candrasekhara dasa,

Chanting has become a very important part of my day. To serve our Blessed Lord Krishna is my deep longing. Thank you for sending “The Foolish Guru.” I eat up everything I can get my hands on by His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda! He really is giving me life through his writings. He has slowly become my loving teacher. Even though His Divine Grace has past on, I feel he is with me, all through the day. Every time I face something, his words speak to me. My love for him keeps growing deeper.

Your Servant,  
Bhakta Brian Bachman,  
Fairton, NJ

**Appreciating Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dear Mother Shyama Priya,

I have been very much enlightened by the teachings of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. I have read many small pamphlets and also the work entitled “The Science of Self-Realization.” I am now reading the Bhagavad-Gita As It Is. I have been a student of Theology for many years, so I am captivated by Swami Prabhupāda’s teachings.

Bhakta Eric Jones,  
Parchman, Mississippi

**A Life Changed**

Dear Mother Shyama Priya

I’m still chanting like crazy and enjoying it greatly. I don’t know why Kṛṣṇa gives me so much joy when I’m so fallen. I’m very thankful to Him for all the little things He gives me. Blue sky, green grass to sit on while I chant in the daytime, and most of all wonderful people to write to.

They say that I’m lucky because I’m happy and when I tell them why, they don’t believe me, by not having sex, coffee, or smoking, and they turn away. They say over 50% of the men in this prison are having sex, 90% smoke and drink coffee or tea, unreal!

I started a new thing. There is a table in the lobby of my building, and when men move or go home they leave their books, so I got Prabhupāda’s books and put them on the table with my name and cell number in them. When a man gets one I tell him what a nice book it is, and when he was done with it to please return it, so other men could read it. I have three men I’ve talked to before who are waiting for the Gita. Maybe I will win one over for Kṛṣṇa. I sure hope so, for I’ve had bad luck every other way I’ve tried. I remember reading that Prabhupāda wanted books passed out and that’s what I want to do.

I never get tired of reading Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, though my memory is getting bad. I’m just falling apart, just part of the AIDS. I have bad days, but they are for me, to get closer to Kṛṣṇa—more chanting, reading and praying. I feel sad for those who have Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books and never read them or don’t chant.

Your Servant,  
Bhakta Ken,  
San Luis Obispo, California

One time a thief approached Kṛṣṇa and asked for His jewels. “No, My mother would get angry.” But the thief stayed around in the village, waiting for his chance to steal the jewels. In due course he fell in love with that beautiful dark boy, and then Kṛṣṇa offered, “Here, take the jewels,” but he refused, saying he was happy simply in Kṛṣṇa’s company. Our only credit here at ISKCON Prison Ministry is that we introduce thieves and rogues to you. You do the rest. You are still present like a touchstone of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in your books. We are amazed by the transformations in character, but after all, you told us, “This is my magic.”

The other day an inmate asked me, “What is your favorite verse?” Śrīla Prabhupāda, I don’t know what my favorite verse is, but I think of you when I hear Svarūpa Dāmodara’s prayer to Lord Caitanya: “I gave up Your company, but You did not give me up. By Your merciful rope, You have bound me by the neck and brought me back again to Your lotus feet.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta Madhya* 10.125)

In Australia a prominent disciple fell away and you said that may happen due to Māyā’s influence, but the spiritual master will never forsake him. My young son Kiśora told me, “Pita, I try to be good, but it’s hard!” *Mahā-patita-pāvana* Śrīla Prabhupāda, when it is hard for us, bind us up, drag us by the hair, or kick us back to Kṛṣṇa, as you like. That you will never forsake us is our constant solace.

*Your inmate servitors at ISKCON Prison Ministry.*  
(written by Candraśekara dāsa)

## Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Academy School

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dear beloved grandfather, on this special day of your appearance, though unqualified and insignificant, we have been mercifully given the opportunity to offer our appreciation.

On the day that we did the *gṛha-praveśa* fire *yajña* for the opening of our school—11 September 2001—our main prayer was that no matter what happens, you should always remain the center of this school, and the school should simply be to please you.

On behalf of all the teachers and other staff members of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Academy School, located in a very remote corner of Africa in Ghana, we are humbly begging you to please forgive us for our imperfections in handling this important project of yours. With a straw in our mouth, we seek your blessings to empower, guide, and direct us on how we can become better menial servants to handle the management of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Academy School to your expectation.

By the kind mercy of His Holiness Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami, His Holiness Mahāviṣṇu Swami, His Holiness Kavicandra Swami, His Grace Lokavarṇottama Prabhu, and many other devotees, we have managed to reach this stage of having eighty-five children in the school, ranging between the ages of four to twelve. They are in four classes now, but we intend to increase the number of classes each year. By your mercy they attend *gurupūjā* daily, chant at least one round daily, sing many devotional songs, act in Kṛṣṇa conscious dramas, do *harināma saṅkīrtana* and *prasādam* distribution every Saturday in town, and study the scriptures in class.

Please engage us in your service, though we are unqualified. Please help us make the school a pleasing place for you to dwell in. Please help us be inspiring examples for the children, so that apart form all the external activities that they are performing, they will develop into loving and compassionate Vaiṣṇavas.

*Your servants at Lord Kṛṣṇa’s Academy School, in Accra, Ghana.*  
(written by Śāstra dāsa and Ilā-devī dāsī)

## Office of Alternative Dispute Resolution

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

As a “*saṅkīrtana* devotee” years ago, I remember that your statements on book distribution and developing compassion for the conditioned souls were our guiding light. I was given a collection of your photocopied letters on those subjects, and the other Prabhus and I would read them again and again. What inspiration they gave us!

I don’t go out on *saṅkīrtana* much these days, but I still want to be a servant of your mission. Despite my weaknesses, I know that my real desire and satisfaction lie in somehow pleasing you by serving your order to help others.

About two years back I spoke at length with your wonderful servant Śeṣa Prabhu about how detrimental our in-house fighting is to the *saṅkīrtana* movement. We figured that one of the greatest enemies of your preaching efforts is the arguments and lack of cooperation we sometimes have amongst ourselves.

Doing a little research, I found some letters from you about this:

We have so much work to do, we cannot lose our solidarity. Do not cause a crack there with any fighting spirit or competition. Whenever I hear complaints or disturbances in our centers my mind becomes too much disturbed and I cannot properly translate my books. So please spare me from such disturbance by cooperating all together Godbrothers and Godsisters. \[Letter, 7 January 1974\]

I am very concerned, however, that in the midst of such good reports, the devotees there are quarreling. This report is not very good. \[Letter, 1 May 1974\]

You have dedicated your life for Krsna and therefore you should be ideal. We are introducing Krsna Consciousness movement for the harmony and good will of humanity. But if you yourselves are suffering from the very ills we are trying to remove, how can the people be influenced favorably? Stop this fighting, tolerate. \[Letter, 1 May 1974\]

I now found a new set of quotes from you to inspire service!

Throughout ISKCON we are setting up a system for your pleasure to resolve differences among devotees in a timely and effective way. This is mainly done through mediation. We’ve had a number of very successful mediations among your servants.

There is also a system of ombudsmen and -women, so that all members of your Society—young or old, senior or junior—have a way to voice concerns, suggestions, and complaints...and be heard.

Our sincere hope is that this program will help devotees feel more taken care of in your society. It was your GBC body that unanimously approved this program, and they have been very encouraging and instrumental in its establishment.

I recall a few years ago being asked what was the happiest moment in my life. I immediately answered: paying obeisances to your *mūrti* in the Detroit temple after returning at 4:00 a.m. on Christmas morning from a six-week *saṅkīrtana* marathon. We felt overwhelmingly sheltered and secure at your lotus feet.

That moment, however, has been nearly rivaled a few times recently. It is a most wonderful experience to see two Vaiṣṇavas embrace each other and work out an agreement by which they can cooperate together and let go of past misunderstandings, when just two hours prior they weren’t talking to each other and thought of each other in less-than-kind ways. It doesn’t happen all the time, but when it does, it’s a great pleasure to serve your mission in that way.

Thank you for providing us with service in the service of your devotees.

*Your servants at the ISKCON Office of Alternative Dispute Resolution.*  
(written by Braja Bihārī dāsa)

## Padma Academy

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Devotees such as Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura pray to take birth even as an insect in the house of Kṛṣṇa’s devotee. Full surrender to the Lord is like this—as long as we can serve Him and His devotees we accept any situation. And one of the benefits of a birth in a devotee’s home is the chance to be educated in knowledge and love of Kṛṣṇa from the very beginning of life. Such a child will develop, as you wrote in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* love of God that will not turn into material lust.

Somehow we are privileged to train these children who have appeared in order to assist you in your mission of infusing the world with Lord Caitanya’s movement. Please give us the strength and intelligence to produce young adults who will be “sober and responsible, the best citizens, and a credit to their state.” (Letter, 25 December, 1971)

Praying for your mercy, we remain,  
*Your servants at Padma Academy, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.*

## Prabhupāda Village

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Of all the qualities of a pure devotee, the one that appears most prominent to me is your purity. Being a pure devotee may be somewhat inconceivable to those of us that are impure but by seeing your example that purity becomes strikingly manifest.

In coming to America from the blissful land of Vrndavana, you exhibited the purity in fulfilling the order of your Spiritual Master by preaching in the English language. You didn't mind the difference of cultures but took that order and carried it out. You were not concerned with the degradation you saw but were concerned to give, not take. You had something very valuable to give as all of us are learning each day. You gave us Krsna consciousness, you gave us a way of life where we could place Krsna and not ourselves in the center of our lives. Basically you gave us a way of life by which we could get out of the material world and return to the blissful kingdom of God. You gave us a lot, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we just need to take it up with the purity you showed us. Thank you for your purity, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for coming into our lives and showing what we needed to do. Again, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you for your example. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at Prabhupada Village, Sandy Ridge, N.C. (written by Sivananda dasa)

## Radio Krishna Centrale

Most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet. By taking shelter under their refreshing shadow, the preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness becomes fearless.

Very recently my wife, Līlāvilāsinī-devī dāsī, a very innocent, simple, and sincere devotee, always ecstatically engaged in devotional service—running the Radio Krishna station, cooking *prasādam*, sewing and embroidering dresses for the Deities, preaching and taking care of guests, has been diagnosed with a malignant cancer at an advanced stage.

We were shocked. She cried, saying to me “I do not want to leave you. Who is going to take care of you and cook for you?” She soon collected her thoughts and said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda has given us everything, now let see what I’ve learned.”

She completely surrendered herself to Kṛṣṇa’s will and begged for strength and encouragement from Guru and all the assembled devotees. Immediately letters and phone calls from all over the world began to arrive. Then she said, “by listening and preaching I’ll become fearless.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’ve created an amazing spiritual family full of wonderful devotees, under whose protection I beg to always remain. By a fraction of your mercy you have bestowed upon us all an unlimited amount of good fortune. By serving you, a simple, soft-hearted “woman” has become a fearless hero. We will never be able to understand your unfathomable greatness.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you.

Please give me the opportunity to continue to serve your heroic devotees, and keep me always under the shelter of your divine lotus feet. I beg Your Divine Grace to please take care of Līlāvilāsinī-devī dāsī and give her all protection, according to your sweet will.

Property, family, children, and wife—  
All friable and crumbling in material life.  
Waste no more time, please make sure that you preach  
To old folk and youngsters, the poor and the rich.  
Chant the holy names and your pure love refresh,  
Detach from these cages of blood, bones, and flesh.  
Fear no more danger from impending death—  
Remember Śrī Kṛṣṇa and do never forget.

*Your servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, Italy.*  
(written by Tridaṇḍi dāsa)

## Rūpānuga Vedic College (Athens)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our respectful obeisances unto you, who are very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.

Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.

On this very auspicious day we can repeatedly try to glorify you, although your glory is unlimited as well as eternal.

You are a gardener who are the well-wisher of all the suffering bees. You are the holder of the pure nectar for the hungry bees. You create many flower gardens to attract the misguided bees. You let them feast and carry seeds for other unhappy bees. You store the nectar and supply it even to the sleeping bees. You are the only means and shelter for all the ignorant bees. You for sure are the life and soul of the fortunate ISKCON bees.

We never experienced your physical presence. Nevertheless, we are most fortunate to have your association through your beloved disciples, your books, and your ISKCON, which is like a garden full of various flowers that are blooming from the rain of your mercy.

Begging for your mercy,

*Your aspiring servants at Rūpānuga Vedic College, European campus, in Athens, Greece.*

## Rūpānuga Vedic College (Kansas City)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We are eternally indebted to you and your sincere followers. By spreading this *saṅkīrtana* movement around the globe, you are kindly fulfilling the prediction of Lord Caitanya. Who can estimate your glories? You are the spiritual master of spiritual masters. The highest truth is resting in your heart. Although you walked on the earth, appearing as an ordinary man, the steps you took were extraordinary. We humbly pray that we can follow in the footsteps of your lotus feet, which adorned the earth for so many years. We do not profess to be lovers of God; we barely even know who Kṛṣṇa is. But now that you have come, we know for certain that the goal of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is obtainable. By your staunch conviction you have set the standard of pure devotional service, the only means by which one can approach the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. You have shown us the smiling face of the Lord.

You planted the seed of Rūpānuga Vedic College, and now it is blossoming into a desire tree, fulfilling the desires of those who truly want to know Kṛṣṇa. Our coursework is the study of your books, our teachers are the messengers of your wisdom, and our living facility is your house, ISKCON. The *Bhagavad-gītā* states that real knowledge is to know the difference between matter and spirit. What college outside of your vision is providing this confidential teaching? At last, we can get real knowledge.

We are insufficient in reflecting the bliss we feel in your moon-like association. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are, among many things, mercy personified.

*Your greatly indebted servants at Rūpānuga Vedic College, American campus, in Kansas City, Missouri, USA.*

## Śaraṇāgati

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda was uniquely enigmatic. He was five inches over five feet, but although we could see over his head, we could not think of him as a small person; he defied measurement. He was utterly serious, yet light-hearted; deeply philosophical, yet possessed of childlike innocence. He untiringly smashed all illusions of material happiness, piercing the world’s masquerade, tearing at its facade, yet he had a healthy love for the world as a gift from God. He was completely his own person yet unswervingly loyal to the tradition he lived; he was steeped in that tradition, yet his vision was always fresh, never hackneyed, stereotyped, or predictable. He marked vast social, political, and historical degenerative cycles—and daily confronted his followers’ failures—yet remained ever hopeful, ever patiently revamping our spoiled disposition. With his followers, Śrīla Prabhupāda was tolerant and intolerant, strict and flexible, accepting and demanding.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s compassion was Godlike, his love for us inspiring. We wanted to emulate him—to be unencumbered by material motivation. His greatness, the epitome of human evolution, was within the reach of those whose only qualification was to have been touched by him. How inexplicable. He had the mind of a saint, the heart of a mother, and dark, pondlike eyes with depth and generosity and gentleness in the most encompassing face we’d ever seen. He tirelessly reiterated spiritual precepts, insisting that we, his students, know who God is, know who we are, know how the material energy operates, and know the consequences of our acts—karma.

His faith was so immovable, so staunch, that it was not “belief” but the substance of things unseen by us. Being in his presence was being in the presence of something beyond him that was immeasurably great; being in his presence was electrifying. And with all of this, he was without pride.

Perhaps of all Śrīla Prabhupāda’s qualities, the one that touched his followers most was the least enigmatic: his authentic, integral, selfless caring. He saw the intrinsic dignity of every individual, for each was rooted in God. Anecdotes of this abound.

In Śaraṇāgati, a remote community in British Columbia, we are not removed from the challenges that face Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers everywhere: financial straits, ideological differences, personality conflicts—but we are hopeful. We *must* be hopeful because Śrīla Prabhupāda is hopeful and because Śrīla Prabhupāda cares about our community and us. So, in spite of everything, we chant and will continue to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. And we pray to receive what is only available causelessly: Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy upon us.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the Śaraṇāgati rural community in British Columbia, Canada.*

## Saraswati Charitable Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your holy lotus feet. All glories to Your Eternal Divine Grace!

One of the many things that you taught us was the critical importance of good association. Among the countless jewels of wisdom that you showered us with, this is surely one of the most important. Perhaps the most important. For if we lack the company of serious (and jolly) devotees in our lives, then our impetus to engage in any and every kind of devotional service will gradually disappear. And the goal of life will similarly disappear for us. That's how important good association is.

You not only taught us about this—you gave it to us. You gave us the highest association of all. Yours! You were, and are, the closest we ever came to God. And you delivered God, Kṛṣṇa, to us. You invited Him and His associates to enter Their Deity forms, to come to this dark world just to benefit all of us. They did that for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Only upon your request. And today Their Lordships are invited by us into Their Deity forms in temples all over the world, and still They decide to enter only by your request, by your mercy.

The success of our lives still rests upon the sincerity with which we associate with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda: through your books, through our prayers to you, the devotional services you taught us to perform, the love we show you by co-operating with each other to spread your mission.

Please allow me to always bathe in the sweet and comforting good association of your sincere devotees-disciples and grand-disciples. And through that good association, please give me the strength and intelligence to someday accomplish things that will bring you much happiness and many wide smiles. All by your causeless mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda kī jaya!

Your servants at The Saraswati Charitable Trust,  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Tauranga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine appearance! All glories to your divine pastimes and instructions, which are like nectar pouring into the ears of us Kali-yuga souls, reviving and relieving our scorched hearts and minds.

Here along the eastern coast of New Zealand’s North Island is a small city, Tauranga, in which a small group of your followers are attempting a humble preaching effort. Over the course of seven or eight years, the group here has been through significant change and evolution. Some devotees arrived, some departed, while others abandoned your movement entirely. Seemingly, these could be seen as difficult times, yet in another light one may see an incredible consolidation of loyalty, purpose, and commitment—yes, certainly the devotee must see Kṛṣṇa’s hand in everything.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we sincerely thank you for this legacy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you have given us, the association of your followers, and the institution by which we may effectively take and give good association. Thank you for instruction in the form of your books, for beautiful Deities and Their worship, for *harināma-saṅkīrtana* and *prasādam*. Thank you for the authorized process of *sādhana-bhakti*, *śraddhā* to *prema—*you certainly gave it all! Thank you for your protection from the onslaught of Māyā in the form of envious adversaries; thank you for the cooling shade of your lotus feet. Although we’re undeserving, you are undoubtedly helping us in our fledgling attempts to render our insignificant service.

On this auspicious day we humbly pray for your continued guidance and mercy. Please help us overcome any personal obstacles that impede our progressive spiritual development. We further pray for the intelligence to enable us to work co-operatively for your pleasure. Aspiring to fall flat in the dust of your lotus feet birth after birth,

*Your insignificant servants in Tauranga, the Bay of Plenty, New Zealand.*  
(written by Trivikrama dāsa)

## TKG Academy

Our dear spiritual father and grandfather, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your merciful feet.

Giving up the period in your life during which you could have been enjoying “your own” children and grandchildren, you came to the West just to rescue other people’s children and grandchildren from their suffering.

Now you have given us the responsibility of looking after the needs of children who are also not “our own” but who are the offspring of your disciples and grand-disciples.

You created the first international *gurukula* here. And to protect the children, you invited Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālachandjī to come and sit down in Old East Dallas; and They accepted your invitation. There were problems with that original school, which saddened you and Kālachandjī and caused the project to be derailed. Then we who came to this community in 1979 had to pick up the pieces and start afresh. But we were not alone: you sent many of your disciples to give us their association and to bring their children for us to teach.

What qualifications do we have, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for training these youngsters to become Vaikuṇṭha men and women? None, yet we have to try, because that is what you have requested us to do. You have given us much knowledge, without which we would be groping around in the dark wondering why bad things happen! From you we have gained the means for instructing our children about how to live free of fear and suffering.

We will always be indebted to you for establishing a school where children can obtain some understanding of the purpose of their lives and can learn of Kṛṣṇa’s love. We understand that these children are the future of ISKCON and that only by following your instruction will we secure that future. Please bless us with the ability to serve your “children” in a way that will please you and Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants, the staff of TKG Academy, in Dallas, Texas, USA.*

## Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E.

To our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your appearance, we beg to offer our humble obeisances at your lotus feet, where you mercifully invite the lost souls of this world to take shelter in order to move out of the darkness and into the light.

With never-ending compassion for our suffering, and knowing that we have for so long wandered from one corner of the material world to the other—from one birth to another—you journeyed far from your home in Vṛndāvana to save us from our useless search for material happiness. With great patience you taught us that this temporary enjoyment inevitably leads to frustration, sadness, and yet another birth. In the short amount of time we had the personal association of Your Divine Grace, you exemplified again and again how much you cared for each soul who had forgotten his or her true identity and eternal relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa. You taught us the simple joys of spiritual life—chanting, dancing, and feasting on *prasādam*. You taught us Deity worship and the many benefits of discussing transcendental knowledge. Śrīla Prabhupāda, while you were here you gave us so many gifts, but before you departed from this world you prepared to leave us the greatest gift of all. You labored long and hard to leave us the gift of your books—volumes of them filled with the most precious and timeless knowledge there is to learn. You did all of this out of your love for us, yet you never asked for much in return.
